### GG-023 — T4 Entry Budget Is Monotonic: Exits Free Economic Capital but Never Replenish Entry Capacity

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-023 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-11 SESSION 46 (UTC; 2026-06-10 PDT) — live observation during Finals G4, mid-Q2 |
| Category | Incremental architecture — exit mechanics (ADR-0018/0019) bolted onto a budget design (ADR-0013) that predates them |
| What was missed | `t4_deployed_usd` in `run_t4_live.py` accumulates monotonically (`+= approved_size` at fire, lines 672/709) and is never decremented by the exit pass. When a position exits, its stake is economically freed (PnL realized, position closed in the T4 portfolio) but the entry-budget counter still counts it as deployed. Observed live in G4: T4 fired 1,199 bets and hit $499.95/$500.00 (100%) **before halftime**; 29 exits then fired correctly and freed capital, but `budget_used` stayed pinned at $499.95 — every subsequent cycle logged `fired=0`. The entire second half, where live Bayesian updates carry the densest signal, had zero entry capacity. |
| When it should have been caught | ADR-0019 acceptance / SESSION 43 DS activation override — activating exits changed the meaning of "deployed" from a one-way commitment to a revolving quantity, and the budget accounting was not re-examined against that change. |
| Phases it survived | Designed pre-exit (ADR-0013); survived ADR-0018, ADR-0019, T-146/T-147 implementation review, and SESSION 43 activation. |
| Why it survived | No test or review question asked "what does an exit do to the budget counter?" Exit tests (test_t146_t147_exit_mechanics.py) validate PnL formulas and signal logic, not budget interaction. The budget-exhaustion halt is *designed* behavior (band-level kill: ADR-0013, GG-010 resolution), which made the observation pattern-match to "working as intended." |
| Impact | MEDIUM now (paper only; T4 pool isolated). Distinct from and NOT relitigating GG-010 (DS ruled shared budget + per-band kills is design intent — that ruling concerned cross-band allocation; this gap concerns within-band accounting after exits). Capacity cost is measurable from G4 logs: T4 was entry-dead from ~Q2 onward with ~24+ min of game remaining. Also degrades T-152 empirical exit-event collection: no new entries in the second half means no second-half exit events. Direction of error is conservative (under-deployment, never over-deployment) — but it silently halves the very evidence window the DS override was created to collect. |
| Discovery | Live log read during G4 (session cycle summaries: `fired=0` every cycle post-01:21 UTC with budget pinned at $499.95 after 29 exits), confirmed against source. Human-directed registration (operator: Earl). |
| Resolution | T-161: DS decision + ADR-0013 amendment (or new ADR) on exit-credit semantics. Options to be evaluated with G4/G5 data: (a) credit full exited stake back to entry budget (revolving budget); (b) keep monotonic — budget = gross commitment cap per game (status quo, document explicitly); (c) credit exited stake only up to realized value (no credit for losing exits). Interaction to check: daily-loss net semantics (`424698a`) and kill-switch inputs must stay coherent with whichever option is chosen. No remediation direction chosen at registration — deliberately left to DS cold review post-G4. |
| Status | RESOLVED — 2026-06-10 SESSION 46, ADR-0022 (T-161 DS ruling: option (c) realized-value credit). Exits credit budget at realized value via `_exit_budget_credit()` (entry cost on a win — no capacity expansion; entry cost + pnl on a loss — losses permanently consume budget). Aggregate band net loss now bounded by t4_band_budget. Restart-safe: `_reconstruct_t4_ledger()` rebuilds the ledger from portfolio state (open pool_isolated exposure + per-exit realized losses, keyed to game_date) — G5 starts at $0.00 deployed automatically. Daily-loss net semantics (424698a) verified independent and coherent. 13 new tests (test_t161_budget_credit.py); 843/843 passing. |

### GG-024 — PIPE-03 Late Cron Places T1 Bets on Games Already in Progress

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-024 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-11 SESSION 46 — audit log review, Phase A post-G4 resolve |
| Category | Architectural gap — PIPE-03 has no in-progress game guard on bet placement |
| What was missed | PIPE-03 (T1 pipeline) checks GAME_DATE but does not check whether the target game has already started before placing new positions. The late cron fires at 01:30 UTC; Finals G4 tipped at 00:30 UTC (~60 min into the game). Audit shows `paper_order_opened` × 2 at 01:31:22 and 01:31:27 UTC for Stephon Castle and Josh Hart — both placed mid-Q2 with live game in progress. These bets used pre-game model estimates (Poisson CDF on pre-game `pts_l10_mean`) against market prices that had already repriced to reflect 60 min of live play. The T1 model has no informational advantage at that point; the market's live price is more informative than the T1 forecast. |
| When it should have been caught | PIPE-03 cron design review (SESSION 10–12) — when the late cron window was established, the overlap with game-in-progress time was not explicitly ruled out. |
| Phases it survived | Every game night since the late cron was activated. G1–G3 likely fired the same way but were not audited at this granularity. |
| Why it survived | The concern was always about the early cron (pre-tip timing), not the late cron. The late cron was designed as a "same-day with T3 overlay" backup window; the guard discussion focused on date alignment (GG-015), not game-start state. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. Bets placed on in-progress games are anti-informed: market has live signal, model does not. In paper trading this is a data quality issue (degrades EXP-017B/EXP-018 evidence — bets placed at a systematic disadvantage). In live trading this would be financially harmful. Also creates ambiguity in G6 evidence: "eligible bets" definition (APR-0019) should exclude mid-game placements. |
| Discovery | Audit log: `player_stats_fetch_failed` × 66 (01:30:10–01:30:56 UTC) then `paper_order_opened` × 2 at 01:31:22/01:31:27 UTC; confirmed `paper_order_opened` payloads show `player_name: Stephon Castle` and `player_name: Josh Hart`; ESPN poll confirmed Q2/clock=~25min at that timestamp. |
| Resolution | T-162: Add game-start guard to PIPE-03 — before the bet-placement loop, check ESPN game status (already polled by T4). If `STATUS_IN_PROGRESS` or past scheduled tip time: skip placement, log `paper_run_skipped_game_in_progress`. Fix is low-complexity (single check against ESPN game state or tip-time comparison). G6 evidence audit: flag any bets placed mid-game as ineligible per APR-0019. |
| Status | RESOLVED — T-162 implemented 2026-06-11 SESSION 46. ESPN `STATUS_IN_PROGRESS` guard added to `run_paper_trade.py` before bet-placement loop (commit `c8a59f2`). Deployed to VPS. 817/817 tests pass. G4 late-cron bets (Castle + Hart) flagged ineligible per APR-0019. |

### GG-025 — T4 delta_p Mixes Calibration Gap with Live Signal (Architectural)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-025 |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-06-10, ADR-0021) |
| Severity | MEDIUM |
| Discovered | 2026-06-10 (SESSION 46) |
| Registered by | ADR-0020 derivation analysis |

**The gap:** `delta_p = p_t4 - p_t1` where p_t4 is raw Poisson and p_t1 is Platt-scaled. At game start (t=48, 0 pts scored), the baseline calibration gap is mean=0.376, σ=0.165 — a systematic model difference, not a live game-state signal. ADR-0020 raises T4_MIN_DELTA_P to 0.50 as a band-aid (above mean+1σ noise floor), but this does not address Q3/Q4 scenarios where the Kalshi market has converged to p_t4, causing |delta_p| to fall to 0.01-0.04. A massive Q4 reversal would not trigger T4 even with budget available because the market has already priced in the game state.

**Correct fix:** Compute incremental delta_p = p_t4_current - p_t4_t0 (change from game-start p_t4, not from p_t1). This isolates live game-state signal from calibration bias. Estimated threshold for incremental version: mean+2σ ≈ 0.48 (G4 trajectory data, n=36 tickers).

**Remediation:** New ADR required. Architectural change — involves storing per-ticker game-start p_t4 and updating GameStateModel.update() and T4EventLoop. Post-G6 scope.

**Resolution (2026-06-10, SESSION 46):** DS overrode the post-G6 deferral ("the architecture needs the correct fix"). ADR-0021 implemented same session. Improvement on the registered fix: no stored game-start snapshot needed — `p_t4_baseline = p_exceeds_threshold(0, threshold, pace×48)` is deterministic from pregame pace, making the fix stateless and restart-safe (G4 mid-game process death would have corrupted a stored-snapshot design). Gate is `|delta_p_incr| ≥ max(2·σ_null(t), 0.04)` where σ_null is the exact Poisson null envelope — no fitted constants. G4 replay (n=2,193): materiality pass rate 74.6% → 9.7%; Q1 false-fire flood eliminated (89.3% → 10.8%) while Q4 sensitivity increased (1.4% → 13.5%), confirming calibration bias is cancelled rather than signal suppressed. T4_MIN_DELTA_P removed; ADR-0020 D2 superseded.

### GG-026 — T4 Band Budget Architecture: PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING Bypasses Production Kelly Budget Path

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-026 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-10 SESSION 46 CLOSE — DS review: "I don't want arbitrary capital restraint (ie $500) — no bandaid fixes" |
| Category | Architectural shortcut outliving its original scope |
| What was missed | `PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING=true` (default) hardcodes `t4_band_budget = PAPER_T4_POOL_USD` ($500 flat) and bypasses the production path `_compute_t4_band_budget(portfolio)` = `deployable × T4_BAND_WEIGHT`. The flat $500 was the correct pool size when first set (SESSION 24, G1 launch). By G4/G5, the T4 pool has grown to $1,869.68 through paper wins — the flat cap is now 26.7% of the actual pool and no longer represents a principled fraction of capital. Additionally, `T4_BAND_WEIGHT = 0.10` is explicitly marked "Phase 8 placeholder — inactive in PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING=true mode" — it was never calibrated. If the flag were removed today, the production path would give $186.97 band budget ($1,869.68 × 0.10) — less than the flat cap, not more. The transition from flat to proportional is undocumented, unplanned, and the calibration constant is a stub. |
| When it should have been caught | ADR-0011/ADR-0017 acceptance — when PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING was introduced as a "paper architecture" shortcut, a sunset condition (pool growth trigger, or G-gate) should have been specified. GG-011 resolution (2026-06-05) explicitly confirmed the bypass as paper architecture; no calibration review was triggered. |
| Phases it survived | G1 launch (pool=$500, flat cap=$500 — coincidentally correct); G2, G3, G4 (pool grew 3.7×, cap unchanged). |
| Impact | MEDIUM now. The cap is effectively underspecified: $500 is neither the current pool size nor a calibrated fraction of it. At G5 with ADR-0021 active (fire volume ~10% of G4's), budget exhaustion should be rare regardless of cap level — but the cap will grow increasingly mismatched as the pool continues to grow. Direction of error is conservative (under-deployment) but arbitrary — the cap has no principled relationship to the system's capital position. |
| Resolution path | T-163: ADR-0023 — remove PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING flag; activate `_compute_t4_band_budget(portfolio)` production path; calibrate T4_BAND_WEIGHT from Kelly math (expected concurrent positions × Kelly fraction × expected edge → target utilization fraction). Derivation must be grounded in G1–G4 trajectory data — no arbitrary constant. T4_BAND_WEIGHT update per ADR-0023; `pool_isolated` flag wiring retained. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-06-11 SESSION 47 — ADR-0023 ACCEPTED; T4_BAND_WEIGHT=0.27 (G4 empirical: $499.95/$1,869.68); PAPER_T4_FLAT_SIZING and PAPER_T4_POOL_USD removed; `_compute_t4_band_budget` is now the single budget path; pool_isolated=True hardcoded; 843/843 tests. |

### GG-027 — PAM Integration Incomplete Across Bands: T2/T3/T5 Have No Archetype Wiring; Environment Classifier Not Built

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-027 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-11 SESSION 50 — operator architectural direction: pressure-context categorization for decision tree filters |
| Category | Architectural capability gap — registered feature not yet integrated across full band stack |
| What was missed | The Player Archetype Matrix (PAM v4) is operationalized in C2 (`QualitativeSignalStore`) and the prod model uses 47 PAM features (prf_w01–15, mod_01–32). However: (1) T2/T3/T5 bands have no PAM wiring — `run_t2_sameday.py`, `run_t3_pretip.py`, and T5 LateGameArithmetic operate without archetype context; (2) T4 `T4EventLoop` received archetype register wiring in SESSION 50 but the archetype does not yet affect the routing math (passed through to audit log only); (3) the "pressure-context environment classifier" described by the operator — game-environment categorical modifiers (high-pressure Finals/elimination context, expanded-role context for role players, crowd/psychological context) — is entirely unbuilt as of SESSION 50; (4) QS snapshot age: classifications dated 2026-05-28 have not been updated for Finals-specific role changes (e.g. Alvarado Q4 expanded role in G4). |
| When it should have been caught | ADR-0019 (exit mechanics) acceptance — when PAM features were confirmed live in the model, a task for full band integration should have been registered. The operator's architectural direction toward environment-context categorization makes the gap more visible. |
| Impact | LOW now — T2/T3/T5 are SKELETON/BLOCKED; T4 routing is correct without archetype math (ADR-0021 handles materiality). HIGH pre-MLB activation — regular season requires full band coverage; environment classifier is architecturally essential for the "Wemby 10 RBG ≠ Zubac 10 RBG" disambiguation at scale. |
| Resolution path | T-165: Full PAM integration across T2/T3/T5 bands + environment classifier ADR — MLB activation gate. Scope: (a) EnvironmentClassifier in C2 (game-level pressure score: elimination_flag, series_momentum, crowd_premium, role_expansion_flag for role players); (b) archetype × environment interaction in C3 (uncertainty multiplier on model_p for sparse-data role players in high-pressure contexts); (c) dynamic MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE in decision tree routing per environment class; (d) T2/T3/T5 archetype register wiring matching T1/T4 pattern. |
| Status | OPEN — **re-scoped 2026-07-06 SESSION 123.** This entry, its "What was missed" text, and T-165 all describe NBA's bands (`run_t2_sameday.py`, `run_t3_pretip.py`) and NBA's PAM v4 taxonomy — GG-027 stays confined to that scope and stays dormant while NBA is tabled (see NBA-deferred directive). **Attempting to action this for MLB (T2/T3 MLB — `run_t2_mlb_sameday.py`, `run_t3_mlb_pretip.py`) this session surfaced that the premise "wire PAM into T2/T3 MLB" does not hold**: direct inspection confirms PAM v4 is NBA-only end-to-end — `qualitative_data/pam_v4/SCHEMA.md` requires `player_id` to be an `nba_api` ID, the only two classification snapshots on disk (`QS-2026-05-28-001/002`) are NBA players, the PRF-1–15 archetype taxonomy is sourced from `Player_Attribute_Matrix_v4.docx` (a basketball role taxonomy — GG-027's own "Wemby ≠ Zubac" example is NBA-specific), and no MLB file anywhere (including the already-Tier-1-validated T1/T4M-PROJ MLB bands) references `prf_w0*`/`mod_0*`/`DS01_FEATURE_NAMES`. There is no MLB archetype system to wire in — building one is a from-scratch DS-source project (taxonomy design, player classification, an ADR per the environment-classifier requirement already in this entry's Resolution path, and a decision on how a frozen, already-validated MLB model consumes a brand-new feature set under Hard Rule 4), not a same-sprint wiring fix. That MLB-side work is tracked separately as **T-243** (registered 2026-07-06, unscoped/unsized — do not treat as "in progress"). GG-027/T-165 remain exactly as originally scoped: NBA-only, dormant until NBA bands approach active status. |

### GG-028 — PIPE-03 No Idempotency Guard: Second Cron Execution Places Duplicate Positions for Same game_date

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-028 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-12 SESSION 52 — G5 portfolio audit: 41 open positions vs expected 29; two batch timestamps confirmed (22:00 UTC 29 bets, 01:00 UTC 12 bets) |
| Category | Operational gap — pipeline has no guard against re-execution for an already-deployed game_date |
| What was missed | PIPE-03 (`run_paper_trade.py`) has no check for existing open positions before running the bet-placement loop. If the script runs twice for the same game_date, it evaluates all markets afresh and places any new bets that pass the edge filter — including markets where positions already exist from the first run. G5 result: the 22:00 UTC batch placed 29 bets; a second run at 01:00 UTC placed 12 more bets, including duplicate Wemby NO ≥40 and NO ≥35 positions (the two that failed in the first batch — the pipeline saw no "resting" order and re-deployed them). Total T1 deployment: $1,003.38 from a $1,000 bankroll reset, pushing reserve to 13% (below RESERVE_MANDATE 15%). T-162's game-in-progress guard (added SESSION 46) guards against in-progress games only — it does not guard against same-game_date re-execution when the game has not yet started. |
| When it should have been caught | GG-024 resolution — when T-162's in-progress guard was added, idempotency for pre-game re-execution should have been addressed in the same pass. |
| Phases it survived | G5 paper trading only; no live capital impact. Likely occurred in other finals games but was not audited at this granularity. |
| Why it survived | GG-024 focused on mid-game placement (game in progress). The idempotency case (game not yet started, positions already open for this game_date) was not explicitly addressed. The second cron may have been triggered by PIPE-03 restart or manual rerun — cause unknown. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. In paper mode: extra bets, reserve mandate breach (~13% vs 15% target), data quality degradation (duplicate positions inflate sample n). In live trading: this would double-deploy real capital on the same market, potentially violating per-event exposure caps. A PIPE-03 accidental rerun (process crash + restart) in live trading would cause material unintended exposure. |
| Resolution path | T-166: Add idempotency guard to `run_paper_trade.py` — before the bet-placement loop, check portfolio for existing open positions with `game_date == target_date`. If any exist: log `paper_run_skipped_already_deployed: {n} positions exist for {game_date}` and exit. Also: investigate what triggered the 01:00 UTC second run (process restart? manual? cron misconfiguration?). |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-11 SESSION 53** — T-166 implemented. `_open_tickers` set in `run_paper_trade.py` now includes ALL open positions regardless of `kalshi_order_status`. Commit: SESSION 53. Root cause of "failed = transient" design confirmed: the exclusion was intended as a Kalshi-connectivity retry mechanism, but a recorded paper position represents a committed local allocation — bankroll already decremented, position already in `open_positions`. The second cron is connectivity redundancy, not a re-evaluation window. |

### GG-029 — MC Script Analyzes Pregame Forecasts, Not Committed Positions: Wrong Source and Wrong Parameters

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-029 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-12 SESSION 52 — MC script reported $106.21 staked; actual portfolio had $1,003.38 deployed; source confirmed as `pregame_20260612.json`, not `paper_portfolio.json` |
| Category | Tooling gap — risk analysis tool not connected to actual system state |
| What was missed | `scripts/run_monte_carlo.py` reads from `pregame_YYYYMMDD.json` (the model's forecast output) rather than `logs/paper_portfolio.json` (the actual committed positions). It then re-applies Kelly sizing with hardcoded parameters: `kelly=0.1, min_edge=0.02, max_exposure=500.0` — none of which match the actual risk engine (`DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION=0.25, MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.04`). Result: every MC run throughout the Finals has simulated a hypothetical re-deploy of the residual bankroll, not the actual risk exposure of committed positions. G5 example: MC reported $106.21 staked from $130.62 bankroll (81% utilization) while the actual portfolio had $1,003.38 deployed. The kill switch risk and P/L distribution the MC reported were for a different portfolio than the one that will resolve. |
| When it should have been caught | MC script introduction (SESSION ~30?) — the source data and parameter alignment were never validated against the actual risk engine configuration. |
| Phases it survived | All Finals games (G1–G5). Every MC report produced this session analyzed a hypothetical portfolio. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. The MC is a governance and operator-communication tool — Earl uses its output to assess risk before game day. If the tool is analyzing a different portfolio than the one deployed, all MC-based risk assessments are systematically disconnected from actual exposure. P/L distribution tails, kill switch risk probabilities, and player concentration figures are all wrong. Actual G5 portfolio analysis (run manually SESSION 52): EV=+$246.67, P(profit)=99.8%, P(kill switch)=0.00%, p50 end bankroll=$382. |
| Resolution path | T-167: Rewrite `run_monte_carlo.py` to: (1) read from `logs/paper_portfolio.json` as primary source; (2) use risk engine constants from `risk.py` (not hardcoded); (3) simulate actual win/loss outcomes for each committed position using stored `model_p`, `direction`, `mid_price_at_entry`, `size_usd`; (4) report actual P/L distribution, not hypothetical re-deploy. Keep `pregame_YYYYMMDD.json` as an optional "pre-deploy preview" mode for analysis before committing. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-11 SESSION 53** — T-167 implemented. `run_monte_carlo.py` rewritten: portfolio mode (default) reads `paper_portfolio.json`, simulates committed positions via Bernoulli draws using `model_p`/`direction`/`mid_price_at_entry`/`size_usd`. Preview mode (`--preview`) retains pregame JSON behavior. Constants corrected to production values (`kelly=0.25`, `min_edge=0.04`). Scenario flags added: `--exclude-failed`, `--yes-only`, `--no-only`, `--haircut`. Commit: SESSION 53. |

### GG-030 — Portfolio MC Draws Positions as Independent Bernoullis: Within-Player Threshold-Ladder Correlation Ignored

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-030 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-12 SESSION 55 — pre-Game-5 protocol review of `run_monte_carlo.py` portfolio mode (T-167 rewrite) |
| Category | Tooling gap — risk model structurally understates correlated tail risk |
| What was missed | Portfolio mode (`run_monte_carlo.py:144`) simulates each committed position as an **independent** Bernoulli draw (`rng.random(n_sims) < p_win` with a fresh draw per position). But the G5 book holds threshold *ladders* on single players — e.g., Brunson NO 25/30/35/40 (×2 each after GG-028 duplicates) and Wembanyama NO 35/40 (×2). In reality these rungs are perfectly correlated: one 40-point game loses every rung simultaneously. The script's own caveat text (line 297–298) acknowledges this, but the headline numbers reported to the operator (G5: P(profit)=99.8%, P(kill switch)=0.00%) come from the uncorrelated path. Notably, the legacy `--preview` mode does this *correctly* — it draws one Poisson score per player and compares it against all thresholds (line 424–438) — so the T-167 rewrite regressed correlation structure while fixing position sourcing. Compounding: the draws use the model's own `model_p` as ground truth, so the MC cannot see model miscalibration — the exact failure mode EXP-017B (DIRECTIONAL_NEGATIVE vs real prices) and the G3 MC miss (predicted +$138 @ 95.1%; actual -$199) point at. |
| When it should have been caught | T-167 implementation review (SESSION 53) / SESSION 54 hygiene audit — the audit verified per-position formulas but not the joint distribution. |
| Phases it survived | G5 risk reporting (SESSION 52 manual analysis used the same independent-draw structure). |
| Why it survived | Hygiene audit checked formula correctness per position, not portfolio-level dependence. The caveat in the report footer made the limitation feel "documented" without changing the headline numbers. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH for operator communication. With $323 on Wembanyama NO rungs and ~$102 on Brunson NO rungs (each rung duplicated), a single star eruption in an elimination game realizes losses approaching the $200 kill-switch trigger (20% of $1,000) — yet the MC reports P(kill switch)=0.00%. The reported risk profile of the G5 book is materially understated in the left tail. No live capital impact (paper). |
| Discovery | Human-prompted adversarial review (SESSION 55 Q6), grep of simulation structure vs live book composition. |
| Resolution | T-168: portfolio mode should draw one latent outcome per player (Poisson score, as preview mode already does) and resolve all of that player's positions against it; treat duplicated tickers as summed exposure. Re-run G5 MC and re-report tail/kill-switch figures. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-06-12 — T-168 SESSION 55: `simulate_portfolio()` now draws one Poisson score per player via `_infer_lambda()` (least-squares fit on threshold/model_p pairs); direction-invariant (NO bets use raw model_p, not 1−model_p); NO-bet inversion bug found and fixed in same session. G5 MC re-run: P(profit)=98.3%, kill-switch risk=1.28%, mean P&L=+$244.23. Commit `530e1c3`. |

### GG-032 — T4 Portfolio Accounting Incoherence: bankroll +$869.68 vs total_realized_pnl −$21.59

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-032 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-12 SESSION 55 — VPS `paper_portfolio_t4.json` top-level field audit |
| Category | Data integrity gap — headline T4 performance metric cannot be reconciled from the file that stores it |
| What was missed | `paper_portfolio_t4.json` shows `bankroll_usd=1869.68`, `initial_bankroll_usd=1000.0`, `total_realized_pnl=-21.59`. These cannot all be true under bankroll = initial + realized P&L: the implied gap is ~$891. Probable contributors: the pool actually started at $500 (PAPER_T4_POOL_USD era) while `initial_bankroll_usd` recorded 1000; the SESSION 46 reset touched realized P&L epochs differently from bankroll; and/or pool-isolated open/close paths credit `bankroll_usd` asymmetrically from `total_realized_pnl`. Whatever the mix, the widely-cited "T4 pool +87% / +108% growth at 51.4% win rate" narrative and the file's own realized P&L (negative since reset) tell opposite stories. |
| When it should have been caught | SESSION 46 bankroll reset (epoch boundary not applied consistently to T4 file) or T-148/APR-0019 T4 evidence separation work. |
| Phases it survived | G3–G5 T4 reporting; MEMORY_BANK milestone entries cite pool growth figures. |
| Why it survived | T4 pool figures were read as a single number (bankroll_usd) without reconciling against initial + realized; pool_isolated semantics made cross-checking against T1 conventions feel inapplicable. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. T4's G6-facing evidence (APR-0019 T4 floor ≥50) depends on knowing whether T4 is actually up ~$870 or down ~$22 since its epoch start. Until reconciled, no T4 performance claim should be cited. Fill-realism haircut (T-153/GG-022) compounds the uncertainty in the same direction. |
| Discovery | SESSION 55 protocol — field-level file audit. |
| Resolution | T-170: reconstruct T4 ledger from `closed_positions` (2,315 records) + C6 audit log; determine true epoch-consistent P&L; correct either `bankroll_usd`, `total_realized_pnl`, or `initial_bankroll_usd`; document which figure feeds APR-0019 Tier 2 T4 evidence. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-06-12 — T-170 SESSION 55: `paper_portfolio_t4.json` corrected on VPS — `bankroll_usd` 1869.68→**478.41**, `initial_bankroll_usd` 1000→**500.0**. Root cause: ADR-0022 `_reconstruct_t4_ledger()` wrote T1 system bankroll into T4 file. True T4 pool = $500 initial + (-$21.59 realized P&L) = **$478.41**. Backup at `paper_portfolio_t4.json.bak_t170`. Do not cite "+87% growth" T4 narrative — based on wrong field. |

### GG-033 — Live Portfolio State Tracked in Git: Deploy Pipeline Silently Broken Since SESSION 55

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-033 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-12 SESSION 56 — VPS `git pull --ff-only` aborted on dirty tree during EXP-018 filter deploy |
| Category | Deploy integrity gap — live mutable state in VCS blocks deploys and risks state destruction |
| What was missed | `logs/paper_portfolio.json` is git-tracked. Every cron write dirties the VPS working tree, so every `git pull` aborts. SESSION 55's deploy record ("git pull + scp confirmed") was inaccurate: the pull failed on this same conflict, and the scp of `run_monte_carlo.py` masked it. Result: VPS sat at `cea8a19` (SESSION 54 close) through SESSION 55–56 while MEMORY_BANK recorded it as current — the T-168 MC fix existed on the VPS only as an uncommitted working-tree file. Verified identical to the committed version this session (no drift), but nothing guaranteed that. |
| When it should have been caught | SESSION 55 deploy verification — "DEPLOY OK" was asserted without `git log -1` confirmation on the VPS. |
| Phases it survived | SESSION 55 (T-168 deploy), SESSION 56 (T-169 sprint) until this session's pull attempt. |
| Why it survived | scp side-channel made the working tree look current; no deploy step verified VPS HEAD == origin/main. |
| Impact | HIGH for live-capital era, MEDIUM now. (1) Two failure modes: deploys silently no-op (stale risk code in production), or a future `git checkout .`/`git reset --hard` on the VPS destroys the live book — open positions, annotations, bankroll. (2) Local repo copy of the portfolio is stale ($1,000/3-open vs VPS $130.62/41-open), so the session-start governance hook prints misleading portfolio state. |
| Discovery | SESSION 56 — pull abort during EXP-018 dedupe filter deploy. |
| Resolution | T-171: (1) `git rm --cached logs/paper_portfolio.json` (and `paper_portfolio_t4.json` if tracked) + add to `.gitignore`; (2) coordinated VPS migration: backup live file outside repo → checkout → pull (file removed from tree) → restore → verify untracked; (3) add `git log -1` HEAD verification to the /vps deploy skill so "deployed" claims require commit-hash proof; (4) decide what the session-start hook PORTFOLIO line should read once local copy is gone (VPS fetch or label as N/A). Schedule for dark period — do NOT perform the untracking migration while 41 positions are open and crons are writing. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-15 (SESSION 63 — T-171 executed in the GG-034 deploy).** `logs/paper_portfolio.json` untracked on VPS (`git rm --cached` + already in `.gitignore`), live book backed up outside the repo before the operation, restored after `git pull`, verified untracked (`git status --porcelain` shows no portfolio entry). VPS working tree now clean → `git pull` no longer aborts and cannot clobber the live book. Deploy verification upgraded to require VPS `git log -1` HEAD == origin/main proof. Migration was performed during the dark period as scheduled (G5 still open, but quiesced — cron stopped + `run_t4_live` killed during the operation, restored after). Linked: GG-034; T-171. |

### GG-034 — Realized-P&L Accumulator Desynced From Ledger: Drawdown Kill Switch Blind to True −25% Loss

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-034 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-13 SESSION 62 — ARE WE COMPROMISED? Q6, reconciling session-start portfolio readout |
| Category | Lying-monitor / ledger-reconciliation gap — self-reported health diverges from append-only ground truth (matches the standing audit thesis) |
| What was missed | The live VPS book `logs/paper_portfolio.json` stores `total_realized_pnl = −$8.46`, but the sum of its own 117 closed-position `pnl` fields is **−$251.21**. The C6 append-only audit log (`audit.jsonl`, `paper_position_resolved` events) independently confirms **−$251.21** for exactly those 117 position_ids, with **zero double-resolutions** — so the ledger is correct and the scalar accumulator is the corrupted value. Gap = **$242.75**. True realized drawdown is **25.1%** of the $1,000 initial bankroll, which **exceeds the 20% `max_drawdown_pct` kill switch** (`risk.py:105`). The drawdown kill switch reads the corrupted scalar (`run_paper_trade.py:274` → `current_drawdown_pct` → `risk.py:418`), as do the C7 monitor (`monitor.py:537`), the C8 dashboard (`dashboard.py:181`), and the G6 positive-P&L gate. All four health surfaces read the lying number; none derive P&L from the ledger. **The same class of desync exists in the T4 book** (`paper_portfolio_t4.json`: stored −$21.59 vs Σ closed pnl −$47.25) — systemic, not a one-off. Per-game-date truth: 06-05 +$75.67 (35 bets, = the last consistent snapshot, matches the stale local `paper_portfolio_vps.json`), 06-08 −$198.84 (47), 06-10 −$128.04 (35). The −$8.46 scalar matches no clean subset → not a single reset; consistent with concurrent-writer clobbering. |
| Root cause | `total_realized_pnl` is a redundant scalar accumulator of a value fully derivable from `Σ closed_positions.pnl`. It is incremented in-memory by `PaperTrader` (`paper_trader.py:528`, `:377`) under an **in-process lock only** (`:188`), with **no cross-process lock**. Multiple runners write the same JSON file load→mutate→save: `run_paper_trade.py`, `run_resolve.py`, `run_t4_live.py`, `run_t4_resolve_local.py`. A runner holding a stale in-memory copy saves it back and clobbers increments from another runner (last-write-wins), so the scalar drifts while the appended `closed_positions` list and the append-only audit log stay correct. Bankroll is also independently inconsistent: entry debits full `size_usd` (`paper_trader.py:199,226`) and resolve credits `size+pnl` (`:527`), so `bankroll = init − Σ(open size_usd) + Σ(closed pnl)` implies Σ closed pnl = **+$134.00**, a third number disagreeing with both −$8.46 and −$251.21 — so `bankroll_usd` cannot be trusted either. |
| When it should have been caught | Any session reading the portfolio summary since 2026-06-08 (first divergent resolution batch). The session-start governance hook prints `total_realized_pnl` verbatim as "P&L $-8.46 / drawdown 0.8%" with no ledger cross-check. GG-030/GG-032/GG-033 each corrected portfolio *values* in prior sessions but never reconciled the accumulator against the ledger, so the mechanism survived every patch. |
| Phases it survived | G4→G5 (2026-06-08 onward); through SESSION 56–61 portfolio-state work; surfaced SESSION 62. |
| Why it survived | No invariant check that `total_realized_pnl == Σ closed_positions.pnl` on load or save. All health surfaces (kill switch, monitor, dashboard, G6 gate) trust the cached scalar instead of deriving from the ledger. The reported 0.8% drawdown looked healthy and far from the 20% switch, so nothing prompted a reconciliation. |
| Impact | **HIGH.** (1) **Drawdown kill switch is blind** — true 25.1% drawdown is past the 20% halt threshold but the switch reads 0.85% and never fires on this. CRITICAL for live-capital era; HIGH now even on paper because every risk decision since 06-08 sized against a false bankroll/drawdown. (2) **G6 readiness is measured on a false number** — G6 requires positive resolved P&L; book shows ≈breakeven (−$8.46) while truth is −$251.21 (−25%). The commercial/gate picture is materially worse than recorded in MEMORY_BANK. (3) `bankroll_usd` itself does not reconcile, so Kelly sizing inputs are suspect. (4) Same desync confirmed in the T4 book. |
| Discovery | SESSION 62 Q6 — cross-checked stored scalar vs Σ closed pnl vs C6 audit log vs cash identity; three of four mutually disagree, audit log corroborates the ledger at −$251.21. |
| Resolution | NOT YET FIXED (operator approved root-cause + registration only; no writes to the live book pending fix approval). Proposed direction for a dedicated session: (1) make `total_realized_pnl` a **derived property** (`sum(p["pnl"] for p in closed_positions)`) rather than a maintained accumulator, OR add a load-time reconciliation assert that halts on `|accumulator − Σledger| > $0.01`; (2) recompute and correct the live `bankroll_usd` from the audit log as ground truth (init − Σ open entry-costs + Σ closed pnl), with backup preserved; (3) point the kill switch / monitor / dashboard / G6 gate at the ledger-derived figure; (4) add a cross-process file lock (or single-writer invariant) so concurrent runners cannot clobber state; (5) apply the same fix + correction to the T4 book; (6) re-evaluate whether the true −25% drawdown retroactively triggers a halt / DS review. Coordinate with T-171 (untracking + migration) since both touch the live book during the dark period. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-15 01:54 UTC (SESSION 63 — T-174 deployed + live book corrected, operator OK given).** Code half (above) deployed to VPS (HEAD `52607f2`) via controlled manual deploy (GHA auto-deploy aborted on pre-existing untracked VPS files — `team_stats_store.py`, a backup file — so deploy was done by hand: quiesce cron + stop `run_t4_live`, backup both books + crontab, `git checkout HEAD -- paper_portfolio.json` → `git pull` → restore book from backup). **Live main book corrected:** constructed `PaperTrader` (auto-`reconcile()` logged C6 `portfolio_pnl_desync` EVT-4CED1860AFCB, drift $242.75) + `_save()` → `total_realized_pnl` −8.4645 → **−251.2134**, drawdown **25.12%**. **Kill switch un-blinded — verified by code path:** `run_paper_trade.py:274` reads the corrected scalar → drawdown 25.12% > 20% `max_drawdown_pct` → `kill_switch_from_c4` halts. T-171 untrack completed in the same deploy (book now gitignored, no longer clobbered by `git pull`). Cron restored. **T4 book needed NO correction** — verified stored_pnl 4.8242 == ledger 4.8242 (the prior −$21.59/−$47.25 desync was already fixed by GG-032; runbook figure was stale). **NEW FINDING → registered as [[GG-035]]:** the corrected `bankroll_usd` came out **−$254.59** (initial $1000 − $251.21 realized − $1003.38 locked in 41 open G5 stakes). Equity is +$748.79 but free cash is negative — the bot was **over-deployed at 100.3% of initial / ~134% of equity**; the corruption hid an over-leverage condition, not just a drawdown. **G5 NOT RESOLVED → [[GG-036]]:** the 41 open G5 positions could not be settled (`run_resolve.py` → "game results not available"; nba_api has no 2026-06-13 box scores), so $1003.38 stays locked and the dataset stands at 117 closed + 41 open. Outcomes NOT fabricated. **G6 baseline ratified by operator at the true figure** (−$251.21 / −25.1% headline, −$302.98 / −30.3% clean-only). Linked: GG-030, GG-032, GG-033, GG-035, GG-036; T-171; T-174. |

### GG-035 — Over-Leverage Hidden by P&L Corruption: Deployed Capital Exceeded Equity

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-035 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-15 SESSION 63 — surfaced the instant the live book was corrected under GG-034 (the corrected `bankroll_usd` came out negative) |
| Category | Risk-control gap — exposure exceeded equity; the corruption that hid the drawdown also hid an over-leverage condition |
| What was missed | After GG-034 correction, the ledger-derived `bankroll_usd` is **−$254.59**. Decomposition under the cash identity (`bankroll = initial + Σ closed non-isolated pnl − Σ open non-isolated stake`): $1000 initial − $251.21 realized − **$1003.38 locked in 41 open G5 stakes** = −$254.59. Equity (realized only) is +$748.79, but **free cash is negative** — the bot deployed $1003.38, which is **100.3% of the $1,000 initial bankroll and ~134% of current $748.79 equity**, into the single G5 game window. The corrupted scalar reported a healthy +$130.62 bankroll, so nothing stopped the bot from sizing past its own equity. The over-leverage is a *consequence* of the same lying scalar (GG-034): Kelly/exposure sizing read the false bankroll and the false near-breakeven drawdown, so per-event and aggregate exposure caps were computed against money that did not exist. |
| When it should have been caught | Each G5 deployment cron (2026-06-13) — an aggregate-exposure check of `Σ open stake ≤ available cash` would have blocked deployment once cumulative stakes crossed equity. No such free-cash invariant exists; exposure caps are per-event (`max_event_exposure`) and Kelly-fraction based, not a portfolio-level cash-availability gate. |
| Phases it survived | G5 deployment window (2026-06-13) through SESSION 63 correction. |
| Why it survived | (1) GG-034's corrupted `total_realized_pnl`/`bankroll_usd` made available cash look far larger than it was. (2) There is no portfolio-level invariant asserting deployed stake ≤ equity (or ≤ free cash); risk controls are per-bet Kelly and per-event caps, which do not sum to a global cash constraint. (3) Paper trading has no real margin call to force the issue. |
| Impact | **HIGH for live-capital era, MEDIUM now (paper).** In live capital this is a margin-breach / forced-liquidation condition — deploying more than you hold. Now it means the recorded G5 exposure was sized against phantom capital; any G5 P&L is on an over-leveraged book. It also means the **41 open G5 positions cannot be honored from free cash** — they are funded only because paper accounting permits a negative bankroll. Compounds with GG-036 (those same positions are also unresolvable). |
| Discovery | SESSION 63 — GG-034 live correction produced a negative bankroll; decomposed via the cash identity to a 100.3%-of-initial open-stake position. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-06-16 SESSION 64 (structural) — ADR-0026 ACCEPTED.** The structural gap is closed: C4 (`RiskEngine.evaluate_forecast`) now enforces a **two-tier portfolio-level free-cash invariant** on aggregate open non-isolated stake. (1) **CHECK 8a — hard solvency invariant:** `Σ open stake + new size > equity` ⇒ KILL_SWITCH (`portfolio_over_leverage`); free cash can never go negative. (2) **CHECK 8b — policy deployment cap:** `> 0.60 × equity` ⇒ REJECT (`aggregate_deployment_cap`), per-bet (not a batch break), so exposure plateaus at the cap. The 60% cap is `1 − RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT` — the ADR-0010 40% reserve **promoted from advisory-warning to enforced aggregate invariant**, no new free parameter. Base is **equity** (stricter than initial when underwater — the GG-035 stress case). C4 stays pure: it receives a portfolio snapshot via new `PaperTrader.exposure_snapshot()`; `run_paper_trade.py` supplies it each forecast. Backward compatible (skipped when `portfolio_equity_usd` is None) — T4/EXP paths unaffected until they opt in (tracked for pre-G7). **Institutional verification:** `test_g5_overleverage_forensic_would_have_been_blocked` replays G5 against real pre-G5 equity ($748.79) — deployment plateaus at the $449.27 cap and **never reaches the actual $1,003.38**; the over-leverage state is unreachable with the invariant active. 10 new tests; full suite 974 passed. **LIVE manifestation already relieved** by GG-036 (G5 resolved → bankroll +$751.10, free cash positive). Remaining G6 obligation: the 60% threshold must be re-derived via ADR at G6 with the live bankroll (the structural invariant is permanent). Linked: GG-034 (root scalar), GG-036 (locked positions freed), ADR-0026, ADR-0010; T-174. |

### GG-036 — G5 Resolution Blocked by Data Unavailability: 41 Positions + $1003.38 Locked

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-036 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-15 SESSION 63 — `run_resolve.py` returned "game results not available" for the 2026-06-13 G5 window during the dark-period close |
| Category | Data-availability / quality-data gap — resolved track record cannot be completed because the stats source has no box scores for the final game |
| What was missed | The 41 open positions from NBA Finals G5 (2026-06-13, NYK series close) **cannot be settled.** `run_resolve.py` (which fetches actual player points from nba_api and settles YES if `actual_pts ≥ threshold`) returns "No positions settled yet — game results not available." nba_api has no 2026-06-13 box scores in the source, while G2/G3/G4 resolved cleanly. Consequence: the canonical NBA dataset stands at **117 closed + 41 open** (not a complete 158-resolved record), $1003.38 of stake stays locked, and the G6 baseline (−$251.21) reflects only the 117 resolved bets. The 41 unresolved bets are NOT counted, and their outcomes were **NOT fabricated** (no-fabrication rule). |
| When it should have been caught | This is a live data-source limitation, not a latent code bug — surfaced at first resolve attempt. The risk that the *final* game of the season might not get a box score in time was not pre-registered as a dark-period dependency. |
| Phases it survived | N/A (newly surfaced) — but it directly affects the G6 baseline being ratified this session. |
| Why it survived | The resolve pipeline assumes the stats source eventually serves every played game; there is no monitor for "game played but box score never landed" and no fallback source for end-of-season games. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM.** (1) The G6 positive-P&L baseline is computed on 117 of 158 bets; the 41 G5 bets could move it (in either direction) when/if they resolve. (2) $1003.38 stays locked (see [[GG-035]] — that same stake is the over-leverage), so derived bankroll stays negative until resolution frees it. (3) Quality-data extraction (the dark-period goal) is incomplete by 41 rows. The dataset must be re-frozen if G5 ever resolves. |
| Discovery | SESSION 63 — resolve attempt during dark-period close. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-06-16 SESSION 64.** Proposed direction (1) succeeded with no new code: `nba_api` backfilled the 2026-06-13 box scores ~3 days post-game (probe: Wembanyama pid 1641705, Playoffs endpoint, 2026-06-13 → 19 PTS). All **41 G5 positions settled via `run_resolve_local.py`** from real box scores — **no fabrication**, no ESPN fallback needed. Result: **22 W / 19 L, G5 net +$2.31** (the ~$1,003 largest-ever exposure settled break-even). Live VPS book now **open=0, closed=158, stored realized −$248.90 == ledger −$248.90** (reconciled), `bankroll_usd` recovered −$254.59 → **+$751.10** (frees the locked stake — relieves the live manifestation of [[GG-035]], though the structural free-cash invariant gap remains). Dataset re-frozen (`data/nba_finals_clean_dataset.csv`, 158 closed, sha256 `54bac8b2…`) and **G6 baseline re-ratified at −$248.90 / −24.9% headline, −$212.25 / −21.2% clean** (SNAP-2026-06-16-001). Backup `paper_portfolio.json.bak_gg036` on VPS. Material: the contamination story flipped — contaminated bets now net −$36.65 (was +$51.76). Linked: GG-034, GG-035; T-174. |

### GG-037 — Calibration Collapse + Inverted Edge Realization: the Model's Edge Signal Is Anti-Predictive

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-037 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-16 SESSION 64 — full-database assessment of the re-frozen 158-bet NBA Finals dataset (`data/nba_finals_clean_dataset.csv`), run after the GG-036/GG-035 sprint. Directly confirms the audit-directive prime suspect "calibration collapse." |
| Category | Model-validity / forecast-integrity gap — the C3 forecast is severely overconfident, and the derived `fee_net_edge` gate (the core EV decision rule) is **negatively correlated** with realized outcomes on the only resolved data we have. Distinct from [[GG-004]] (which is about *sample adequacy* for the commercial claim); this is about the *direction and calibration* of the signal on the sample we already hold. |
| What was missed | No governance artifact asked whether the model's probabilities are **calibrated** or whether **higher claimed edge actually produces better outcomes.** The full database says no on both counts: (1) **Calibration collapse** — directional Brier 0.2308 (barely beats 0.25 coinflip); mean stated confidence 0.799 vs actual win rate 0.601 (**≈20-point overconfidence**); every confidence bucket ≥0.7 is materially overconfident; **72 of 158 bets (46%) sit in the [0.90,1.00) bucket predicting 0.970 but realizing 0.792 (−$108.19).** (2) **Inverted edge realization** — within the YES-frame positive-edge bands, `fee_net_edge`[0.00,0.05) → wr 0.714 / **+$15.46**; [0.05,0.10) → wr 0.529 / **−$78.08**; [0.10,0.20) → wr 0.368 / **−$119.93**; [0.20,1.0) → wr 0.500 / −$10.74. **The bets where the model claims the largest mispricing lose the most.** High claimed edge clusters at mean `model_p`≈0.74–0.76 — i.e. apparent "edge" is the same overconfidence, not real alpha; the market is closer to truth precisely where the model cries mispricing. |
| Mechanism (fully traced) | Overconfident `model_p` → inflated `gross/fee_net_edge` → larger Kelly stake → systematic loss. This also explains the headline paradox: **60.1% win rate yet −$248.90 net** (−7.28% ROI on $3,420 turnover, profit factor 0.742). Payoff is asymmetric — avg win $7.53 vs avg loss −$15.31 (payoff ratio 0.492) → **breakeven win rate needed = 0.670, actual only 0.601.** The bot wins often but small and loses big (fading at short prices), and the Kelly stake is largest exactly on the worst-calibrated high-confidence bets. |
| When it should have been caught | At any point a reliability diagram / edge-decile P&L curve was produced. None was — all prior EV work measured against `mip=0.50` ([[GG-004]]/KI-017) or reported aggregate win rate, neither of which exposes calibration or edge monotonicity. |
| Phases it survived | G2–G5 paper trading. The 60% headline win rate read as "healthy" in self-reported summaries — the exact audit-directive thesis (self-reported health > reality). |
| Why it survived | No monitor for calibration (predicted vs realized by bucket) and no monitor for edge-realization monotonicity. C7 watches drawdown/drift, not whether the probability estimates mean what they claim. |
| Impact | **HIGH (commercial-thesis-level).** This is direct evidence that, on the only resolved data in existence, the model does **not** beat the market and its edge signal is **anti-predictive** — the core EV claim (`model_p > mip + vig`) is not just unproven ([[GG-004]]) but pointing the wrong way where the stake is largest. It does not by itself prove the strategy fails (see caveat), but it places a heavy, specific burden on EXP-017 at scale and is a G6 blocker alongside GG-004. |
| Honesty caveat (ADR-0024) | **n=158, single series (NBA Finals NYK), T1-only, ~20–30 players.** Per ADR-0024 this is *structurally insufficient* for a commercial verdict (floor: n≥500 unique markets, ≥15 opponents, or ≥2 sports each ≥150). These are **strong directional RED FLAGS, not statistical proof.** A Finals series is a high-variance, low-sample, correlated regime. The finding is "the available evidence is damning and the signal looks inverted," not "the strategy is proven to lose." |
| Discovery | SESSION 64 — post-sprint full-database assessment (this session). |
| Status | **PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-06-22 SESSION 68 (alpha monitoring added) + 2026-06-16 SESSION 64 (calibration half) — OPEN (alpha half).** **SESSION 68 update (T-177 Part a):** `scripts/run_t177_edge_monitor.py` built (29 tests). Reads live portfolio JSON or CSV; computes edge-tercile winrate + Fisher's exact test; emits ANTI-PREDICTIVE/MONOTONIC/INCONCLUSIVE verdict with `--ci` exit codes. Verified on Finals clean subset: n=103, edge>0 wr=0.389 vs edge≤0 wr=0.687, p=0.006 → ANTI-PREDICTIVE. This is the recurring monitor for MLB/regular-season EXP-017 data. **DS decision OPEN:** if EXP-017 MLB ANTI-PREDICTIVE at ADR-0024 adequate sample (n≥500), authorized action options are (A) halt+ADR-0004, (B) direction inversion, or (C) feature-set diagnosis — must be cold-committed before MLB data available. **SESSION 64:** DS directive: "recalibrate and record it disproved miscalibration." **(A) Calibration RESOLVED via ADR-0027 ACCEPTED.** Root located: production T1 `model_p` is the **pure Poisson** survival prob (`game_state_model._poisson_cdf`, `run_paper_trade.py:543`), NOT LR+Platt (`prod_model.joblib` only logs `p_estimate_lr`) — so recalibrating Platt would have been a **no-op**; the overconfidence is Poisson ignoring NBA over-dispersion (var>mean; `scoring_distribution.py` already has an unused NB path). Fix: a **temperature-scaling layer** `p_cal=σ(logit(p)/T)` in new `c3_forecasting/calibration.py`, wired at the single production prob site; per-sport registry with **fail-safe identity (T=1.0)** so MLB cannot inherit NBA's shrink. NBA T=**2.05** (OOS-validated: fit G2–G4, test G5 → Brier 0.315→0.282, confidence 0.807→0.731 vs 0.537; in-sample optimum was 3.10 — conservative OOS chosen). Re-derivable + monitored via `scripts/derive_calibration.py` (the GG-037 calibration+edge-decile monitor, item 2). 14 new tests; full suite **988 passed**. **(B) MISCALIBRATION HYPOTHESIS DISPROVED — alpha half stays OPEN.** Recalibration does **NOT** create alpha: after calibration the residual edge vs the Kalshi price is still **anti-predictive** — edge>0 winrate **0.356** vs edge≤0 **0.747**; highest-edge tercile 0.346 vs lowest 0.792. The edge inversion **survives calibration** — it was never just overconfidence. Calibration makes the bot honest (declines ~70% of prior bets) but not profitable on Finals data. **GG-037 remains OPEN** for the no-alpha finding; deferred to EXP-017 at scale (MLB dark period + NBA regular season) per DS ("we have the rest of season MLB testing data to toggle it"). Does NOT relieve [[GG-004]]. NBA T=2.05 inherits the n=158 single-series limit (ADR-0024) — provisional, re-derive on adequate sample at G6. Linked: ADR-0027, [[GG-004]], audit directive (calibration collapse CONFIRMED, now corrected), ADR-0024, ADR-0004/ADR-0015 (amended), GG-008. |

### GG-038 — T4 Kill Switch SLA: 60s NFR-03 Is 12× Too Slow for Continuous T4 Evaluation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-038 |
| Category | Architectural gap — safety SLA insufficient for new execution regime |
| What was missed | NFR-03 specifies kill switch ≤60 seconds. At Kalshi's 60 calls/min execution rate, 60 seconds = up to 60 additional bets submitted between kill trigger and halt. For monolithic T4 with a small pre-scheduled bet set, this was acceptable. For continuous T4 evaluation potentially approving multiple bets per second, 60 seconds of uncontrolled execution after a kill trigger represents unacceptable capital exposure. |
| When it should have been caught | When ADR-0029 T4 sub-band architecture was first designed — the moment continuous evaluation was proposed, the kill switch SLA should have been immediately re-examined. |
| Phases it survived | T4 is BLOCKED (Phase 8+), so no live harm has occurred. |
| Why it survived | NFR-03 was designed for a batch-bet system (T1: ~10–20 bets per night). The T4 continuous evaluation architecture is new (SESSION 66). |
| Impact | **HIGH pre-Phase 8.** Without a T4-specific fast-halt mechanism (≤5s), a kill trigger during a live T4 evaluation burst could result in 60+ bets submitted before halt — the exact scenario the kill switch exists to prevent. |
| Discovery | SESSION 66 — ADR-0029 hygiene check. Surfaced during three-layer T4 architecture design. |
| Resolution | New ADR amending NFR-03 for T4 band + C7 implementation of two-tier fast-halt mechanism: (1) T4-specific halt in ≤5s; (2) system-level halt preserves current 60s SLA for T1/T2/T3. Both tiers must operate independently. |
| Status | **OPEN — SESSION 105 CORRECTION: paper-trading exemption RETRACTED.** ADR-0036's exemption was true as a fact (T4FastHalt exists in `t4_live_runner.py`, SESSION 71) but conflated the NBA T4 engine with MLB — `t4_live_runner.py`/`t4_price_cache.py`/`t4_execution_engine.py` are the NBA sub-band stack (ADR-0029), not MLB; MLB T4 (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`) has no sub-band architecture and does not consume this code at all. See ADR-0037 (correction). No paper-trading exemption currently applies to any live MLB work. **Remains OPEN.** Formal production validation of T4FastHalt (as an NBA-scope item) is tabled per the NBA work-tabling directive until ~1 month before 2026-27 tip-off. Linked: ADR-0029, ADR-0036, ADR-0037, GG-061, NFR-03. |

### GG-039 — T4 Price Cache / Staleness Check Architecture

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-039 |
| Category | Architectural gap — missing component required for Layer 2 staleness arbitration |
| What was missed | ADR-0029's Layer 2 execution queue requires current Kalshi market prices to run the staleness check on each dequeued bet. The existing venue constraint for T4 specifies `max_api_calls_per_decision = 0` — zero live API calls at execution time. These two requirements are in direct tension: the staleness check needs current prices but cannot call the API at execution time. |
| When it should have been caught | When the staleness-arbitration execution queue was designed in T-179 (SESSION 66). This gap is intrinsic to the architecture — it cannot be discovered before the architecture is specified. |
| Phases it survived | T4 is BLOCKED. No live harm. |
| Why it survived | The gap is created by the architecture specified in this session. It has not had time to "survive" any phase. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM — design gap only; no live capital risk until Phase 8.** If unresolved, the staleness check cannot function as designed and the execution queue would either need to drop all bets without a price check (unsafe) or use stale prices from trade time (defeats the purpose). |
| Discovery | SESSION 66 — ADR-0029 hygiene check. |
| Resolution | Design and implement a separate read-only price polling loop: polls Kalshi market prices for all open markets at a cadence ≤ the shortest expected edge window across active T4 sub-bands; writes to an in-memory price cache; operates independently of the execution rate limit budget (not counted as execution API calls). Cache staleness windows per sub-band defined in ADR-0029. Full architectural design requires a separate ADR. |
| Status | **OPEN — SESSION 105 CORRECTION: paper-trading exemption RETRACTED.** `fetch_player_points_markets()` (the function this exemption cited) is an NBA-only function — points props, not MLB hits. ADR-0036 conflated the NBA T4 engine (ADR-0029, `t4_live_runner.py`/`t4_execution_engine.py`) with MLB; no MLB sub-band system exists for this exemption to apply to. See ADR-0037 (correction). **Remains OPEN.** MLB's actual T4 runner (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`) has no sub-band price-cache dependency of this kind — it is a separate, flat-tree implementation. Linked: ADR-0029, ADR-0036, ADR-0037, GG-061. |

### GG-040 — T4 Real-Time Data Sources: Required Feeds Not in Current C1 Architecture

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-040 |
| Category | Architectural gap — data source prerequisite for T4 sub-band activation |
| What was missed | T4 sub-bands require data feeds that do not exist in the current C1 implementation. Current C1 uses ESPN scoreboard API for box scores with multi-minute latency — insufficient for T4-EVENT (requires ≤2s event detection) and T4-LINEUP (requires ≤5s substitution detection). T4-MICRO requires real-time order book feed. T4-SENTIMENT requires Kalshi tick-level price history. None of these are built. |
| When it should have been caught | During T4 Phase 8 planning. The gap exists because T4 is BLOCKED and no Phase 8 data source planning has been done. |
| Phases it survived | T4 BLOCKED — no live harm. |
| Why it survived | T4 Phase 8 data source requirements were never formally inventoried. ADR-0029 is the first document to specify them concretely. |
| Impact | **HIGH pre-Phase 8; LOW now.** Without Phase 8 data sources, T4-EVENT, T4-LINEUP, T4-MICRO, and T4-SENTIMENT cannot function as designed. T4-STAGED and T4-PROJ can function with enhanced versions of current C1 data. |
| Discovery | SESSION 66 — ADR-0029 design analysis. |
| Resolution | Phase 8 C1 rebuild to add: (1) Live box score feed with ≤2s event detection latency (T4-EVENT, T4-ARITH); (2) Live play-by-play / lineups API with ≤5s substitution detection (T4-LINEUP); (3) Kalshi tick-level market price feed (T4-MICRO, T4-SENTIMENT). Each requires vendor evaluation and C1 module design. Full data source ADR required before Phase 8. |
| Status | **OPEN — SESSION 105 CORRECTION: "PARTIALLY RESOLVED" status RETRACTED.** ADR-0036's sub-band data-sufficiency table described T4-PROJ/ARITH/FLOW/BLOWOUT inputs in MLB vocabulary ("batter stats," "innings," "batting slot") but was actually evaluated against `t4_execution_engine.py` — the NBA sub-band engine, which operates on `pts_so_far`/`quarter`, not MLB fields. No MLB sub-band architecture exists in the codebase for this table to describe; the ruling does not correspond to any real code. See ADR-0037 (correction). **Reverts to OPEN, fully unresolved**, pending either (a) a genuine MLB sub-band build with its own data-sufficiency analysis, or (b) confirmation that the NBA ruling stands on its own NBA-scoped merits once NBA work resumes (~1 month before 2026-27 tip-off). Linked: ADR-0029, ADR-0036, ADR-0037, GG-038, GG-039, GG-061. |

### GG-041 — KXNBAPTS Orderbook Depth Data: Finals Window Missed; ADR-0005 Limit Empirical Basis Absent

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-041 |
| Discovered | SESSION 72 — 2026-06-22; T-127 execution attempt found no active KXNBAPTS markets |
| Category | Data collection miss — evidence required for ADR-0005 hard limit not captured during the only available window |
| What was missed | Live KXNBAPTS orderbook depth data was not captured during the 2026 NBA Finals window (Jun 3–22). T-127 required querying `GET /markets/{ticker}/orderbook` during an active Finals game day to set an empirical `max_order_pct_of_depth` limit for ADR-0005. The one logged run (`logs/orderbook_depth_20260602_151325.json`, 2026-06-02) queried before Finals markets opened — all 5 markets showed `combined_depth_usd: 0`. No subsequent script run was triggered during any Finals game day. VPS has zero orderbook depth logs. All KXNBAPTS markets finalized 2026-06-14 (after G5 on 2026-06-13). |
| When it should have been caught | Any Finals game day Jun 3–13 during active market hours. T-127 was registered as HIGH priority with explicit "if this window is missed, no equivalent data exists until 2026-27 pre-season" warning. |
| Phases survived | G5 |
| Why it survived | T-127 script automation was registered as COMPLETE (script built); but script execution during an active game day was not automated or calendared. The single pre-Finals run returned zeros and was not flagged as "wrong window." No VPS cron for orderbook depth polling was set up. |
| Impact | **LOW now (dark period, no live capital).** ADR-0005 (`max_order_pct_of_depth` hard limit in `_HARD_LIMITS`) cannot be set on empirical Finals orderbook data. The limit must use a conservative placeholder or be deferred to 2026-27 regular season Day 1 data collection. Impact is LOW because no live capital is at risk until G6; the limit is a G6 prerequisite, not a paper-trading constraint. |
| Discovery | SESSION 72 — `python3 scripts/query_orderbook_depth.py` returned "No active KXNBAPTS markets found." Confirmed via VPS run and direct Kalshi API orderbook query (empty `yes_dollars`/`no_dollars` arrays on finalized markets). |
| Resolution | Re-run `scripts/query_orderbook_depth.py` on 2026-27 regular season Day 1 (target October 2026, Phase 4). Capture live depth for 3–5 active KXNBAPTS contracts during a regular-season game window. Use result to set `max_order_pct_of_depth` in ADR-0005. Until then: ADR-0005 must not specify an empirical `max_order_pct_of_depth` value; any placeholder must be labeled provisional pending Phase 4 data. |
| Status | **OPEN.** Deferred to Phase 4 (October 2026 — 2026-27 regular season Day 1). LOW urgency. Linked: T-127, KI-023, ADR-0005. |

---

| Gap ID | GG-042 |
| Discovered | SESSION 81 — 2026-06-24; T-187B per-threshold model diagnostic |
| Category | Active scope exclusion — threshold with inadequate Brier signal deferred pending feature injection |
| What was missed | MLB KXMLBHIT threshold t=3 (3+ hits per game) excluded from `THRESHOLDS` in `train_mlb_model.py` and `_MODEL_THRESHOLDS` in `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`. Exclusion rationale: at t=3, base rate=3.9% and AUC=0.61 produce a Brier improvement of only +0.0002 (shadow gate requires ≥0.001). Even if Brier passed, t=3 bets would almost never fire — model predicts ~3.9% while Kalshi asks ~8¢, always negative edge. The t=3 market exists on Kalshi (threshold 4 in Kalshi ticker format: `KXMLBHIT-...-4`) but the bot will skip it with a C6 log entry on every run. |
| When it should have been caught | Before T-183 architecture was committed. The pooled-model approach masked per-threshold base-rate divergence; the ECE failure on the pooled model forced the per-threshold redesign that revealed t=3 inadequacy. |
| Phases survived | N/A — pre-paper-trading discovery; no capital at risk. |
| Why it survived | T-183 (initial training) used a pooled model which never triggered per-threshold Brier checks; the ECE failure on the first real training run (T-187B setup) forced the diagnostic. |
| Impact | **LOW — paper trading only.** t=3 markets are skipped silently; no bets are placed. No capital risk. EXP-017 commercial validation accumulates t=1 and t=2 bets only, which is the statistically meaningful sample. t=3 exclusion does not affect G6 evidence review. |
| Discovery | SESSION 81 — T-187B per-threshold diagnostic: separate pipeline fit per threshold revealed t=3 Brier delta=+0.0002 < 0.001 gate. Confirmed that even at AUC=0.61, the low base rate (3.9%) means correct t=3 predictions contribute near-zero Brier improvement in absolute terms. |
| Resolution | **T-187H (2026-06-24, SESSION 82) — shadow gate re-evaluation COMPLETE. VALID NEGATIVE — structural failure confirmed.** T-187G's live SP overlay prerequisite was met. SP coverage verified: 99.9% of t=3 positive events (h≥3) already had `opp_sp_is_known=1` in training data. T-187G addresses inference-time SP staleness only — it does not change training data. Shadow gate re-evaluation: Brier delta=+0.0002 (FAIL — identical to T-187B), ECE=0.0037 (PASS), AUC=0.6141. Score distribution: p50=0.037 (≈ base rate); only 1.9% of holdout predictions exceed 0.08 (≈ Kalshi market price). Root cause is structural: at 3.9% base rate, AUC=0.61 cannot produce absolute Brier delta ≥0.001. Further reconsideration requires architectural improvement (non-linear model, richer pitcher-batter matchup features, or lower Kalshi market price ≤5–6%) — not SP data quality improvement. `THRESHOLDS = [1, 2]` and `_EXCLUDED_THRESHOLDS = [3]` remain unchanged. |
| Status | **DEFERRED — structural.** T-187H (2026-06-24 SESSION 82) confirmed the failure is base-rate structural, not a data quality issue resolvable by better SP coverage. t=3 deferred indefinitely pending architectural improvement. LOW urgency. Linked: T-187B, T-187G, T-187H, GG-004. |

### GG-043 — MLB Kelly fraction inherited from NBA without MLB-specific calibration

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-043 |
| Category | Architectural placeholder — calibration value ported cross-sport without derivation |
| What was missed | `DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION = 0.25` (global) and T1 MLB band config `kelly_fraction = 0.10` are inherited from the NBA arm. The 0.10 fraction was calibrated for NBA (AUC, variance, and base-rate structure validated at G4). MLB is a structurally different sport: different hit-rate distributions, different market thinness, different base-rate variance across thresholds (t=1 base_rate≈27%, t=2≈6.5%). The fractional Kelly appropriate for MLB has not been derived. |
| When it should have been caught | Before MLB T1 activation. Should have been a pre-MLB-G1 derivation task. |
| Phases it survived | MLB pre-G1 (paper trading not yet active) |
| Why it survived | MLB arm was built using NBA architecture as a template. The band config file propagates the NBA 0.10 fraction; no MLB-specific Kelly derivation task was registered. |
| Impact | **LOW now — paper trading only, NO live capital.** Kelly fraction affects position size. If 0.10 underestimates the optimal fraction, we under-size bets and accumulate evidence more slowly. If it overestimates, we risk larger drawdowns per resolved bet. Neither consequence is material at paper trading scale ($958.99 bankroll). Risk escalates at G6 (live capital). |
| Discovery | SESSION 90 (2026-06-27) — T-198 governance gap registration during MLB-G1 gate close. Identified during pre-gate institutional audit. |
| Resolution | **T-198 (registration).** MLB-specific Kelly fraction derivation required before G6 live capital. Resolution path: after ≥30 MLB paper bets resolved, compute empirical win rate, edge distribution, and variance from MLB paper portfolio. Derive MLB band kelly via `f* = edge / variance`. Register result via ADR before G6 MLB capital authorization. |
| Status | **OPEN.** Deferred to MLB pre-G6 (requires resolved paper bet sample). LOW urgency at paper stage. Linked: T-198, ADR-0010, DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION in risk.py. |

### GG-044 — C7 drift baselines use NBA calibration values for MLB sport monitoring

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-044 |
| Category | Architectural placeholder — monitoring baseline ported cross-sport without derivation |
| What was missed | `BRIER_BASELINE = 0.2416` and `ECE_BASELINE = 0.0247` in `monitor.py` are NBA G4 TEST set values (fitted on 80-player NBA scoring outcomes). These constants are used by `SystemMonitor` to evaluate drift in the rolling forecast window. MLB hit prediction is a structurally different problem: binary classification (hits ≥ threshold) with lower base rates (t=1: 27%, t=2: 6.5%) vs. NBA points thresholds (base rates 30–50% depending on line). The correct MLB Brier baseline depends on the MLB model's calibration and hit-rate distribution — values that will only be known after the first resolved MLB paper bets. Using NBA baselines for MLB monitoring means the C7 drift alarm could fire incorrectly (NBA baseline too tight for MLB) or miss real drift (NBA baseline too loose). |
| When it should have been caught | Before MLB monitoring was configured. Should have been a pre-MLB-G1 baseline derivation task. |
| Phases it survived | MLB pre-G1 (calib_monitor cron not yet enabled — explicitly PAUSED in deploy/cron.conf pending this gap) |
| Why it survived | `SystemMonitor` is sport-agnostic by design (C7 requires no sport-specific logic). The constants are module-level defaults that were never sport-parameterized. MLB monitoring was parked behind the calib_monitor cron which is NOT enabled at MLB-G1 (requires closed positions). |
| Impact | **LOW now — calib_monitor cron is explicitly NOT enabled at MLB-G1 (no closed positions to evaluate).** Risk escalates when the calib_monitor cron is enabled (post first MLB resolutions). If enabled with NBA baselines, drift alarms could mis-fire or fail to fire on genuine MLB model degradation. |
| Discovery | SESSION 90 (2026-06-27) — T-198 governance gap registration during MLB-G1 gate close. Drift baselines flagged during ARE WE COMPROMISED? protocol review. |
| Resolution | **T-198 (registration).** MLB-specific baselines required before enabling the calib_monitor cron. Resolution path: after ≥30 MLB paper bets resolved, compute empirical MLB Brier score and ECE from the resolved paper portfolio. Register MLB baselines via ADR as sport-specific `BRIER_BASELINE_MLB` / `ECE_BASELINE_MLB` constants, or parameterize `SystemMonitor.__init__` to accept sport-specific baselines. Enable calib_monitor cron only after baseline ADR is accepted. |
| Status | **OPEN.** Deferred to post-first-MLB-resolutions. LOW urgency (calib_monitor cron not enabled). Linked: T-198, monitor.py BRIER_BASELINE/ECE_BASELINE, calib_monitor cron in deploy/cron.conf. |

### GG-045 — Maximum Effective Bankroll Constrained by Kalshi Market Depth: Capital Ceiling Unanalyzed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-045 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — scaling ceiling absent from governance documentation |
| What was missed | Kelly criterion assumes infinite market depth. Kalshi KXMLBHIT player prop markets are thin (estimated $500–$2,000 per market). At $1K bankroll with 25% Kelly fraction, position sizes are $50–$250 — within market depth. At $10K+ bankroll, full Kelly sizing would start moving prices against the bot, degrading edge before the position fills. The maximum effective bankroll this strategy can deploy is constrained by Kalshi market depth, independent of bankroll math or Kelly fraction calibration. This ceiling has never been analyzed or documented. Current paper-trading bankroll ($1,000 reset) is well below any likely ceiling. The gap becomes binding during G8–G9 (multi-sport, large capital deployment). |
| When it should have been caught | Before G6 go/no-go on live capital. Current $1K paper makes it non-urgent, but the live capital threshold (G6: 30 resolved bets, positive P&L) does not include market depth analysis as a gate condition. If live capital is authorized and then scaled, the ceiling could be hit without warning. |
| Phases it survived | G0–G5 (bankroll small enough that depth is not binding at any point in the paper period) |
| Why it survived | Kelly sizing math is correct at current bankroll. Market depth was never explicitly modeled — the system treats Kalshi markets as having infinite liquidity, which is correct at $1K but not at scale. |
| Impact | LOW now ($1K paper). MEDIUM at G6 live capital ($1K–$5K depending on market). HIGH at G8–G9 (multi-sport deployment, $10K+). If unaddressed, live capital deployment could hit the depth ceiling without a mechanism to detect it — positions partially fill at degraded prices, effective edge disappears, but C4 continues sizing at full Kelly. |
| Discovery | SESSION 94 (2026-06-26) — strategic read-through of project by external analyst; surfaced in four-point structural assessment of project architecture. |
| Resolution | Pre-G8: empirical analysis of Kalshi KXMLBHIT order book depth per market; derive maximum effective stake per market as a function of market depth; add depth-aware position size cap to C4 (overrides Kelly when market is thin). Alternatively: accept the ceiling as a known hard limit, document the maximum deployable bankroll explicitly, and commit to never exceeding it without a depth re-analysis. |
| Status | **OPEN.** LOW urgency (current bankroll $1,000). Must be addressed before G8 live capital scaling beyond estimated market depth ceiling. |

### GG-046 — T4 Commercial Validation: Independent Alpha Hypothesis Untested and Unregistered

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-046 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — T4 has a distinct alpha hypothesis with no validation experiment registered |
| What was missed | T4's alpha hypothesis ("market is slow to update to live game state; bot can get there first") is structurally distinct from T1's hypothesis ("calibrated historical model beats pregame market pricing"). EXP-017 tests T1's commercial case. No experiment is registered to test T4's commercial case. The governance framework (GG-004, EXP-017 design, ADR-0024 validation gates) was designed entirely around T1-style hypothesis testing. If T4 wins on a different information edge (market microstructure / reaction speed), T1 failure does not imply T4 failure. Conversely, T1 success does not validate T4. |
| When it should have been caught | Before T4 (MLB) was enabled for paper trading. ADR-0033 authorized T4 paper positions; T4's independent commercial validation was not a prerequisite. |
| Phases it survived | All phases — T4 and T1 were conceptually treated as parts of the same strategy without articulating the structural distinction in governance documentation. |
| Why it survived | The structural distinction (T1 = forecasting accuracy, T4 = market microstructure) was never explicitly documented. T4 was treated as a signal extension of T1 rather than an independent alpha source requiring its own validation track. |
| Impact | LOW in paper phase (no live capital). MEDIUM post-G6 if live capital is authorized based on T1 EXP-017 evidence and T4 is scaled alongside it — T4 could have negative expected value independently of T1. The kill switch and Kelly sizing provide protection, but a misclassified alpha thesis can bleed slowly without triggering either mechanism. |
| Discovery | SESSION 94 (2026-06-26) — strategic read-through surfaced the structural distinction between T1 (forecasting accuracy) and T4 (market microstructure / information speed). |
| Resolution | Define a T4 commercial validation experiment (EXP-T4-01) analogous to EXP-017: compare T4 model_p against live Kalshi market price at the moment each evaluation fired; compute net_edge over resolved bets; apply ADR-0024 Tier 1/2 thresholds independently. Requires ≥500 resolved T4 bets at unique markets (ADR-0024 Tier 2). Gate: T4 should not receive live capital allocation until independently validated by EXP-T4-01. Bug GG-050 must be fixed and T4 portfolio reset before clean data accumulates. |
| Status | **OPEN — SESSION 99 CORRECTION.** EXP-T4-01 SESSION 98 analysis RETRACTED: GG-050 bug (per-cycle `fired_tickers` reset) inflated n from 73 true unique markets to 1274 (17.5× overcounting). Corrected findings (73 independent markets, 2 game nights): win rate 37.0%, P&L −$0.18, EV −$0.0025/market (INCONCLUSIVE). n=73 does NOT satisfy ADR-0024 Tier 2 (need ≥500). Model calibration severely overconfident at model_p extremes. One positive signal: edge monotonicity correct (Q1→Q4 monotonically ordered win rates). Current T4 portfolio data is contaminated — must reset after GG-050 fix deployed. GG-046 OPEN until EXP-T4-01 re-run on ≥500 clean unique markets. See DECISION_LOG SESSION 98/99. |

Dataset re-frozen (158 closed, sha256 `54bac8b2…`); G6 baseline re-ratified −$248.90/−24.9% headline, −$212.25/−21.2% clean (SNAP-2026-06-16-001). Contamination story flipped to a −$36.65 net drag. |
| GG-035 | 2026-06-16 | **ADR-0026 ACCEPTED** — C4 portfolio-level free-cash invariant. Two tiers on aggregate open non-isolated stake: 8a solvency (`>equity` ⇒ KILL_SWITCH `portfolio_over_leverage`), 8b policy cap (`>0.60×equity` ⇒ REJECT `aggregate_deployment_cap`, per-bet). 60% = `1−RESERVE_MANDATE_PCT` (ADR-0010 reserve promoted from advisory to enforced). C4 stays pure via new `PaperTrader.exposure_snapshot()`; `run_paper_trade.py` supplies it. Verified: G5 replay plateaus at $449.27 cap, never reaches the real $1,003.38. 10 new tests, 974 suite green. Live manifestation already relieved by GG-036 (bankroll +$751.10). 60% threshold re-derived via ADR at G6. |
