### GG-050 — T4 MLB Re-Betting Bug: `fired_tickers` Per-Cycle Scope Allowed Same Market to Be Bet 17.5× Per Game Day

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-050 |
| Category | Implementation bug — per-cycle dedup guard instead of game-day dedup guard |
| What was missed | `fired_tickers: set[str] = set()` was initialized INSIDE `_run_cycle()` and reset on every ~90-second evaluation cycle. The intent was to prevent a market from being bet more than once. The actual effect: markets could receive a new position on every cycle (1 new position per 90s × number of cycles per game ≈ up to 65 positions per market per game day). Observed data: 73 unique kalshi_tickers bet an average of 17.5 times each (range 1×–65×) during June 27–28 game nights. n=1274 total positions from only 73 independent markets. |
| When it should have been caught | Before T4 MLB paper trading was enabled. The dedup logic was copied from the NBA T4 runner but not audited for scope correctness. |
| Phases it survived | June 27–28 game nights (SESSION 94 T4 MLB paper enabled through SESSION 99). GG-046 EXP-T4-01 analysis (SESSION 98) consumed the contaminated data and produced a wrong DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE verdict. |
| Why it survived | No test validated that a market bet in cycle N would be skipped in cycle N+1. The per-cycle scope appeared correct in unit tests (which test single-call behavior). The cross-cycle dedup was never part of any test assertion. |
| Impact | HIGH. (1) Portfolio data contaminated: `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` contains 1441 closed positions but only 73 independent markets. The data is usable only with first-occurrence deduplication. (2) EXP-T4-01 analysis falsely showed n=1274, EV=+$0.0795, DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE, GG-046 RESOLVED — all wrong. True corrected result: n=73, EV=−$0.0025, INCONCLUSIVE. (3) GG-046 incorrectly marked RESOLVED in SESSION 98; corrected to OPEN in SESSION 99. |
| Discovery | SESSION 99 — re-analysis of `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` with `kalshi_ticker` deduplication. 1274 bets → 73 unique markets. 81/92 markets bet more than once. `fired_tickers: set[str] = set()` located at line 525 inside `_run_cycle()` body. |
| Resolution | **T-208 COMPLETE — SESSION 99.** `fired_tickers` removed from `_run_cycle()`. `already_fired_game: Optional[set] = None` parameter added to function signature (default `None` → fresh `set()` per call for test backward-compatibility). `main()`: `already_fired_game: set[str] = set()` initialized before the polling loop; passed to every `_run_cycle()` call; `already_fired_game.clear()` added to date-rollover block. 2113/2113 tests. **T4 portfolio on VPS must be reset before new EXP-T4-01 data is accumulated.** GG-046 remains OPEN. |
| Status | **RESOLVED SESSION 99 (T-208) — code fix complete.** Portfolio reset + new EXP-T4-01 data accumulation required before GG-046 can be closed. |

### GG-047 — T1 MLB Ticker Date Off-By-One: Kalshi Markets Consistently Dated One Day Before Game

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-047 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-28 SESSION 96 — PIPE-05 ran June 27 with 149 Kalshi markets, matched 0 players. Root cause: `_ticker_to_game_date` extracted June 26 from ticker `KXMLBHIT-26JUN271310NYYBOS-...` but `GAME_DATE_ISO=2026-06-27`. Kalshi uses the market creation date (day before game) in the ticker, not the game date. |
| Category | Implementation bug — date parsing assumption wrong |
| What was missed | `_ticker_to_game_date` was written assuming Kalshi's `{DD}` field equals the game date. Observed: Kalshi opens MLB hit markets the day before the game and uses that creation date in the ticker. T1 has been dark since MLB-G1 gate open (no matches, 0 bets placed). |
| When it should have been caught | Before MLB-G1 gate close (SESSION 90). The function was unit-tested with developer-chosen ticker dates, not live Kalshi samples. |
| Impact | HIGH during MLB season — T1 blind since MLB-G1 gate open. All 11:00 UTC PIPE-05 runs through June 27 returned 0 matched players. Zero T1 bets placed. |
| Resolution | **SESSION 96 fix was wrong.** T-205 assumed `+timedelta(days=1)` because Kalshi creates markets exactly 1 day before the game. **Real ticker format is `{CREATE_DD}{MON}{GAME_DD}{HHMM}`** — the 3rd 2-digit group IS the game day-of-month, not a 2-digit year. Kalshi creates markets 1–3 days early. SESSION 97 (T-206a): regex changed from `r"-([0-9]{2})([A-Z]{3})[0-9]{2}[0-9]{4}"` to `r"-[0-9]{2}([A-Z]{3})([0-9]{2})[0-9]{4}"`, date from `f"{m.group(2)}{m.group(1)}{year}"` (no timedelta). Tests corrected and extended (3 new cases). Committed `d5289a0`. DECISION_LOG entry 2026-06-29. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-29 SESSION 97** — Real fix: extract GAME_DD directly. 2113/2113 tests pass. VPS at `d5289a0`. |

### GG-048 — MLB Stats API 406 Errors: Resolution Pipeline Cannot Score T4 Bets When API Rejects VPS

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-048 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-28 SESSION 96 — PIPE-05R June 27 morning run: ALL MLB Stats API calls returned `406 Not Acceptable` for player game logs. 80+ T4 bets placed during June 26-27 games were unresolvable. June 28 morning run succeeded (same API, same endpoints). |
| Category | Operational fragility — external API returns client error; resolution pipeline silently skips all bets with no retry |
| What was missed | MLB Stats API (`statsapi.mlb.com`) occasionally returns `406 Not Acceptable` for the game log endpoint used in PIPE-05R. The existing `_fetch_mlb_hits()` catch handles the error gracefully (skips with log), but there is no retry with delay, no fallback endpoint, and no alert if the entire resolution run scores 0 bets. |
| When it should have been caught | Before T4 paper positions were placed. The resolution pipeline was validated with responses but not tested for systematic 406 rejection. |
| Impact | MEDIUM in paper phase — 406 errors cause T4 bets to age out under 48h grace period if the API is consistently unavailable. P&L signal for those bets is permanently lost. In live trading: every 406 run extends open position exposure and delays capital release. |
| Resolution path | (1) Add exponential retry (3×, 5s/10s/30s) to `_fetch_mlb_hits()` before returning empty. (2) Add session-level alert if resolution run scores 0 bets but had ≥1 eligible position (possible silent 406 condition). (3) Register T-207. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-29 SESSION 98 (T-207).** Exponential retry (3×: 5s/10s/30s) added to `_fetch_mlb_hits()`. Zero-resolution alert added to `_resolve_pool()` (warns when eligible positions exist but 0 resolved). DECISION_LOG SESSION 98 T-207. 2113/2113 tests. |

### GG-049 — Session-Start Protocol Has No Operational Heartbeat: Active Pipelines Not Verified at Session Open

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-049 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-29 SESSION 97 — Post-mortem on T1 MLB going dark June 27–28. GG-047 was resolved in S96 with wrong fix (`+timedelta(days=1)`). S97 ARE WE COMPROMISED check did not re-flag it because Step 3 showed GG-047 RESOLVED. No protocol step reads live VPS cron output. |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — governance protocol checks code and documents but not live operational state |
| What was missed | Three consecutive protocol runs (S95 Q6 → registered GG-047; S96 trusted resolved doc → did not re-check live logs; S97 → same omission) each passed without ever running `grep` on `paper_mlb_cron.log`. A single grep would have surfaced "0 players in live MLB markets" immediately. |
| When it should have been caught | S96 session open — after deploying the S96 fix, a cron output check would have confirmed whether the fix actually produced matches. |
| Phases it survived | MLB-G1 gate through SESSION 97 (active paper trading phase) |
| Why it survived | Session-start protocol Steps 1–3 are code and document scans. None reads live VPS cron output. There was no operational heartbeat step. A governance doc can say RESOLVED while the underlying pipeline is still dark — and the protocol had no mechanism to distinguish these two states. |
| Impact | HIGH during active betting phase — T1 MLB placed 0 bets across June 27 and June 28 (2 full game days). Accumulated paper P&L signal permanently lost for those days. The S96 wrong fix was deployed, marked as resolved, and the error went undetected for an entire session. |
| Discovery | SESSION 97 post-mortem when user noted the 3 prior hygiene checks all missed the failure. |
| Resolution | SESSION 97: Added Step 4 (Operational Health Check) to CLAUDE.md mandatory session-start protocol. Command SSHes to VPS and greps `paper_mlb_cron.log` for "players in live MLB markets" / "Bets placed today" / PIPE-05 status. Any "0 players" output during MLB season (March–October) is a Q6 blocker. Also checks T4 MLB systemd and T1 NBA log. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-29 SESSION 97** — Step 4 added to protocol. Future sessions will see VPS cron output at every session open. |

### GG-051 — No Layer 3 Governance: Pipeline Can Be Structurally Sound and Semantically Dark

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-051 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-29 SESSION 100 — post-mortem synthesis on GG-049 (T1 MLB dark 2 days) and GG-050 (T4 re-betting 17.5×). Both failures were invisible to Layer 1 (architecture review) and Layer 2 (C7 Brier/ECE/P&L monitoring). GG-049 was "resolved" by adding an operational heartbeat to the session-start protocol — a human check, not a machine assertion. The structural absence: no runtime layer asks "is what is currently happening coherent with what this band is supposed to do?" |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — governance framework has two of three required layers; Layer 3 was never designed |
| What was missed | Layer 3 semantic contracts: explicit runtime assertions that each pipeline stage and each execution band is behaving consistently with its stated intent. Layer 1 verifies architecture at gate review. Layer 2 verifies model accuracy per monitoring cycle. Neither layer can detect: (a) a pipeline that runs without error but produces no evaluations (GG-049 pattern), (b) a T4 evaluation loop that fires on stale or ghost data (contamination pattern), (c) a band-level model property violation (e.g., T4-CORR increasing a position size, violating ADR-0029 Principle 6). These are not bugs (they pass all tests). They are behavioral coherence failures — the gap between what runs and what was meant. |
| When it should have been caught | Before active paper trading (MLB-G1 gate). The three-layer governance framework is implied by the architecture (C1→C8 + C7 monitoring) but was never explicitly named or formally designed. |
| Phases it survived | G0 through SESSION 100 (all phases). The absence was invisible because Layer 1 + Layer 2 appeared to constitute a complete governance framework. The third layer was only articulated after observing two silent failures whose common root cause was the absence of semantic assertions. |
| Why it survived | Two layers are easier to reason about than three. "Architecture review" + "model monitoring" felt complete. The structural question — "is what's running doing what was intended?" — is a different category of check from both, but was never framed as its own governance requirement. |
| Impact | HIGH (GG-049: 2 dark betting days, T1 MLB; GG-050: 73 independent markets inflated to 1274 positions by re-betting; EXP-T4-01 verdict contaminated). Any future band can go silent or behave incoherently without triggering either existing layer. T4-CORR upward violation (ADR-0029 Principle 6) would be structurally undetectable without this layer. |
| Discovery | SESSION 100 — post-mortem synthesis identified the common root cause of GG-049 and GG-050 as behavioral coherence: no layer asserts that what runs is doing what it was designed to do. |
| Resolution | ADR-0034: `c7_semantic_contracts.py` — SemanticFloorGuard (C1→C2 boundary), ContaminationGuard (T4 pre-evaluation gate), BandInvariantChecker (per-band structural checks), SemanticContractMonitor (C7 health state integration). Integration patches for PIPE-05, run_t4_mlb_live.py, and C7 monitor.py written separately. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-29 SESSION 100** — Module written (`c7_semantic_contracts.py`). Integration patches in `09_INTEGRATION_PATCHES/SEMANTIC_CONTRACTS_INTEGRATION.md`. ADR-0034 registered. Terminal windows deploy. |

### GG-052 — T4 MLB `cycle_start_at` Captured Before Data Fetches: Freshness Window Burned by Kalshi Pagination

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-052 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-30 SESSION 103 — full root cause audit of T4 MLB firing 2 bets all day (vs. expected 50–100). Audit JSONL showed all 1632 staleness halts fired at exact same second per cycle — proving _run_cycle() is sub-second, not the slowness source. |
| Category | Architectural placeholder — freshness window calibrated for a system where data fetches take <5s; actual Kalshi paginated fetch takes 60–130s |
| What was missed | `cycle_start_at = datetime.now(timezone.utc)` was placed BEFORE the ESPN poll loop and Kalshi paginated fetch. Kalshi `fetch_player_points_markets()` paginates across 3+ pages of KXMLBHIT markets with rate-limit backoff (RETRY_DELAY_S × 2^attempt delays of 4, 8, 16, 32s). Total fetch time: 60–130 seconds per cycle. `_FRESHNESS_WINDOW_S = 2.0 × POLL_INTERVAL_S = 90s`. By the time `_run_cycle()` started, `cycle_start_at` was 90–130s old — all `game_state_fresh` checks failed immediately. |
| When it should have been caught | Before T4 MLB went live. The freshness guard was designed for a single fast API call; the Kalshi pagination pattern was added later without revisiting the guard timing. |
| Phases it survived | T4 MLB first live deployment through SESSION 103 (June 30, 2026) |
| Why it survived | The freshness guard appeared to work in tests (single-game, single-page Kalshi response). In production with 15 games and 500+ KXMLBHIT markets, Kalshi returns 3+ pages and triggers rate-limit retries. The test environment never produced a multi-page response. |
| Impact | T4 MLB fired 2 bets on June 30 (expected 50–100). All 1632 staleness contamination halts were false positives from expired `cycle_start_at`, not actual data staleness. |
| Discovery | SESSION 103 — audit JSONL timestamp analysis: all halts per cycle at exact same second → _run_cycle() is sub-second → window burns in the fetch, not the evaluation loop. |
| Resolution | `run_t4_mlb_live.py`: removed `cycle_start_at` from before ESPN polls (line 1098); added `cycle_start_at = snapshot_fetched_at` after Kalshi fetch completes (after line 1116). Data is always fresh at time of evaluation; 90s window now starts when data was actually fetched, not when the cycle began. Committed SESSION 103. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-06-30 SESSION 103** |

---

### GG-053 — GG-052 Staleness Fix Deployed but Unverified Under Live Load

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-053 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — session-start protocol operational health check. |
| Category | Verification gap — fix committed and deployed, but never exercised against real traffic. |
| What was missed | The GG-052 fix (`cycle_start_at = snapshot_fetched_at` after Kalshi fetch, commit `bbaf86a`) was deployed and the `t4-mlb` service restarted at 2026-07-01 01:19:19 UTC. As of this check (05:01 UTC), every MLB game for the day was still `STATUS_SCHEDULED` — zero live batter states have flowed through the fixed code path. The `audit_mlb_t4.jsonl` tail (last entry 00:02:45 UTC) predates the restart and is the pre-fix incident already logged under GG-052, not new evidence the fix works. |
| When it should have been caught | N/A — not a missed bug, a verification gap. Should be closed the first time real live-game data runs through the fixed cycle. |
| Impact | LOW-MEDIUM. If the fix is incomplete (e.g., a different code path still captures a stale timestamp), T4 MLB will silently resume false-positive staleness halts the moment tonight's games go live, and nobody will know until the next manual audit. |
| Resolution path | At next session start (or mid-game spot check) during a live MLB slate, grep `audit_mlb_t4.jsonl` for `CONTAMINATION_HALT` events timestamped after 2026-07-01T01:19:19Z. Expect near-zero `staleness_halts` growth and actual bet fires consistent with the ~50-100/game design target (CLAUDE.md throughput model), not 2/game. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-02 SESSION 108 CLOSE.** Reverified 2026-07-02: the 2026-06-30 evening → 2026-07-01 early-morning game night produced 2,120 `CONTAMINATION_HALT` records with `total_halts=1632, staleness_halts=1632` (100% staleness) — confirmed real, not a measurement artifact. Root cause identified: this is **not** a second, unidentified staleness source distinct from GG-052 — it is **[[GG-062]]** (dual concurrent `t4-mlb` processes), recurring on this exact night. Forensic replay of the 22:36–23:26 UTC segment shows the same interleaved-cycle-ID signature GG-062 documented for the later 23:31–01:19 window (paired `C0000`/`C0001`/... cycle IDs, one series flat at `cg_halts=0`, the other climbing 28→716→...→1632): two processes independently polling and evaluating, one of them chronically unable to keep its `cycle_start_at`/`snapshot_fetched_at` within the 90s freshness window relative to the other's cadence. GG-052's fix (moving `cycle_start_at` after the Kalshi fetch) was correct and is not implicated — the staleness was a symptom of process duplication, not a timestamp-capture bug. Fixed via GG-062's resolution (T-216: atomic `flock`-based singleton lock in `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, deployed). Severity was raised LOW-MEDIUM → MEDIUM-HIGH during the investigation; now closed. |

### GG-054 — SemanticFloorViolation Guard Has Never Fired in Production

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-054 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — session-start protocol, cross-checking `SemanticFloorViolation` count (0) against 4 consecutive T1 MLB 0-player days (June 26-29). |
| Category | Verification gap — a safety mechanism built specifically to catch a known failure pattern (GG-049) has zero real-world activations. |
| What was missed | The guard (ADR-0034, integrated commit `501c4ca8`, 2026-06-29 22:10:14 -0700 = 2026-06-30 05:10 UTC) was deployed *after* the last real 0-player incident it was designed to catch (June 26-29, root-caused as GG-047). The only T1 MLB cron run since deployment (June 30, 11:00 UTC) was healthy — 36 players matched. The guard's fire path (exit code 2, C6 CRITICAL log write) has never executed against genuine bad data end-to-end. |
| When it should have been caught | N/A — timing artifact of when the fix landed relative to when the bug was live. Not a design flaw. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. No confirmation that `_count_scheduled_mlb_games()` vs `matched_players` comparison, the exit(2), and the C6 write all actually function together outside of unit tests. If a similar 0-player bug recurs, we are trusting a guard that has never proven itself live. |
| Resolution path | Consider a deliberate one-time drill: run `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` against a date with known games but an intentionally broken market-matching filter (in a scratch/non-prod invocation) to confirm the guard actually exits non-zero and writes the CRITICAL C6 event, rather than waiting for an unplanned recurrence to be the first test. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-06 SESSION 123.** Added `TestMainSemanticFloorViolationFiresLive` to `tests/test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py` — an isolated (no prod paths touched: `LOG_DIR`/`MODEL_PATH`/`FEATURE_CSV`/`PORTFOLIO_PATH`/`REPORT_PATH` all redirected to a pytest `tmp_path`) end-to-end drill that exercises the exact branch this gap identified as untested: unlike the existing `TestMainNoMarkets` (which passes an *empty* `snap.markets` and exits at the earlier step-5 `sys.exit(0)` guard, never reaching the SemanticFloorGuard call site at all), this drill gives `main()` a non-empty `snap.markets` whose ticker date is deliberately mismatched (simulating a broken market-matching filter), mocks `_is_mlb_game_in_progress→False` and `_count_scheduled_mlb_games→2`, and lets the real, unmocked `AuditLogger` write to a real JSONL file on disk. Confirms, by reading that file back and parsing it: (1) `main()` raises `SystemExit(2)` — the documented exit code; (2) exactly one `CRITICAL` `semantic_floor_violation` event was actually written, with `payload.scheduled_preview_games==2`, `payload.kalshi_markets_total==1`, `component=="PIPE-05"`. Full suite: 52 passed (51 prior + 1 new). The guard's fire path is now proven end-to-end without waiting for a real production incident to be the first test. |

### GG-055 — Ticker Date Parser Fixed Twice; No Month-Boundary Regression Test

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-055 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — session-start protocol, reviewing GG-047 resolution history. |
| Category | Latent risk — two sequential wrong fixes to the same date-parsing function, shipped under deadline pressure. |
| What was missed | T-205 (SESSION 96, June 28) shipped `_ticker_to_game_date() + timedelta(days=1)`, an incorrect assumption that Kalshi always creates markets exactly one day before the game. It passed its own tests and was documented as resolving GG-047, but T1 stayed dark for June 28 and June 29 anyway. T-206a (SESSION 97, June 29, commit `d5289a0`) shipped the real fix — extracting `GAME_DD` directly from the `{CREATE_DD}{MON}{GAME_DD}{HHMM}` ticker format via regex, with no offset arithmetic. No test case exists for a market created in one calendar month for a game in the next (e.g., created 2026-06-30 for a 2026-07-01 game) — exactly the class of edge case that an offset-based (rather than direct-extraction) bug tends to hide in, and the class of bug this function has already produced twice. |
| When it should have been caught | T-206a should have added a month-rollover test case alongside the 3 new cases it did add, given the function's track record. |
| Impact | LOW currently — the current implementation extracts `GAME_DD`/`MON` directly rather than by day-offset, which is structurally safer — but this is unverified by an explicit test, and the same function has now been wrong twice. |
| Resolution path | Add a test case: a ticker created in month N (e.g., `30JUN...`) for a game whose ticker month field is N+1 (e.g., `01JUL...`), asserting correct game-date extraction across the month boundary. Register a T-NNN. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-06 SESSION 123.** Added two tests to `TestTickerToGameDate` in `tests/test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py`: `test_month_boundary_created_in_prior_month` (`KXMLBHIT-30JUL011200...` — created 30-Jun, implied by CREATE_DD, for a 1-Jul game) and `test_month_boundary_short_month_to_long_month` (`KXMLBHIT-31AUG011200...` — created 31-Jul for a 1-Aug game). Both assert the correct `{year}-07-01` / `{year}-08-01` extraction. Confirms directly what the Impact field only asserted by inspection: the current implementation extracts `MON`/`GAME_DD` straight from the ticker via regex and never does CREATE_DD-relative offset arithmetic, so a month boundary between creation and game date cannot desync it — the exact bug class that broke this function twice (T-205's offset-based fix) does not apply to T-206a's direct-extraction fix, and that is now asserted by a test rather than only argued in prose. All 7 `TestTickerToGameDate` cases pass. No production code changed — this closes a test-coverage gap, the underlying function was already correct. |

### GG-056 — NBA Main Pool Dormant at 24.9% Drawdown; No Reactivation Plan Before October Resume

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-056 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — session-start protocol Q1/Q6 cross-check: `risk.py` `max_drawdown_pct = 0.20` (kill-switch trigger) vs. live NBA main-pool realized drawdown of 24.9% (bankroll $751.10 / $1,000 initial, realized P&L -$248.90, per GG-036 ratified baseline). |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — same failure shape as GG-049 (a pipeline that produces nothing looks identical to "no games," not "kill switch engaged"). |
| What was missed | The kill switch is working correctly: `current_drawdown_pct` is computed live per-pool in `run_paper_trade.py` and passed into `evaluate_forecast()`, which correctly halts at >= 0.20. NBA is currently off-season (0 games), so this hasn't manifested as an operational failure — no bets are being evaluated at all. But there is no decision on record for what happens when NBA resumes in October 2026 with the pool still 4.9 points above the kill-switch threshold: the very first live `evaluate_forecast()` call will trip `drawdown_limit_breached` and reject every subsequent bet indefinitely, with no reset mechanism currently defined. |
| When it should have been caught | Should have been flagged as a forward-looking action item when the G5/GG-036 baseline was ratified (-$248.90/-24.9%, SESSION ~66-67) or when the dark-period build plan was adopted, since both predate knowledge that NBA would sit dormant across an off-season while already past its own kill-switch threshold. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. If unaddressed, October's first NBA cron run will silently place 0 bets and, absent Step 4 of the session-start protocol (or a specific check of the kill-switch reason code), will look identical to "no games scheduled yet" rather than "kill switch permanently engaged." |
| Resolution path | Decide, before NBA 26-27 season open (or explicitly defer via a registered T-NNN): (a) NBA resumes on the current drawdown baseline and stays dark until a G6-scoped bankroll/limit revision, or (b) the drawdown baseline / initial_bankroll is formally reset for the new season via ADR, consistent with the standing G6 obligation to formally revise all hard limits (CLAUDE.md Q1, DECISION_LOG 2026-05-29 retroactive entries). |
| Status | OPEN. |

### GG-057 — C8 Web Dashboard Built and Deployed to Production With Zero Governance Trail

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-057 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — institutional read comparing untracked files against governance logs. |
| Category | Process failure — a new production component shipped outside every institutional control this project has (no DECISION_LOG entry, no GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG mention, no MEMORY_BANK/TODO_NEXT reference, not committed to git). |
| What was missed | `scripts/web_dashboard.py` (868-line stdlib HTTP server, replaces a curses TUI that crashed on SSH disconnect), `Open Dashboard.command` (Mac launcher), and `scripts/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` (systemd unit) were all built and deployed. The service is confirmed **live in production** on the VPS — `systemctl status pmbot-web-dashboard` shows `active (running) since 2026-07-01 00:54:58 UTC`, serving portfolio/audit data on `127.0.0.1:8050`. All three files are untracked in git (`git status` shows `??`). This is a genuine new C8 component change with zero paper trail — exactly the class of gap the session-start protocol's Step 1 (`decisions_logged: false` scan) and Step 3 (open GG scan) are designed to catch, except this gap predates any doc existing to scan. |
| When it should have been caught | Before deploying to the VPS — CLAUDE.md hard rule #7 requires every strategic/architectural decision to hit DECISION_LOG.md immediately, not at session end. A new systemd service reading production logs and exposing them on a network port (even localhost-only) is exactly this kind of decision. |
| Impact | LOW-MEDIUM technical risk (read-only, localhost-bound, hardened with `PrivateTmp`/`NoNewPrivileges`/`ReadOnlyPaths`/`MemoryMax`/`CPUQuota` — the engineering itself is sound). MEDIUM-HIGH process risk: it establishes precedent that production deploys can happen without touching a single governance artifact, which is the exact failure mode this project's entire protocol exists to prevent. |
| Resolution path | (1) Commit the three files to git with a commit message documenting what/why. (2) Add a retroactive DECISION_LOG.md entry (following the same pattern as the 2026-05-29 retroactive entries) describing the dashboard's purpose, scope (read-only, localhost+SSH-tunnel only, no write path, no secrets), and the fact it was built/deployed before this entry. (3) Add one line to MEMORY_BANK.md's component inventory / Compass OS Health table so future sessions see it exists. |
| Status | **RESOLVED — SESSION 106.** Files were already committed (`b247cf2`, SESSION 104) — that commit's message claimed to resolve GG-057 but steps (2) and (3) of the resolution path were never actually done. SESSION 106 completed them: retroactive DECISION_LOG.md entry written (see SESSION 106 / GG-057 entry), and MEMORY_BANK.md "Last reviewed" line updated to reference the dashboard's existence. No further action required. |

### GG-058 — T4 MLB UTC-Midnight Rollover Drops All In-Progress Games Every Night

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-058 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 104 — investigating why zero live evaluations occurred anywhere in `t4_mlb_live_cron.log` since the GG-052 fix restart (2026-07-01 01:19:19 UTC). |
| Category | **Architectural bug, not a verification gap.** This is more severe than GG-053's framing ("fix hasn't been tested yet, wait and see") — the fix structurally cannot be tested against West-coast late games under current logic, and a real coverage hole exists independent of GG-052/GG-053. |
| What was missed | `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s date-rollover refresh (lines 1049-1077, "24/7 systemd service support") uses `date.today()` — the VPS's UTC calendar date — as the day boundary. At UTC midnight every night, it runs `game_ids[:] = _espn_mlb_game_ids_today()`, **replacing** the entire tracked game list with the new UTC day's ESPN slate. Confirmed from `t4_mlb_live_cron.log`: this fires nightly (`2026-06-28 00:00:13`, `2026-06-29 00:00:30`, `2026-06-30 00:00:51`, `2026-07-01 00:03:30` — each logged "rollover complete" with a *different, smaller* game/player count than the prior evening, e.g. June 29→30 dropped from 74 players to 31). The new day's ESPN game-ID list (verified: July 1's IDs `401815979-992` vs June 30's `401815964-978` — disjoint ranges) does **not** include the previous day's still-in-progress West-coast games (first pitch commonly 22:30 UTC-01:45 UTC, i.e. straddling the boundary — the schedule validator itself models this correctly, computing one continuous T4 window from 22:05 UTC through ~06:10 UTC the *next* day). The practical effect: every night, at UTC midnight, T4 MLB stops polling whatever games are still live (often mid-to-late innings — the highest-value evaluation window) and does not pick up the new day's games until they start hours later. Confirmed directly for tonight: the 01:19:19 UTC restart (deploying GG-052) initialized with July 1's 14-game list — the 5 June 30 West-coast games still in progress at that moment (first pitch 01:40 UTC, ~21 min before restart) were never touched again. Zero `cycle=` log lines (the marker for an actual live evaluation cycle) appear anywhere in the log after 01:19:19 UTC as of this check (05:20 UTC) — the process has been sleeping in "No live MLB batter states" for 4+ hours despite West-coast games that were in progress. |
| When it should have been caught | At T4 MLB (PIPE-07) design time — the schedule validator's own model (one T4 session spans a UTC-midnight-crossing window) is the correct mental model; the live runner's date-rollover code was never reconciled against it. |
| Impact | **HIGH.** This directly suppresses the ~1,000 evals/game design target (CLAUDE.md throughput mandate) for every West-coast/late game, every single night, not just as a one-off restart artifact. It also means GG-053 cannot be closed by "wait for the next live game" — under current logic, the fix will *never* see live West-coast innings unless the rollover boundary is fixed, because those games get dropped at the exact UTC-midnight instant regardless of restart timing. |
| Resolution path | Change the rollover trigger from a hard UTC-midnight `date.today()` boundary to one anchored to the schedule validator's actual slate window (e.g., only refresh `game_ids` when ALL currently-tracked games have reached `STATUS_FINAL`/`STATUS_POSTPONED`, or merge-in rather than replace: `game_ids` should be the union of not-yet-final previous-day games and newly discovered today-games, dropping a game only once it resolves). Register a T-NNN for immediate investigation — this blocks meaningful T4 MLB throughput validation more than GG-052/GG-053 do. |
| Status | **RESOLVED SESSION 106 (2026-07-01).** Implemented the merge-in fix exactly as the resolution path specified. `run_t4_mlb_live.py`: added `_ESPN_STATUS_FINAL`/`_ESPN_STATUS_POSTPONED`/`_ESPN_TERMINAL_STATUSES` constants; added `_espn_game_status(espn_game_id) -> str` (fetches single-game ESPN status, returns `""` on any fetch failure — a failure is never treated as terminal, so a transient ESPN error cannot silently drop a live game); added `_rollover_game_ids(tracked_game_ids, newly_discovered, status_fn=_espn_game_status) -> list[str]` (pure, independently-testable — carries forward any tracked game not yet in `newly_discovered` and not at a terminal status, then appends new-day discoveries). The main-loop rollover block now calls `merged = _rollover_game_ids(game_ids, newly_discovered)` and assigns `game_ids[:] = merged` instead of the old destructive `game_ids[:] = _espn_mlb_game_ids_today()`. Also fixed a related silent-drop risk found during implementation: the pregame-context refresh (`by_player_id`, `name_to_pregame`) was calling `.clear()` before `.update()` at rollover, which — combined with `_load_pregame_context()` only ever loading the single most-recent day's pregame file — could silently drop player-matching context for carried-over games. Changed to plain `.update()` (additive, no clear); accepted tradeoff that these dicts now only grow, never shrink, judged acceptable given per-player context is small and the service restarts daily regardless (timer + `Restart=on-failure`). Test coverage: `TestEspnGameStatus` (3 tests) and `TestRolloverGameIds` (7 tests) added to `tests/test_run_t4_mlb_live.py`, covering status extraction, fetch-failure-as-non-terminal (the safety-critical case), carry-over across midnight, FINAL/POSTPONED dropping, dedup, and confirming `status_fn` is not called for games already rediscovered. Full suite: 2159 passed, 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (stale Kalshi test-market dates, stale feature CSV — both live-data freshness checks, not regressions). Registered as **T-215**. |

### GG-059 — No Formal Data Quality Contracts at C1 Ingestion: Contamination Reaches C3 Undetected

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-059 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-30 SESSION 104 — Opus strategic review identified absence of schema/cardinality/staleness validation framework at C1 ingestion boundary. |
| Category | Architectural gap — missing validation layer between data sources and forecasting pipeline. |
| What was missed | C1 ingestion has no formal schema contracts, no cardinality validators, and no staleness checks. Concrete failure modes already observed: (1) Feature CSV showed a 3× cardinality jump — not caught at ingestion time; discovered post-hoc during model diagnostics. (2) Player name matching silences (ticker/MLBAM ID pipeline breaks) pass validation-free through C1→C2 and reach C3 producing forecasts on an empty player set. (3) ESPN scoreboard data arriving stale during live T4 windows has no freshness SLA to reject against. The Semantic Floor Guard (ADR-0034, SESSION 101) partially addresses (2) by asserting `matched_players > 0` downstream, but this fires at C2 output, not at C1 ingestion, and does not cover (1) or (3). There is no schema manifest documenting what each source should return, no cardinality baseline to alert against, and no structured per-source validation pass/fail in the audit log. |
| When it should have been caught | Before the T4 MLB staleness defenses (ADR-0034) were designed — the correct layering is: ingestion validates at the source boundary, semantic contracts guard at the pipeline boundary. Currently only the second layer exists. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM ongoing.** Any future data source failure (Stats API format change, ESPN API schema update, Kalshi REST pagination change) passes through to C3 and generates forecasts on corrupted data. The audit log records the bet but not the data quality failure that produced it. Retroactively diagnosing source failures requires manual CSV inspection rather than a structured log query. |
| Discovery | Opus strategic review — SESSION 104. |
| Resolution | Phased: (1) data_contracts.yaml schema manifest per source; ingestion validators (schema + cardinality + staleness + semantic floor) emitting to audit JSONL; C1 early-exit protocol on validator failure. (2) C2 downstream cardinality guard (post-C1 semantic floor). (3) Data quality dashboard view in C8. (4) Per-sport feature CSV isolation (features_mlb.csv, features_nba.csv) to prevent cross-sport contamination. Registered as T-212. |
| Status | **OPEN.** Multi-session build. Non-blocking for current T4 MLB work. Linked: ADR-0034, T-212. |

### GG-060 — Silent Success Indistinguishability: Zero Bets from "No Edge" Looks Identical to Zero Bets from Pipeline Failure

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-060 |
| Discovered | 2026-06-30 SESSION 104 — Opus strategic review identified the inverse of GG-049: a run that evaluates markets and finds no edge is currently indistinguishable in the log from a run where the pipeline silently died. |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — diagnostic oracle gap. Inverse of GG-049. |
| What was missed | C4 risk.py logs bet approvals (positions opened, sizes, Kelly fractions). It does not log evaluations that were rejected due to insufficient edge (`model_p ≤ market_implied_p + vig`), rejected due to risk controls (drawdown limit, exposure cap, size floor), or evaluated and simply found to have no positive edge signal. The result: a cron run that produces zero bets could mean: (A) pipeline died at some layer (GG-049 pattern), (B) C4 correctly evaluated N markets and found 0 with positive edge signal, (C) C4 found positive edge signals but rejected them (drawdown limit breached, exposure cap hit, size too small). Currently, case (B) and case (C) are indistinguishable from each other and from case (A) without manually reading `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` or the audit log at a per-event level. There is no structured per-evaluation log record, no run-level edge opportunity summary, and no mechanism to detect when zero-edge nights cluster (which would indicate model drift or market regime shift). The Semantic Floor Guard (ADR-0034) partially mitigates case (A) for the C1→C2 layer but does not address cases (B) or (C), and provides no edge accounting at the C4 layer. |
| When it should have been caught | When C4 risk.py was designed — every risk control that rejects a bet should emit a structured rejection record, not just a decision to not call `open_position()`. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM ongoing.** Zero-bet runs from "no edge tonight" go undetected as a legitimate outcome vs. a silent failure. Model drift (market getting more efficient over time relative to the model) will not be caught until it shows up as sustained P&L underperformance — which requires resolved bets to diagnose, a 24–48 hour lag at minimum. No experiment (EXP-017, EXP-T4-01) can distinguish "we found edge and captured it" from "we found edge but risk controls blocked it" from the current log structure. |
| Discovery | Opus strategic review — SESSION 104. |
| Resolution | Phased: (1) Per-evaluation C4 decision audit log (every market: model_p, market_implied_p, vig, edge_signal True/False, decision: APPROVED/REJECTED_NO_EDGE/REJECTED_SIZE/REJECTED_DRAWDOWN/REJECTED_EXPOSURE). (2) Run-level edge opportunity report (markets evaluated, edge signals found, bets placed, rejection reasons). (3) Silent Success detector: flag runs where edge_signals == 0 for ≥50 evaluations (market is pricing efficiently relative to model — not a failure, but a signal). (4) Edge signal rate trend (rolling 7-day): detects sustained drift within 24–48h. (5) EXP-017/EXP-T4-01 retrospective template using edge accounting data. Registered as T-213. |
| Status | **IN PROGRESS.** 2026-07-02 SESSION 108: Phase 1 (resolution items 1-3 — per-evaluation edge-decision JSONL sink, `summarize_edge_run()` PIPELINE_BROKEN/NO_EDGE/RISK_REJECTED/ACTIVE classifier, `silent_success_flag` at the ≥50-evaluation threshold) is code-complete, tested (16 new tests, `test_c4_risk.py` 50→66 passing), and wired live into T1 MLB (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py`). Full DECISION_LOG entry: SESSION 108 / T-213 Phase 1 row. Phases 2 (7-day edge-rate trend), 4 (EXP-017/EXP-T4-01 retrospective template), and wiring into `run_t4_mlb_live.py`/`run_t2_mlb_sameday.py`/EXP harnesses remain OPEN. Structurally independent of GG-059 but complements it. Linked: GG-049, T-213. |

### GG-061 — ADR Authorization Conflated the NBA T4 Engine with an MLB Paper-Trading Ruling

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-061 |
| Category | Process gap — ADR drafting did not verify the target file/system matched the sport domain the ADR described. |
| What was missed | ADR-0036 (SESSION 104, 2026-06-30) authorized setting `_PHASE8_ACTIVE = True` in `t4_execution_engine.py` and ruled GG-038/GG-039 exempt and GG-040 partially resolved, describing the data sources in MLB terms ("batter stats," "innings," "batting slot"). `t4_execution_engine.py` is the NBA T4 sub-band engine (ADR-0029, SESSION 66-67) — it operates on `quarter`/`pts_so_far`/`stat_type="pts"`, a 48-minute NBA game clock, and is consumed only by `t4_live_runner.py`/`t4_price_cache.py` (NBA-only per CLAUDE.md's own band description). `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, the actual live MLB T4 runner, does not import any of this code and has no sub-band architecture. No step in drafting or accepting ADR-0036 checked that the file being authorized for a change actually matched the sport its rationale described. |
| When it should have been caught | During ADR-0036 drafting — a single grep for MLB-specific field names (`h_so_far`, `ab_so_far`, `batting_slot`) against `t4_execution_engine.py` would have shown zero matches and surfaced the conflation immediately. |
| Phases it survived | None in production — caught before any code was written for T-211 (the implementation task ADR-0036 authorized), during pre-implementation file review. No live or paper capital was affected. |
| Why it survived | ADR-0036 was drafted in the context of an MLB-focused session discussion and described data availability using MLB vocabulary without re-verifying against the actual code being amended. The same session's own NBA-tabling directive (which explicitly lists `t4_live_runner.py`/`t4_price_cache.py` as off-limits) was logged separately and never cross-referenced against ADR-0036, despite both entries being written the same day. |
| Impact | **Would have been MEDIUM-HIGH if executed as written.** Setting `_PHASE8_ACTIVE=True` in `t4_execution_engine.py` would have activated the dormant NBA in-play sub-band engine — a live-trading-logic change to a component under an explicit operator tabling directive — currently a practical no-op only because NBA is off-season (0 active games), not because the change was reviewed as NBA-scoped and approved. Caught pre-implementation; zero actual impact. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-01 SESSION 105 — during T-211 implementation prep, direct code read of `t4_execution_engine.py` (field names, GamePhase thresholds, evaluator internals) contradicted ADR-0036's MLB-flavored rationale. Confirmed via grep: no file imports `t4_execution_engine` except `t4_live_runner.py`, `t4_price_cache.py`, and `c7_monitoring/semantic_contracts.py`; `run_t4_mlb_live.py` has zero references to it. Flagged to DS via AskUserQuestion before any T-211 code was written; DS chose to halt and correct rather than proceed. |
| Resolution | ADR-0037 (correction): vacates T-211 as scoped, reverts GG-038/GG-039/GG-040 to OPEN with the conflation documented, and states that no MLB sub-band system currently exists to activate in its place. Process fix: any ADR that authorizes a code/flag change to a specific file must include a verification step confirming the file's actual domain (grep for sport-specific field names or an explicit read of the target function) before the ADR is drafted, not just before implementation. |
| Status | **RESOLVED SESSION 105** — via ADR-0037. Process-fix recommendation (verify target file domain before drafting an ADR that changes it) is not itself enforced by tooling; it is a documented practice, not a gate. Linked: ADR-0029, ADR-0036, ADR-0037. |

### GG-062 — Dual Concurrent `t4-mlb` Processes Recurred Undetected for ~1h48m (T-210 Fix Class Regressed)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-062 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 106 — while investigating a "double-rollover, 28s apart" oddity flagged in the prior session's handoff, during GG-058 root-cause work on `t4_mlb_live_cron.log`. |
| Category | Regression of a previously-fixed failure class. T-210 (commit `670b88b`, "eliminate dual concurrent T4 MLB sessions") was believed to have closed this bug permanently via `ExecStartPre=-/usr/bin/pkill -f run_t4_mlb_live.py` in `t4-mlb.service`. It did not. |
| What was missed | Forensic log analysis proved two independent `run_t4_mlb_live.py` processes ran concurrently for roughly **1h48m, from ~23:31 UTC 2026-06-30 through 01:19:19 UTC 2026-07-01**. Evidence chain: (1) `cycle_n` is monotonic-only by construction — initialized to 0, incremented exactly once per completed cycle, no reset path anywhere in the file. (2) `t4_mlb_live_cron.log` showed non-monotonic cycle numbers interleaved (a `cycle=0029` summary line followed by a `cycle=0028` line). (3) Extracting the full window of `cycle=` summary lines resolved this into two clearly distinct, independently-progressing series: one with `cg_halts` climbing 878→1632 and session totals `eval=50`/`fired=2`; the other permanently flat at `cg_halts=0`, `eval=0`, `fired=0` (an orphaned process that never found live batter states, likely started against a stale/rolled-over game list). (4) `audit_mlb_t4.jsonl` showed only one `session_id` in the window — consistent with the orphan producing no audit-worthy events, not proof against it. (5) `systemctl status`/`pgrep -af run_t4_mlb_live` confirmed only one PID (781451, started 2026-07-01 05:52:38 UTC) alive at investigation time — the orphan is gone now, but was not caught while it ran. (6) `journalctl -u t4-mlb` showed systemd itself restarting the unit twice, 10 seconds apart, at `Jun 30 06:30:35` and `Jun 30 06:30:45` — the most probable origin: the second restart's `ExecStartPre=-pkill` fired before the first restart's new process had fully registered under `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s process name, or the `sleep 2` grace window raced a slow-starting first instance. The orphan then survived undetected for ~19 hours until the next restart (01:19:19 UTC, deploying GG-052) finally cleared it. This is the same failure shape T-210 was built to eliminate; the `pkill -f` pattern-match approach is race-prone by construction (it depends on timing between process death and process rename/registration, not on an atomic lock), and this incident is direct proof it can still fail. |
| When it should have been caught | T-210 should have included a regression test or a runtime self-check (e.g., a PID-file lock that refuses a second start rather than relying on best-effort pre-start cleanup of the *previous* instance). No such check exists today — this recurrence was only caught by manual forensic log reading, not by any automated signal. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM-HIGH.** For ~1h48m, T4 MLB ran with unknown double-counting risk on any bet placed by either process against the same shared portfolio/ledger file (paper capital only — no live capital exposure). It also means the operational health check in this project's own session-start protocol (Step 4) would not have caught this: both processes report to the same log file, and `systemctl status` only shows the current PID, not historical overlaps. Undetected for the full window; only surfaced via manual cross-session forensic work. |
| Resolution path | Replace the `pkill -f`-based singleton guard with a proper PID-file lock (e.g., `flock` on a well-known lock file, or an atomic PID-file check-and-write at process start that refuses to proceed if a live PID is already recorded) — this is deterministic and race-free, unlike pattern-matching a process name that may not have deregistered yet. Additionally, add a runtime self-check that logs a `DUAL_SESSION_DETECTED` audit event if two `session_id`s ever appear active in the same audit window, so this failure class is caught automatically rather than requiring manual log forensics. Registered as **T-216**. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-02 SESSION 108 CLOSE (T-216).** Confirmed the incident window this status previously documented extends further than first scoped: GG-053's independent reverification (staleness_halts=1632, 100% staleness, on the 2026-06-30→07-01 game night) is the *same* dual-process incident, not a second unexplained staleness source — the 22:36–23:26 UTC segment of that night shows the identical interleaved-cycle-ID signature (paired `C0000`/`C0001`/... cycle IDs, one series flat at `cg_halts=0`, the other climbing 28→716) that is the early portion of the 878→1632 climb this entry already documented for the later window. Fix implemented: `acquire_singleton_lock()` in `run_t4_mlb_live.py` — an atomic, non-blocking `flock(LOCK_EX \| LOCK_NB)` on `logs/t4_mlb_live.lock`, acquired as the first action in `main()` before any shared-state file is touched. A losing process logs the conflict and `sys.exit(1)`; systemd's existing `Restart=on-failure` (60s cooldown) retries until the lock is free — no crash-loop risk. Unlike the `pkill -f` pattern-match this replaces as the authoritative guard, `flock` is kernel-serialized (no race window) and self-releases on process death (crash or normal exit), so it cannot leak a stale lock across a restart the way a manual PID-file check-and-write could. `t4-mlb.service`'s `ExecStartPre=pkill` is kept as a fast-path cleanup only — comment updated to state it is no longer relied on for correctness. 5 new tests in `test_run_t4_mlb_live.py::TestAcquireSingletonLock` (first-acquire, concurrent-holder-blocks, released-after-close, PID recorded); full suite 2176/6/1 (1 pre-existing unrelated local-data-freshness failure, not from this change); mypy strict shows 26 pre-existing errors in this file (GG-065 baseline, none in new code). Deployed 2026-07-02 05:41 UTC: `git pull` (fast-forward `b001441..f81edb0`) then `deploy/install_systemd_t4_mlb.sh` (reinstalls unit + reloads systemd + restarts service) — run during a confirmed non-live window (all MLB games `STATUS_SCHEDULED` at deploy time, no in-flight evaluation interrupted). Post-restart verification: `ps aux` shows exactly one `run_t4_mlb_live.py` process (PID 808451); `logs/t4_mlb_live.lock` contains that same PID; fresh log shows a single `session_id=T4-MLB-DCD47381` with no duplicate/interleaved cycle IDs. Single-process operation confirmed. Deferred, not blocking: the `DUAL_SESSION_DETECTED` audit self-check from the original resolution path — the deterministic flock fix removes the race this was meant to catch, so it's now a lower-value defense-in-depth item rather than the primary fix; can be picked up later if desired. |

### GG-063 — 100% of All MLB Kalshi Demo Order Submissions Have Failed Since Inception (T1 and T4, ADR-0008 Never Achieved for MLB)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-063 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 106 — while ruling out a duplicate-rebet bug as a contributor to the T1 MLB 50-bet anomaly, inspecting `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` for a sample record surfaced `"kalshi_order_status": "failed: HTTP 410"` on every position checked. Widened to the full portfolio history for confirmation. |
| Category | Silent, 100%-reproducible execution failure, present since the feature's introduction — not a transient/rate-limit condition like the previously-known GG-014 (429/503) failure class. |
| What was missed | Full-history census of every MLB paper position, both bands: **T1 MLB — 52/52 positions ever recorded, 100% `"failed: HTTP 410"`, zero successes** (`paper_portfolio_mlb.json`). **T4 MLB — 15/15 positions ever recorded, 100% `"failed: HTTP 410"`, zero successes** (T4 MLB portfolio). By contrast, **NBA T1 on the identical code path** (`kalshi_client.submit_order()`, shared across sports) shows `{'resting': 87, 'executed': 28, 'failed: HTTP 503': 18, 'failed: HTTP 429': 16, 'failed: transient...': 9}` out of 158 positions — a 73% real-confirmation rate. This proves the failure is not RSA signing, not credentials, not the shared `_post()`/retry path — it is specific to MLB (`KXMLBHIT` series) tickers. HTTP 410 ("Gone") is semantically distinct from 429/503: it indicates the referenced resource does not exist / is no longer available at the given path, not a transient server condition — retries do not apply and none of the existing GG-014 retry logic (`(429, 503)`) covers it. Root cause is NOT yet isolated: `mkt.ticker` used in `submit_order()` is the exact same string returned moments earlier by the live `GET /markets?series_ticker=KXMLBHIT&status=open` DS-02 fetch (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py:956`, `run_t4_mlb_live.py:889` both pass `mkt.ticker`/`ticker` straight through), so the market is confirmed open/listed at fetch time yet is rejected as Gone at order-submission time seconds to minutes later. The actual Kalshi response body is not captured anywhere in the current code — `kalshi_client.py` `_post()` truncates `r.text[:500]` into the `_post` return tuple, but `submit_order()` only forwards `str(data.get("error", data))`, which resolves to the bare `f"HTTP {r.status_code}"` string — the diagnostic body text is discarded before it ever reaches `paper_trader.py` or the audit log. This is why root cause cannot be conclusively determined from historical records alone. |
| When it should have been caught | At ADR-0008 signing (2026-05-31) the ADR's stated purpose was "external confirmation that the bot's forecast → sizing → order chain works end-to-end" and "two-level confirmation" strengthening G6 evidence quality. No verification step was ever run to confirm MLB order submissions actually succeeded — the SESSION 95 DECISION_LOG entry (T4 MLB Kalshi wiring) asserts "Verified: ... Kalshi client active" based on `budget_used` incrementing, which confirms the *attempt* was made, not that any order was *accepted*. This is the same failure shape as GG-060 (Silent Success Indistinguishability): the local ledger continues normally regardless of Kalshi confirmation status (by ADR-0008 design — "the bot does not halt"), so a 100% external-confirmation failure rate produces zero visible symptoms in bankroll, P&L, or bet-count metrics. Every session-start protocol run to date checked cron liveness and bet counts, never Kalshi order confirmation rates per sport. |
| Impact | **HIGH — evidentiary, not capital.** No live capital is at risk (paper mode; local ledger remains authoritative per ADR-0008 §"Governance constraints," so the G6 gate's 30-resolved-paper-bets criterion is technically unaffected). But ADR-0008's actual purpose — external, Kalshi-side confirmation of the forecast→sizing→order chain, offered as *strengthened* evidence for G6 — has never been realized for MLB, for either band, for the entire history of MLB paper trading. If G6 sign-off leans on "two-level confirmation" as part of its evidence narrative, that narrative is false for the MLB leg of the portfolio. This also means MLB order-sizing/contract-count logic in `submit_order()` (price-cents rounding, contract-count flooring) has never been exercised against a real Kalshi acceptance path — an untested code path going into any future live-capital transition. |
| Resolution path | (1) **Immediate, low-risk diagnostic fix**: thread the actual response body (already captured as `r.text[:500]` inside `_post()`) through `submit_order()`'s error path and into `kalshi_order_status`/the `kalshi_order_failed` audit payload, so the *next* MLB order attempt reveals Kalshi's actual rejection reason instead of a bare status code. (2) Once the real error text is visible, isolate whether this is a market-vs-event ticker mismatch, a Kalshi KXMLBHIT-specific order-eligibility rule (e.g., a settlement/trading-window restriction not present on KXNBAPTS), or an account/permissions scoping issue specific to the demo key's MLB series access. (3) Re-verify against a manual, deliberate single-order test once root cause is hypothesized, before assuming any code fix resolved it. Registered as **T-217**. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-01 (SESSION 108, T-217).** Step (1) done: `submit_order()`'s error path now surfaces `data.get("body")`/`data.get("detail")` alongside the existing `data.get("error", data)`. Step (2) real root cause, once visible: `deprecated_v1_order_endpoint` — Kalshi hard-deprecated `POST /portfolio/orders` (v1) platform-wide; not a ticker mismatch, not a KXMLBHIT eligibility rule, not a demo-key permissions gap (all three original candidate hypotheses ruled out). NBA's 73% success rate on the "identical" code path is explained by timing, not a code-path difference: NBA's 158 T1 bets predate Kalshi's v1 enforcement (zero HTTP 410s in the NBA breakdown above); MLB paper trading began after enforcement, so 100% of MLB orders hit the dead endpoint. Fix: `submit_order()` migrated to `POST /portfolio/events/orders` (v2) — collapses yes/no+buy/sell into `side=bid/ask`, `count`/`price` now fixed-point decimal strings (not ints), plus two new required fields (`time_in_force`, `self_trade_prevention_type`). Step (3) empirical verification: real test order submitted against Kalshi DEMO for a live-sourced KXMLBHIT ticker — error type changed from `410 deprecated_v1_order_endpoint` (pre-fix) to `409 market_closed` (post-fix), proving Kalshi now parses and evaluates the v2 request rather than rejecting it at the endpoint level. Full 200/201 confirmation not obtained this session (see [[GG-064]] — separate, newly-discovered, OPEN issue, not a residual GG-063 concern). Touches ADR-0008 (immutable) — superseding addendum drafted this session per operator-selected "migrate now, ADR after" option. Tests: `test_kalshi_client.py`/`test_t164_order_throttle.py` 52/52 passed; full suite 2155 passed, 1 pre-existing unrelated failure, 6 skipped. See DECISION_LOG SESSION 108/GG-063/T-217 entry for full narrative. |

### GG-064 — Kalshi DEMO Market `open_time` Lags LIVE for Same Ticker (Discovered During GG-063 Verification, Unconfirmed as Structural)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-064 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-01 SESSION 108 — while empirically verifying the GG-063/T-217 v1→v2 order-endpoint migration fix via a manual test order against Kalshi DEMO. |
| Category | Unconfirmed — possible DEMO/LIVE environment desync, or simply a testing-window artifact (no live MLB games "today" to test against). Not yet distinguished. |
| What was missed | Comparing `GET /markets/{ticker}` for the same live-sourced KXMLBHIT ticker against both DEMO and LIVE base URLs showed: DEMO `status=initialized`, `open_time=2026-07-02T05:12:39Z` (not yet reached); LIVE `status=active`, `open_time=2026-07-02T04:12:00Z` (already active) — a ~1hr `open_time` discrepancy for the identical ticker, with DEMO consistently lagging LIVE. All 60 currently-open KXMLBHIT markets at test time were for 2026-07-02 games (test was run 2026-07-01, no live in-progress MLB games existed to test against same-day). Every one of 15 test orders submitted against DEMO in this window returned `409 market_closed` — could not be distinguished between (a) a genuine DEMO/LIVE market-activation-timing lag that would also affect real T1/T4 MLB cron runs scheduled close to `open_time`, or (b) an artifact of testing against next-day markets outside any live game window, which is the more likely explanation given no MLB games were in progress at test time. |
| When it should have been caught | N/A — this is a fresh observation from this session's manual verification, not a process failure with a prior missed catch point. Flagging now specifically so it isn't silently absorbed into "GG-063 resolved" when it is a distinct, unconfirmed question. |
| Impact | **UNKNOWN, likely LOW.** Zero capital risk (paper mode). If structural (DEMO genuinely lags LIVE `open_time` by ~1hr for every market), the next T1/T4 MLB cron run scheduled near a game's `open_time` could see spurious `409 market_closed` on DEMO even though the market is live/active in production pricing — this would look identical to GG-063's original symptom (order rejected) but with a different, fully expected root cause once understood. If purely a testing-window artifact, no action is needed. |
| Resolution path | Do not attempt further synthetic testing to resolve this — the correct test is a real T1/T4 MLB cron run against a genuinely in-progress game, where DEMO and LIVE market state can be compared under realistic conditions. Monitor the next scheduled MLB cron run's `kalshi_order_status` distribution: if `409`/`market_closed`-class failures persist against markets confirmed `active` on LIVE, escalate to a structural DEMO/LIVE desync finding; if orders succeed normally, close as a testing-artifact false alarm. |
| Status | **LIKELY RESOLVED 2026-07-03 (SESSION 114) — testing-artifact false alarm, per this gap's own resolution path.** The real-world cron-run observation this gap called for has since occurred: T4 MLB's live orders against genuinely in-progress games show `kalshi_order_status="submitted"` for 23 orders on 2026-07-02 and 10 orders on 2026-07-03, with zero `409`/`market_closed` failures against markets confirmed active. Verified via code read of `submit_order()` in `kalshi_client.py` that `status="submitted"` can only be set on the `ok=True` branch — i.e., only after a genuine successful (200/201-equivalent) HTTP response from Kalshi's v2 endpoint, not a client-side default — so this evidence satisfies the "orders succeed normally → close as testing-artifact false alarm" branch of this gap's own resolution path. **One nuance keeping this at LIKELY RESOLVED rather than fully CLOSED:** all confirming evidence is from T4 (live, in-progress-game orders); T1/PIPE-05 has not itself placed an order since the GG-063/T-217 fix deployed, because it independently HALTed on 2026-07-02 (see [[GG-072]]) and the betting day was unrecoverable. Close fully once T1 places and confirms at least one post-fix order with the same clean `submitted` status. Not conflated with GG-063 (RESOLVED) — that gap's specific claim (v1 endpoint deprecation) remains conclusively fixed regardless of this outcome. |

### GG-065 — `pyproject.toml` Declares `mypy strict = true` But mypy Has Never Been Run Against This Codebase

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-065 |
| Discovered | 2026-07-02 SESSION 108 — while executing the T-213 close-out task "run full test suite + mypy strict" (from the SESSION 107 handoff's open-item list). `mypy` was not installed in `.venv311`; `pyproject.toml` `[tool.mypy]` has declared `strict = true` since at least G4 per `03_DECISIONS/DECISION_LOG/DECISION_LOG.md` references. |
| Category | Architectural placeholder — a governance control (strict static typing) was configured but never activated or enforced. |
| What was missed | mypy was installed (`pip install "mypy>=1.9.0,<2.0"`) and run against `risk.py` and `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` with `--strict`. Result: 3 errors in `risk.py` (missing `pandas-stubs`, missing `py.typed` marker on the in-repo `c6_audit_logger` package, one pre-existing `no-any-return` in `RiskEngine._log`) and 26 errors in `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` (missing `py.typed` markers across effectively every C1–C7 in-repo package it imports, two `requests.get(params=...)` type mismatches, and 8 bare `dict`/`list` generic-type-argument omissions). None of these trace to T-213's new code (`edge_logger` param, `evaluate_forecast()` wrapper, `_emit_edge_decision()`, `summarize_edge_run()`, or the two `RiskEngine(...)` call-site edits at lines 618/962) — confirmed by cross-referencing error line numbers against the T-213 diff. All 29 errors are pre-existing baseline debt that predates this session. |
| When it should have been caught | Any prior session that ran (or claimed to run) "mypy strict" as part of a gate check or session-close protocol. No DECISION_LOG or SESSION_HANDOFF entry found asserting a clean mypy run — the SESSION 107 handoff itself flagged "mypy not installed" as an open item rather than a broken run, suggesting this may be the first time mypy has actually been executed against this codebase in its current form. |
| Phases it survived | G0→G1→G2→G3→G4→G5→G6-pending. `strict = true` has been in `pyproject.toml` throughout. |
| Why it survived | mypy was never installed in the working venv (`.venv311`), so `python -m mypy` silently failed with `No module named mypy` rather than a visible CI/test-suite failure. No test-suite step or session-start protocol step invokes mypy, so a missing/broken type-checker was invisible to every scan in the mandatory session-start protocol (Steps 1-4) and the Q1-Q6 adversarial checklist. |
| Impact | LOW-MEDIUM, no capital risk. The `py.typed`-marker gaps mean mypy cannot currently type-check cross-module calls at all (it degrades to `Any` at every internal package boundary) — so even a "clean" mypy run today would not catch type errors in inter-module contracts (e.g., a C3→C4 forecast-record shape mismatch), only within-file errors. This means the strict-typing safety net implied by `pyproject.toml` is substantially weaker in practice than its configuration suggests. |
| Discovery | Human-directed (session task list explicitly required running mypy strict as part of T-212/T-213 close-out), not caught by any automated protocol step. |
| Resolution path | Not resolved in this session — out of scope for T-213 (would require adding `py.typed` markers to 8+ in-repo packages, installing/vendoring `pandas-stubs`, and fixing 8 dict/list generic-argument omissions plus 2 `requests` param-type mismatches across at least 2 files, likely more once the rest of `src/` is checked). Recommend a dedicated task (T-NNN, not yet registered) scoped to: (1) add `py.typed` to all `src/prediction_market_bot/*/` packages, (2) add `pandas-stubs` to dev dependencies, (3) run `mypy --strict` against the full `src/` tree (not per-file) to get a true baseline error count, (4) fix or explicitly `# type: ignore[code]` each remaining error with justification. Until that task closes, no session should assert "mypy strict passes" without re-verifying — the current true state is "installed and runnable, 29+ known pre-existing errors, cross-module type-safety not actually enforced." |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-06 SESSION 123** (steps 1-3 of the recommended resolution path fully executed against the originally-scoped files; step 4 fully closed for `risk.py` and `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`, and a new gap registered for the rest). Concretely: (1) added `py.typed` markers to all 9 `src/prediction_market_bot/*/` sub-packages plus the top-level `prediction_market_bot/` package, and added `[tool.setuptools.package-data] "*" = ["py.typed"]` to `pyproject.toml` so the marker actually ships if the package is ever built (PEP 561). (2) Added `pandas-stubs>=2.2.0,<3.1` to `pyproject.toml` dev deps and installed it in `.venv311`. Also found and removed a stray, dead `src/__init__.py` (Phase-1 scaffolding, confirmed nothing imports `src.*` — the editable install's `.pth` adds `src/` directly to `sys.path`) that was making mypy see every in-repo module under two names (`src.prediction_market_bot.X` and `prediction_market_bot.X`) and fail with "Source file found twice" the moment `py.typed` markers made mypy actually try to resolve those cross-module imports. (3) Ran `mypy --strict` against the full `src/` tree: **365 errors across 57 files**, all pre-existing baseline debt unrelated to this fix. (4) Fixed all errors in the two files GG-065's own investigation had scoped (`risk.py`, `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`) — both now `mypy --strict` clean: `risk.py`'s `_emit_edge_decision`/`RiskEngine._log` had a `pd.Series.to_dict()` → `dict[Hashable, Any]` vs. `dict[str, Any]` invariance mismatch across an if/else assignment, fixed by explicit `fc: dict[str, Any]` annotation with a `{str(k): v ...}` rebuild on the Series branch (no behavior change, keys were always strings); `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` had 2 `requests.get(params=...)` type mismatches (mixed `int`/`str` dict values — fixed by using `"1"` not `1` for `sportId`, MLB Stats API accepts both since query params are always strings on the wire), 8 bare `dict`/`list` generic-argument omissions (added `[str, Any]` / `[dict[str, Any]]` throughout), 1 `joblib` import with no publishable stubs (`# type: ignore[import-untyped]` with an inline justification comment — confirmed via `pip index versions types-joblib` that no stub package exists), and 1 variable-scope collision in `main()` where a `for _, row in latest.iterrows()` loop bound `row` as non-Optional `pd.Series` for the rest of the function, then a later, logically-unrelated `row = cohort_lookup.get(norm)` (Optional) reassignment in the same scope tripped an incompatible-assignment error — fixed by renaming the second, unrelated variable to `player_row` (a real latent scope-hygiene issue mypy surfaced, not a false positive). Full regression check: `pytest` full suite 2,375 passed / 6 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_feature_csv_freshness`, stale feature CSV — a data-freshness operational matter already tracked separately, e.g. TODO_NEXT.md's GG-077 note; untouched by this fix). The remaining 353 errors in 55 other files (including live ACTIVE code — `t4_mlb_execution_engine.py` — and dark/frozen NBA code — `run_t4_live.py`, `run_paper_trade.py`, `t4_live_runner.py` — plus several `run_exp0*.py` research scripts) were deliberately NOT batch-fixed in this pass: touching `t4_mlb_execution_engine.py`'s live `EvaluationSignal.kelly_fraction` typing deserves its own focused review, not a rushed sweep alongside four other unrelated governance items in one sitting. **See GG-091 (new)** for that full-tree remediation, now scoped with a real, quantified baseline instead of an estimate. GG-065 itself is closed: mypy strict is installed, runnable, produces a real (not silently-broken) signal, and the two files this gap's own investigation flagged are clean. |

### GG-066 — MLB C1 Has No Real-Time/Sub-Poll-Interval Event Feed (Blocks Event-Driven T4 Sub-Bands)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-066 |
| Category | Architectural placeholder / data-availability gap (same class as NBA's GG-040, discovered independently for MLB). |
| What was missed | ADR-0039 (T-218, SESSION 108) scoped an MLB-native T4 sub-band taxonomy and, in doing so, confirmed by direct code read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py` that MLB's only live data source is a 45-second poll of the ESPN MLB summary endpoint (`_espn_poll_once_mlb`, `POLL_INTERVAL_S = 45`) that diffs `h_so_far`/`ab_so_far` between cycles. There is no play-by-play, no live event stream, and no sub-45s detection latency anywhere in current MLB C1. |
| When it should have been caught | This was never claimed to be otherwise — it is a newly surfaced scoping finding, not a regression. Registered proactively as part of ADR-0039's design work rather than being discovered as a live failure. |
| Phases it survived | N/A — this is the first ADR/task to formally scope event-driven MLB T4 sub-bands, so there was no prior point at which this gap could have been flagged as blocking. |
| Why it survived | Not applicable in the "should have been caught" sense; flagged as a direct consequence of ADR-0039's Tier B design work applying GG-061's process fix (verify actual data availability before proposing a sub-band that assumes it). |
| Impact | Blocks T4M-EVENT, T4M-LINEUP, and real-time firing of T4M-ARITH (all three degrade to "same signal as T4M-PROJ, one 45s cycle later" without this). Does not block T4M-BOUNDARY, T4M-ARITH's poll-driven firing, T4M-STAGED, T4M-CORR, or T4M-EXIT, none of which require sub-poll-interval detection. Zero capital risk — no sub-band gated by this has been built or activated. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 108, during ADR-0039 scoping (T-218), by direct read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s ESPN polling implementation. |
| Resolution | Not resolved. Requires evaluating real-time or near-real-time MLB event sources (e.g., MLB Stats API live game feed `v1.1/game/{gamePk}/feed/live`, which offers finer-grained play-by-play than the ESPN summary endpoint currently used) as a new C1 MLB data source, scoped under its own task once T4M-EVENT/T4M-LINEUP/T4M-PITCHER implementation is prioritized. Out of scope for T-218 (scoping-only). |
| Status | OPEN — registered, no task assigned yet. Zero capital risk; blocks three of ten proposed MLB T4 sub-bands from ADR-0039, does not block T-218 itself or G6. |

### GG-067 — EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 Design Docs Cite Data Sources That Don't Exist In Production

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-067 |
| Category | Architectural placeholder / data-availability gap (same class as GG-066, discovered while operationalizing the two experiments GG-066 itself references). |
| What was missed | Both design docs (T-219, SESSION 109) state "No new data sources are required — this experiment is a retrospective slice-and-recompute over data T4M-PROJ already ingests live." Direct code read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle()` (SESSION 110) found this false on two independent counts: (1) the raw per-cycle context each experiment needs (`h_so_far`/`ab_so_far`/`ba_l10`/`inning`/`batting_slot`/`mip`/`vig`/`p_t4`) is computed in memory every cycle but is **only persisted to `_append_dryrun_record()`'s JSONL log when `dry_run=True`** — the normal/production execution path (the one actually running on the VPS) discards it after building the minimal `fc_record` passed to `engine.evaluate_forecast()`. (2) Both docs cite `t4_price_cache.py` (`CachedPrice`) as the source of "the existing T4M-PROJ price cache audit log," but `t4_price_cache.py` is Phase-8-blocked NBA-oriented infrastructure (`T4PriceCache._PHASE8_ACTIVE = False`; `start_polling()`/`stop_polling()` raise `T4Phase8BlockedError`) that MLB T4M-PROJ's live path never touches — live MLB prices flow through a separate `live_prices` dict (populated elsewhere in the poll loop) with its own `mid_price`/`vig`/`has_live_quotes` attributes, not `CachedPrice`. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-219 doc-drafting time (SESSION 109) — the docs asserted data availability without a corresponding code read of the actual normal-path logging behavior or of `t4_price_cache.py`'s Phase 8 gate. |
| Phases it survived | N/A — first attempt to operationalize either experiment (SESSION 110) is what surfaced this; no prior point existed to catch it. |
| Why it survived | T-219's scope was evaluator construction + BLOCKED-status registration, not backtest-runner implementation — the docs' data-source claims were written by inference from what T4M-PROJ conceptually "should" log, not verified against the actual `_run_cycle()` code path or `t4_price_cache.py`'s module docstring/guard clauses. |
| Impact | LOW, zero capital risk — no sub-band gated by this has fired or can fire (both remain `status="BLOCKED"`). Impact is to experiment planning only: neither EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 nor EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 can run against real accumulated data until production-path logging is added and games accumulate post-fix. Without this gap being caught, a future session could have attempted to "run" either experiment against a nonexistent log and produced a false INSUFFICIENT_DATA-vs-fabricated-data ambiguity, or worse, silently proceeded on the incorrect assumption that data already existed. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 110, while scoping the EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 backtest runner build (T-219 follow-up), by direct read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle()`'s normal execution branch and `t4_price_cache.py`'s header/guard clauses. |
| Resolution | Addressed same session: additive per-cycle raw-context JSONL logging added to `_run_cycle()`'s normal (non-dry-run) execution path (observational only, zero live-path logic change); both design docs corrected to remove the false "no new data sources required" claim and the `t4_price_cache.py` citation; backtest runner built to consume the new production log and correctly report insufficient-data until it accumulates. See DECISION_LOG SESSION 110 entry. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 110) — logging gap closed by additive change; doc claims corrected. Real experiment data will not exist until games accumulate post-deploy; this is expected, not a reopening condition. |
