### GG-068 — `mlb_feature_csv` Contract Marked `days_rest`/`train_test_label` Non-Nullable Against Documented, Intentional Null Behavior — HALTed PIPE-06pre During Live Games

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-068 |
| Category | Contract-calibration defect (T-212's `data_contracts.py`, built SESSION 107, never exercised against real production edge cases before today). |
| What was missed | `_MLB_FEATURE_CSV_NULLABLE` in `data_contracts.py` omitted `days_rest` and `train_test_label`, both marked non-nullable by default. But `mlb_features.py:225-228` explicitly sets `days_rest=NaN` for a player's first game in the dataset (no prior game to diff against — documented in the function's own docstring), and `mlb_features.py:315`/`features.py:802` explicitly set `train_test_label=None` for "retained" rows with no `temporal_cutoff` (TI-02, deliberately excluded from train/val/test splits). Both nulls are intentional, documented data-model behavior, not sparsity or corruption — the contract simply never accounted for either. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-212 build time (SESSION 107) — the nullable set was derived by inference from "which columns look like rolling-window features," not by cross-checking every column against `mlb_features.py`'s actual null-producing logic. SESSION 108's regression-fix pass (rebuilding test fixtures to the 44-col schema) also missed it because every fixture row populated all columns — no fixture exercised a first-game-in-dataset or no-cutoff row. |
| Phases it survived | T-212 build (SESSION 107) → SESSION 108 regression-fix pass (17 tests touched, this one untested) → every VPS cron run since T-212 shipped until today, because no `mlb_feature_csv` regeneration happened to include a fresh first-appearance player or a no-cutoff row in a HALT-checked run until 2026-07-02. |
| Why it survived | No dedicated `test_data_contracts.py`/`test_validators.py` exists yet (SESSION 108 explicitly deferred this); all coverage was indirect, via runner-level fixtures that always fully populated every row. Unit tests cannot catch a contract/reality mismatch when the test data never contains the real-world null case the contract is supposed to tolerate. |
| Impact | HIGH for one day, zero capital risk. `run_mlb_pregame.py` (PIPE-06pre) HALTed at 15:20 UTC on 2026-07-02 — `pregame_mlb_20260702.json` was never written. `run_t4_mlb_live.py` fell back to 2026-07-01's stale pregame roster/tickers for the entire day, producing `eval=0 fired=0` on every 45s cycle despite two MLB games live in progress (PIT@PHI, CIN@MIL) at discovery time (18:56 UTC) — meaning the entire day's T4-MLB cycle-log accumulation (the exact data GG-067's fix exists to collect, and the blocker EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 has been waiting on since SESSION 110) was silently lost. No bets were placed on stale data (T1 paper trade also reads the same pregame file, so it was equally dark) — the failure mode is "pipeline goes quiet," not "pipeline acts on wrong data," but the quiet was not caught by the session-start protocol's Step 4 operational health check until this session's SSH investigation. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 111, while checking whether `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` (GG-067's new log) had accumulated data per the SESSION 110 handoff's explicit instruction. Live ESPN scoreboard check showed 2 games in progress; VPS `t4_mlb_live_cron.log` showed batters being found (3 + 1) but `eval=0` every cycle; traced to `pregame_mlb_20260702.json` missing on disk; traced to `pregame_mlb_cron.log`'s `ERROR: DataContractViolation` at the 15:20 UTC run; traced to the two null columns against `mlb_features.py`'s own documented null-producing logic. |
| Resolution | Added `days_rest`, `train_test_label` to `_MLB_FEATURE_CSV_NULLABLE` in `data_contracts.py`, matching `mlb_features.py`'s documented behavior exactly. Full suite re-verified: 2235 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure — no regressions (no existing test asserted non-nullability for either column). Deployed to VPS same session; `run_mlb_pregame.py` re-run manually post-fix to recover same-day pregame context for the remainder of the two in-progress games. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 111) — contract fix deployed and verified same session. |

### GG-069 — `fetch_actual_hits` Resolves Against MLB Stats API `gameLog` With No FINAL-Status Gate — Resolves Partial/In-Progress Hit Totals As Final Outcomes

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-069 |
| Category | Correctness defect in a resolution primitive shared by production paper-trade resolution and both new backtest runners (GG-067's downstream consumers). |
| What was missed | `fetch_actual_hits`/`_fetch_mlb_hits` (`src/prediction_market_bot/run_resolve_mlb.py:77-138`) queries the MLB Stats API `gameLog` endpoint and returns `float(split["stat"]["hits"])` for whatever split matches `game_date` — with no check of `abstractGameState`/`codedGameState`/`status` anywhere in the function. `gameLog` updates live during a game, so this can and does return partial (mid-game) hit totals for a game that has not finished. Discovered when EXP-ARITH-MLB-01's first real run against SNAP-2026-07-02-001 (frozen while PIT@PHI and CIN@MIL were both confirmed still IN PROGRESS via ESPN) unexpectedly resolved 7 bets instead of the expected 0 — those 7 were resolved against live, non-final partial box-score totals. |
| When it should have been caught | At `run_resolve_mlb.py`'s original build (T-187E) or at GG-048's retry-logic fix (SESSION ~96) — either point should have paired "fetch hits" with "confirm game is Final," matching the pattern already used elsewhere in the codebase (`run_t4_mlb_live.py:165`, `scripts/reconstruct_mlb_score_diff.py:58`, `scripts/poll_and_resolve_g1.py:41` all gate on ESPN `status.type.name == STATUS_FINAL` before trusting game data). |
| Phases it survived | T-187E build → GG-048 retry-logic fix → every production `run_resolve_mlb.py` cron execution since → this session's first real backtest-runner invocation, which is what surfaced it. |
| Why it survived | Production's `_resolve_pool()` (`run_resolve_mlb.py:145-167`) filters eligible positions to `game_date < today` (a calendar-date check, line 167) before ever calling `fetch_actual_hits`. In the normal daily-cron cadence this incidentally shields production: by the time "yesterday's" game_date positions are resolved, the source game is almost always actually over. That incidental protection masked the absence of a true completion-status gate inside `fetch_actual_hits` itself — the function was never actually correct, production just rarely exercised the failure path. The new backtest runners (`run_exp_arith_mlb_01.py`, `run_exp_flow_mlb_01.py`) call `fetch_actual_hits` directly against a frozen snapshot with no calendar-date filter at all, immediately exposing the underlying defect on same-day games. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. Zero capital risk today (no live capital; T4M-ARITH/T4M-FLOW both BLOCKED; the 7 corrupted resolutions were in a research backtest, not a real bet). But this is a live correctness bug in the same resolution primitive production paper-trading depends on (`run_resolve_mlb.py`) — the calendar-date incidental guard is not equivalent to a real completion check (edge cases: suspended/postponed games, doubleheader Game 2s sharing a `game_date`, games starting late and finishing after midnight UTC relative to the cron's "today"). It also invalidates the EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 real-run result recorded this session: the 7 resolved bets / 0.143 win rate are resolved against confirmed-incomplete data and must not be read as even a preliminary signal — the run should be characterized as "insufficient data, AND the small number of records that did resolve are known-invalid," not simply "insufficient data." |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 111 CONTINUATION, via a background Explore agent tasked to explain why EXP-ARITH-MLB-01's real run against SNAP-2026-07-02-001 (both source games confirmed in-progress at freeze time) produced 7 resolved bets instead of the expected 0. Confirmed by direct code read of `run_resolve_mlb.py:77-138` and cross-reference against the FINAL-status-gating pattern used in three other modules. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 112 / T-221).** Added `_is_game_final(game_pk)` to `run_resolve_mlb.py` — queries the lightweight `GET /api/v1/schedule?gamePk={pk}` endpoint (MLB Stats API, same host as `gameLog`; verified live against gamePk 822791 → `status.abstractGameState: "Final"`) and checks `abstractGameState == "Final"`. `_fetch_mlb_hits` now extracts `split["game"]["gamePk"]` from the matched `gameLog` split and calls `_is_game_final` before returning a hits value; if the game is not confirmed FINAL (or `gamePk` is missing, or the status check itself fails) it returns `None`, which flows into the existing "no result yet" path (`_resolve_pool` retries next cron run, ages out after 48h — no new code needed there). Fixes the shared primitive, so both `run_resolve_mlb._resolve_pool()` (production) and the two backtest runners (`run_exp_arith_mlb_01.py`, `run_exp_flow_mlb_01.py`, both import `fetch_actual_hits` directly) get the fix simultaneously. 9 new/updated tests added to `tests/test_t187e_mlb_resolve.py` (`TestGameFinalGate` + `_is_game_final` patches added to existing `TestFetchMlbHits` happy-path tests). Full suite: 2282 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (local feature-CSV staleness, not code). This session's SESSION 111 EXP-ARITH-MLB-01 real-run result (7 resolved / 0.143 win rate) remains invalidated as stated below — the fix prevents recurrence, it does not retroactively repair that prior run's output. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 112 / T-221) — FINAL-status gate deployed and test-verified same session. Superseded caution: any EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 run using this fixed code path may now be trusted to skip non-final games; the SESSION 111 pre-fix run (7 resolved bets) is still invalid and must not be cited as signal. |

### GG-070 — Session-Start Governance Hook Displayed NBA Ledger Unlabeled as Generic "Portfolio," Indistinguishable From the Active MLB Book

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-070 |
| Category | Tooling/session-start protocol defect — the hook itself, not the trading system. |
| What was missed | `.claude/hooks/governance_scan.sh`'s portfolio section read only `paper_portfolio.json` (NBA) and printed it as unlabeled generic `PORTFOLIO (VPS LIVE BOOK)`. NBA and MLB paper trading use fully separate, isolated ledger files (`paper_portfolio.json` vs `paper_portfolio_mlb.json`, confirmed via `PORTFOLIO_PATH` constants in `run_paper_trade.py`/`run_mlb_paper_trade.py`) with independent `initial_bankroll_usd`/`total_realized_pnl`. The hook showed only the NBA figure (frozen, dark, -24.9% drawdown — the already-adjudicated GG-034/GG-037 baseline) with no indication it excluded the live, active MLB pool (currently +$100.00 P&L, 0.0% drawdown, 49/30 resolved). |
| When it should have been caught | When the hook was written — should have accounted for multi-sport ledgers from the start, especially once MLB became the sole active priority (2026-07-01 operator directive). |
| Phases it survived | Every session-start scan since the hook's creation through SESSION 113. |
| Why it survived | No test/assertion on hook output; the hook is bash+inline-python with no review gate of its own. NBA's number happened to look plausible (a real, valid, if stale, historical figure) rather than obviously broken, so nothing forced a recheck until it was read cold in a fresh session. |
| Impact | MEDIUM — no capital risk (both figures were individually correct, just unlabeled/incomplete), but real time/token cost: SESSION 113 spent a full investigation pass (~20k tokens) re-deriving from source that the 24.9% figure was NBA-only and not a live kill-switch breach, before reaching the same conclusion this gap entry now documents permanently. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 113 — user flagged that the hook's drawdown figure has caused this exact re-investigation before and must be delineated permanently. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 113).** Hook rewritten to fetch and print both `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` and `paper_portfolio.json` from the VPS as two explicitly labeled blocks — `MLB (ACTIVE)` and `NBA (DARK/FROZEN — historical, not live risk)` — with MLB printed first. Verified locally: output now shows both books unambiguously. Memory saved (`feedback_portfolio_ledger_delineation.md`) so future sessions don't re-derive this. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-02 (SESSION 113). |

### GG-071 — Forward-Compounding Monte Carlo Shows Proportional-Stake Sizing Produces Materially Elevated Permanent-Kill-Switch Breach Probability at High Bet Cadence

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-071 |
| Category | Systemic risk-model finding — a property of the current proportional-stake sizing mechanic under compounding, not a code defect. |
| What was missed | No prior session had run a genuine forward-time (calendar-day, compounding) Monte Carlo of either MLB band. All earlier MC work resolved only the currently-open position book plus real closed P&L — a terminal-outcome view, not a forward trajectory view. That masked a property of proportional/Kelly-style stake sizing at T1's real cadence (~26 bets/active-day): compounding a genuinely real, positive edge does not suppress ruin risk — it amplifies both tails simultaneously. |
| When it should have been caught | Not clearly assignable to a single missed gate — this is a new class of analysis (forward simulation vs. terminal resolution) that the project had not yet built. Flagged here specifically so it is not lost as a one-off artifact finding. |
| Phases it survived | N/A — first time this analysis has been run. |
| Why it survived | All prior Monte Carlo work in this project answered "given the open book today, what's the P&L distribution," not "if this edge and cadence persist, what does the bankroll path look like a year out." The forward-simulation script (`mc_forward_projection.py`, this session) is the first to ask the second question. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH, forward-looking only (no live capital deployed; G6 not cleared). Real finding: T1's calibrated edge (53.06% win rate, +115.8%/−100% avg win/loss multiple, 1.153% of bankroll staked per bet, 26 bets/active-day) produces a permanent 20%-drawdown kill-switch breach probability of 17.05% by day 30, 43.5% by day 182, 46.45% by day 365 — even though the edge itself is real and positive. This means the current stake-sizing mechanic (proportional/Kelly-style, uncapped fraction growth until the $500/bet hard cap engages at ~$43,373 bankroll) has a ruin-risk profile that has never been formally reviewed or bounded independent of "is the edge real." Before this system scales bet cadence or bankroll further, the stake-sizing/Kelly-fraction policy needs an explicit ruin-risk review — e.g., fractional-Kelly de-rating at high cadence, a lower ceiling before the $500 hard cap engages, or a formal maximum-acceptable P(kill-switch hit) target used to size DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION going forward. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 SESSION 113 (continuation) — user requested a genuine forward-projection Monte Carlo (1mo/6mo/1yr, spaghetti-chart format) in `mlb_mc_report.html`. Building it surfaced the kill-switch-probability finding; user explicitly flagged it for future architectural attention ("the compounding effect needs to be addressed in the future. Flag for future look."). |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-04 (SESSION 118 continuation).** T-223 review completed 2026-07-03 with `ADR-0040` drafted PROPOSED, recommending `risk.py`'s `DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION` 0.25 → 0.14. Before implementing, 2026-07-04 code tracing (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py`, `execution_bands.py`) found that recommendation targeted the wrong constant: `DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION` is a `RiskEngine.__init__` default only, never consumed by the live T1 MLB path, which always explicitly passes `kelly_fraction=T1_KELLY` sourced from `execution_bands.py`'s `T1_MULTI_DAY.kelly_fraction = 0.10` (ADR-0010). Had the original 0.14 figure been mechanically applied to the real 0.10 baseline, it would have **increased** live stake sizing 40%, worsening the ruin-risk this ADR exists to fix. Re-ran `scripts/mc_forward_projection.py --sensitivity` (source-agnostic math, unchanged) and re-derived the correct mapping onto the real 0.10 baseline; presented three corrected candidate targets to the operator (0.050/2.0x, 0.061/≤5% 1yr target, 0.067/1.5x). **Operator selected 0.061** (≤5% 1yr kill-switch target, ~5.6% actual). `ADR-0040` corrected in place (still PROPOSED at time of correction, so directly editable) and then **ACCEPTED** by the operator; renamed to drop the stale `-proposed` filename suffix. Implemented: `execution_bands.py`'s `T1_MULTI_DAY.kelly_fraction` 0.10 → 0.061 (superseding ADR-0010's value for this field only). Corrected three stale cross-references that assumed `DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION` was the operative T1 constant: `t4_execution_engine.py:961`, `tests/test_execution_bands.py:181` (both NBA T4-PROJ-scoped, unrelated to T1 MLB), and `scripts/run_monte_carlo.py:43` (was already stale against ADR-0010 too — cited 0.25 when T1 had been 0.10 since 2026-06-02). Updated `tests/test_execution_bands.py::test_t1_kelly_fraction` (was asserting the pre-ADR-0040 literal 0.10). Full test suite re-run: 2334 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature-CSV staleness, tracked separately) — no regressions from this change. `risk.py`'s `DEFAULT_KELLY_FRACTION` is left unchanged at 0.25 (confirmed inert for T1 MLB; not part of this remediation). |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-04 — ADR-0040 ACCEPTED, `execution_bands.py` T1_MULTI_DAY.kelly_fraction live at 0.061, T-223 closed. |

### GG-072 — T1 MLB (PIPE-05) HALTed by the Same GG-068 Bug On the Same Day, But Was Never Cross-Checked or Recovered — 2026-07-02 Betting Day Permanently Lost

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-072 |
| Category | Incident-response scope gap — a fix was correctly applied but not propagated to every consumer of the thing it fixed. |
| What was missed | `MLB_FEATURE_CSV_CONTRACT` (`data_contracts.py`) is imported and enforced identically by two independent pipelines: `run_mlb_pregame.py` (PIPE-06pre) and `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` (PIPE-05/T1). GG-068's `days_rest`/`train_test_label` nullability bug HALTed **both** pipelines on 2026-07-02, since both validate the same feature CSV against the same contract. SESSION 111's GG-068 response discovered and fixed the bug via PIPE-06pre's failure (it HALTed first, at 15:20 UTC, and was the one actively being investigated for the `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` accumulation check), manually re-ran PIPE-06pre post-fix to recover same-day pregame context, and closed GG-068 as RESOLVED — without checking whether PIPE-05, gated by the identical contract, had also HALTed earlier that day (it had, at 11:00:11 UTC, 4+ hours before the fix). PIPE-05 was never re-run. |
| When it should have been caught | At GG-068's resolution step (SESSION 111) — "this contract HALTed one consumer, does it gate any other pipeline?" is a direct corollary of the fix and was not asked. `grep -rn MLB_FEATURE_CSV_CONTRACT src/` at fix time would have surfaced both call sites in under a minute. |
| Phases it survived | GG-068 fix and close (SESSION 111, same day) → GG-068's own RESOLVED status write-up (which describes only PIPE-06pre's recovery) → this session's mandatory session-start protocol Step 4 operational health check, which caught the *symptom* (T1 MLB produced zero bets 2026-07-02) but required a full SSH/audit-log investigation to trace it back to a governance gap GG-068 itself should have closed. |
| Why it survived | GG-068's resolution narrative was written from the vantage point of the pipeline being actively debugged (PIPE-06pre, because its downstream consumer T4 was visibly firing `eval=0`). PIPE-05/T1 has no live-consumer symptom as immediate as T4's `eval=0` — its failure mode is simply "zero bets today," which does not distinguish a correctly-functioning HALT from a silent hang without a follow-up audit-log check. No process step exists that says "when a shared contract/module is patched, enumerate and re-verify every pipeline gated by it, not just the one whose failure prompted the fix." |
| Impact | **MEDIUM, evidentiary — one full betting day permanently and irrecoverably lost.** T1 MLB placed zero bets on 2026-07-02 despite the HALT being fixable same-day; VPS wall-clock confirmed (`date -u` → 2026-07-03T04:24:59Z) that 2026-07-02's MLB game slate has fully resolved with no manual PIPE-05 backfill ever executed — Kalshi markets for that date are closed, so no retroactive paper bet can be placed against real market prices for that day. This subtracts one day's worth of resolved-bet evidence from the G6 30-bet accumulation target (T1's positive-P&L gate) at zero notice, and was invisible to every governance check until this session's Step 4 deep-dive. Zero capital risk (paper mode). |
| Discovery | 2026-07-03 SESSION 114, mandatory session-start Step 4 operational health check — `paper_mlb_20260702.json` report file was never generated (only `paper_mlb_20260701.json` exists); traced via `audit_mlb.jsonl` to a `data_contract_violation` HALT at 11:00:11.379Z for session `PT-MLB-F5E5491F`, on the identical `days_rest`/`train_test_label` failure signature as GG-068. |
| Resolution | **Not recoverable for 2026-07-02** — no backfill attempted; the betting day is permanently lost and should be recorded as such in the G6 evidence trail (a known, explained gap in the 30-bet accumulation sequence, not silent missing data). **Process fix registered as T-224:** when any data contract or shared validation module is patched in response to a HALT, the incident-response step must include `grep -rn <ContractName> src/` (or equivalent) to enumerate every pipeline gated by that contract, and each one must be checked against that day's run log before the incident is marked RESOLVED — not just the pipeline whose failure prompted the investigation. |
| Status | OPEN — T-224 (process-fix task) assigned 2026-07-03 SESSION 114. 2026-07-02 T1 MLB betting day recorded as a permanent, explained data gap — no further recovery action possible. |

### GG-073 — GHA `deploy.yml` Auto-Deploy Has Failed 100% of Its Runs Since Inception (~30 Consecutive Pushes); Production Has Stayed In Sync Only Via Manual SSH Deploys

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-073 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — an automated control (T-171's CI/CD deliverable) has never worked, and its total failure has been invisible because a manual compensating control (operator-directed ad hoc `ssh ... git pull` after every push) has silently done its job instead. |
| What was missed | `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` SSHes into the VPS on every push to `main`, aborts with `exit 1` if `git status --porcelain` on the VPS is non-empty (the T-171 guard against clobbering cron-written tracked files), then runs `git pull`. `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml` shows **every run back to at least 2026-07-01T01:27:35Z (30/30 checked) has `completed / failure`**, each failing in 8-25 seconds — consistent with the dirty-tree guard firing immediately, before `git pull` is ever attempted. Root cause, per `git status --porcelain` on the VPS: two stray untracked files present since **2026-06-03/04** (`scripts/live_terminal.py.pre-s28-backup`, `src/prediction_market_bot/c3_forecasting/team_stats_store.py` — a dead duplicate of the real, tracked `c1_signal_ingestion/team_stats_store.py`; nothing imports the `c3_forecasting` copy), an untracked `exp_output/MLB/` directory present since **2026-06-24**, and (as of 2026-07-02) the newly-tracked-but-cron-mutated `data/data_contracts_state.json` (see resolution below). Any one of these alone is sufficient to fail every run; together they mean **deploy.yml has never once successfully completed an automated deploy in its entire ~3-week existence** (workflow written 2026-06-12, SESSION 59, T-171). |
| When it should have been caught | At any point after 2026-06-12 — no session ever checked `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml` as part of session-start or session-end protocol. The workflow's own GitHub Actions status has shown a red X on every run for three weeks; nothing in this project's governance checks reads CI status. |
| Phases it survived | Workflow creation (SESSION 59, 2026-06-12) → every subsequent push (~30+, spanning SESSIONS 59 through 113) → this session's Worry #4 investigation (originally scoped only to a cardinality-drift baseline question), which is what surfaced it. |
| Why it survived | Production never visibly drifted, because this project's standing operational practice (recorded operator/session convention, not itself in any protocol doc) is: after every `git push`, manually SSH to the VPS and `git pull` regardless of GHA status. That manual step has 100% compensated for a 100%-broken automated pipeline, for three weeks, with zero automated detection of the underlying failure. Confirmed today: local HEAD and VPS HEAD are identical (`5747d70`, 0 commits behind) purely because of this manual habit — if it were ever skipped (different operator, different session style, a session that ends before the manual deploy step, illness, vacation), the VPS would silently stop receiving deploys with no alert, and the false confidence created by "deploy.yml exists, so deploys are automated" would mask the drift. This is the same failure shape as GG-060 (Silent Success Indistinguishability): a green-adjacent state (code exists, workflow runs) coexists with 100% functional failure. |
| Impact | **MEDIUM, latent.** Zero current drift (manual compensation has been perfect to date), but the automated deploy pipeline that G6/production-readiness evidence implicitly relies on is pure liability in its current state — it provides zero actual redundancy and its existence could cause a future session to skip the manual step, trusting the automation. Also directly caused today's narrower Worry #4 finding: `data/data_contracts_state.json`, once it started being written by every PIPE-05/PIPE-06pre run (T-212, 2026-07-01), became a 4th independent contributor to the same dirty-tree failure — reproducing, for a new file, the exact `paper_portfolio.json` problem T-171 was built to solve in the first place, because T-171's fix (untrack + .gitignore) was applied to one specific file rather than established as a standing policy for all runtime-state files. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-03 SESSION 114, while investigating why the cardinality-drift baseline (`data/data_contracts_state.json`) showed `last_known_good_count=1` in a WARN payload (Worry #4 of the operator's requested 4-worry review) — checking whether this file's git-tracked status could affect VPS deploys led to `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml`, which surfaced the 100% failure history. |
| Resolution | Executed this session: (1) `data/data_contracts_state.json` added to `.gitignore` and `git rm --cached` (mirrors the T-171 pattern for `paper_portfolio.json` — this file is pipeline-generated runtime state and should never have been tracked); (2) the two stray dead files (`scripts/live_terminal.py.pre-s28-backup`, `src/prediction_market_bot/c3_forecasting/team_stats_store.py`) deleted from the VPS (confirmed unreferenced by any import); (3) `exp_output/` added to `.gitignore`. Recommend as follow-up (not executed this session, registered as **T-224b**, sub-item of T-224): add a session-start protocol check of `gh run list --workflow=deploy.yml --limit 1` so a reverted-to-broken deploy pipeline is caught automatically rather than depending indefinitely on manual habit. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-03 (SESSION 114).** All four dirty-tree contributors cleared; VPS pulled to `d629275`, two stray files deleted, `git status --porcelain` confirmed empty on VPS. Closure evidence, strongest first: two subsequent real `push`-triggered runs (`28639306402` for the GG-073 closure commit itself, `28639421734` for the documentation-remediation commit landed the same session) both completed `success` automatically in ~10-11s with no manual intervention — durable proof this isn't a one-off, since the earlier manually-triggered `workflow_dispatch` run (`28639247078`, success, 13s) only proved the guard *could* pass, not that real pushes would keep passing. All three follow ~31 consecutive `failure` runs going back to the workflow's creation on 2026-06-12. T-224b (session-start CI-status check, to catch a future regression to this same failure mode automatically rather than depending on manual habit) remains OPEN as a follow-up. |

### GG-074 — Grounding/Gate Documents (01_SPEC, 02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN, 07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE, 08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE) Drift From System Reality With No Session Trigger to Catch Scope-Level Change

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-074 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — process gap, not a code defect. |
| What was missed | The session-start protocol's Step 1 (`decisions_logged: false` scan) and Hard Rule 7 (log decisions immediately) keep the *rolling* logs (MEMORY_BANK.md Current State Snapshot, TODO_NEXT.md, GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md, DECISION_LOG.md, SESSION_HANDOFF files) current every session — that discipline is working well and was not the failure. But nothing checks whether the *structured, point-in-time* grounding documents (01_SPEC/, 02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/, 07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/, 08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE/) still describe the system's actual current shape. Concretely found stale/false as of 2026-07-02: CLAUDE.md stated "T4 (MLB): Does not exist" (T4M-PROJ has been ACTIVE under ADR-0023 for weeks); ROADMAP_MILESTONES.md showed Phases 5/6/7 as "Not Started" despite G5 CLOSED and Phase 6/7 running live for 5+ weeks; RISK_APPETITE_CONSTRAINTS.md's Hard Limits table said "TBD at G4 — Placeholder" for limits its own footer said were confirmed a month earlier; EXECUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md said C4/C5 were "gated and not yet implemented" while both had been running continuously in production; DATA_FLOWS_LINEAGE.md said `QualitativeSignalStore` was "a stub" and F-100+ features "PENDING" when both had been live and confirmed-positive-signal since EXP-007; MODEL_CARD.md/PERFORMANCE_METRICS.md described the superseded v1 model (`v1-9939a6a5`) with no mention that production had moved to v2 (`v2-065b9510`, ADR-0031, SESSION 73); MEMORY_BANK.md's own "Compass OS Health" summary table (a structured section within an otherwise-current file) said "23 accepted ADRs"/"852/852 tests" against an actual 36/38 accepted and 2,282 tests — stale even within a document whose narrative body was current. None of these were caught by the existing session-start protocol because none of them are `decisions_logged: false` flags or code stubs — they are narrative claims in prose/tables that silently stopped matching reality. |
| When it should have been caught | Each document's own stated "Staleness triggers" (e.g., ROADMAP_MILESTONES.md: "Phase gate passed"; RISK_APPETITE_CONSTRAINTS.md: "live capital authorization"; EXECUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md: "paper→live promotion") did fire multiple times (G4 close, G5 close, ADR-0004 acceptance, MLB Phase 3 gate open, v2 model retrain) without triggering a review of the document that named the trigger. |
| Phases it survived | Roughly G4 close (2026-05-28) through this discovery (2026-07-02) — over five weeks and ~100 sessions of governance activity in the rolling logs, zero corresponding updates to the structured grounding docs. |
| Why it survived | The rolling logs are genuinely excellent and are what every session actually reads for "what do I need to know right now" — MEMORY_BANK.md's Current State Snapshot answers that question well, which likely reduced the felt need to also update the slower-moving structured docs. But the structured docs are what a person (or a future session) reads when they want the *designed* answer to "what does this system do and why," not just "what happened recently" — and those had quietly diverged from truth. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. No capital risk (no live capital exists), but real risk of a future session or a non-engineer reader (per CLAUDE.md's own opening line: "This system will be used by people who trust the person who built it. They are not engineers.") drawing false conclusions from a document that reads as authoritative and dated but is stale — e.g., concluding MLB T4 needs to be built from scratch, or that hard risk limits are still unconfirmed placeholders. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02 — operator requested a full project sit-rep, explicitly flagging suspicion that grounding docs (CLAUDE.md, SPEC folder, test validation folder, "and many more") had not been updated despite substantial component growth. Investigation confirmed this via direct file-timestamp and content cross-checks against MEMORY_BANK.md's narrative and live source code. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-06 SESSION 123.** A background audit agent completed the remaining 19-file scope this pass left open: all 7 of 02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/'s un-audited files, all 8 of 08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE/'s, plus SCOPE_IN_OUT.md and REQUIREMENTS.md/VISION_OUTCOMES.md/NON_FUNCTIONAL_REQUIREMENTS.md in 01_SPEC/. Per-file verdicts: 15 of 19 FIXED (real drift corrected), 2 FIXED-MATERIAL (drift concealed a real operational/scope gap, not just stale prose), 2 confirmed CURRENT (VISION_OUTCOMES.md, NON_FUNCTIONAL_REQUIREMENTS.md — abstract, no drift). Notable corrections: SCOPE_IN_OUT.md's "Phase 0-4"/pending-ADR-0004 framing (system is Phase 6/7 ACTIVE); ARB_FRAMEWORK.md's flat `0.030` Kalshi fee constant replaced with ADR-0016's real `0.07 × mid × (1−mid)` formula (inert today — arb/ is reference-only per Hard Rule 1 — but would ship wrong if ever built from this doc); DEPENDENCIES.md/INTERFACES_CONTRACTS.md/SYSTEM_CONTEXT.md's "TBD"/"gated"/"Phase 8 only" framing corrected against live ADR-0008 order execution and Kalshi-only routing; METHODOLOGY.md's FEATURE_VERSION (1.1.0→1.4.0) and hard-limits table reconciled against risk.py; G6_GATE_CHECKLIST.md's T0-A EXP-017 status updated (n=33/96→n=151/DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE) with the Tier1/Tier2-vs-formal-bar terminology collision flagged in place (T0-A correctly stays OPEN/BLOCKING). Two FIXED-MATERIAL findings surfaced real, not merely documentary, gaps and were independently spot-verified before registering as new gaps below (both confirmed by direct inspection, not taken on the agent's word alone): **RESILIENCE_FAILURE_MODES.md** references a `RUNBOOKS/` directory for FM-01/FM-02(CRITICAL)/FM-05/FM-07 — registered as **GG-092** *(corrected 2026-07-07, T-244 triage: the directory checked here, `02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/RUNBOOKS/`, was the wrong path — the real directory lives at `10_INFRA_OPERATIONS/RUNBOOKS/`, does exist, and already holds 3 of 7 named runbooks; only FM-01/FM-02(CRITICAL)/FM-05/FM-07 are genuinely missing, not the whole folder — see GG-092's own entry for the full correction)*. **QUALITATIVE_SIGNALS.md**/MLB_BUILDOUT_SCAFFOLD_PLAN.md's claim that MLB has zero qualitative/PRF-analogue feature layer was confirmed by direct grep (`grep -c "qualitative\|PRF\|prf_w0" src/prediction_market_bot/c2_feature_store/mlb_features.py` → 0) despite MLB being the sole ACTIVE pool and CLAUDE.md's own T1 alpha description naming "PRF qualitative factors" as a model input — registered as **GG-094**. Two further findings were independently verified during this closeout and also registered fresh: **THREAT_MODEL.md** never re-expanded post-G5 despite live Kalshi RSA credentials since ADR-0008 (**GG-093**), and a real, direct-inspection-confirmed numeric/structural mismatch between DECISION_LOG T-094 (2026-06-02, absolute 4-level Brier ladder: L1=0.255/L2=0.265/L3=0.275/L4=0.280 kill-switch, baseline~0.245) and `monitor.py`'s actual shipped scheme (`BRIER_BASELINE=0.2416` + relative deltas 0.02/0.05/0.10, only 3 levels, kill-switch at L3 not L4) — registered as **GG-095**. ST-006 (Kelly-fraction drawdown stress test) having been run via GG-071/ADR-0040's `mc_forward_projection.py` but never logged back into SENSITIVITY_STRESS_TESTS.md's canonical checklist is registered as **GG-096** (documentation-completeness only — the test itself is real and already governs `execution_bands.py`'s de-rated kelly_fraction via ADR-0040). The terminology collision between GG-004's informal "Tier 1/Tier 2" shorthand and G6_GATE_CHECKLIST.md's formal Tier 0-3 framework, and the ARB_FRAMEWORK.md fee-constant fix, were both resolved inline in the same files during this pass and do not warrant separate gap numbers. **Process fix for recurrence (the piece this gap could not close in the 2026-07-02 partial pass):** a recurring grounding-docs review is now a standing obligation, not just a recommendation — add to `04_EXECUTION/TODO_NEXT.md` a monthly trigger point (tied to Compass OS Health M1 reporting, the same cadence MEMORY_BANK.md already uses for its own health table) that re-runs this file-by-file staleness audit across 01_SPEC/, 02_ARCHITECTURE_DESIGN/, 07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/, 08_MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE/ — the same failure mode (rolling logs current, structured grounding docs silently stale) will recur without a standing trigger independent of any one gate closing. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-06 — all 19 remaining files audited (15 FIXED, 2 FIXED-MATERIAL → GG-092/GG-094, 2 CURRENT); GG-093/GG-095/GG-096 registered from findings surfaced/verified during closeout; monthly grounding-docs review added as the standing process fix (see TODO_NEXT.md). |

### GG-075 — Formal Acceptance-Criteria Evidence Trail (`07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/EVIDENCE/`, `TEST_CASES/`, `UAT_SIGNOFFS/`) Specified by ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md and VALIDATION_PLAN.md Was Never Populated

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-075 |
| Category | Incremental architecture — a designed evidence trail that was never actually built out, discovered while correcting an unrelated staleness issue. |
| What was missed | `01_SPEC/ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md` and `07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/VALIDATION_PLAN.md` both specify, per-requirement, a formal evidence folder (e.g. `EVIDENCE/FR-05/` for the kill switch drill). Direct filesystem check confirms `07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/EVIDENCE/`, `TEST_CASES/`, and `UAT_SIGNOFFS/` are all empty directories — zero files — despite G4 and G5 both formally closing via other evidence artifacts (VALIDATION_REPORTS/G4_VALIDATION_REPORT.md, PERFORMANCE_METRICS.md, the kill-switch drill record KS-C8D7D90AF942 recorded only in MEMORY_BANK/DECISION_LOG). The underlying capabilities all demonstrably work — this is a paperwork/traceability gap, not a functional one. |
| When it should have been caught | G4 and G5 gate closure (2026-05-28) — both ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md and VALIDATION_PLAN.md name their own gate as the point their Status columns should be populated ("metric threshold finalized at G4, threshold values populated, not criteria unfrozen"). Neither gate closure triggered it. |
| Phases it survived | G4, G5, and the entirety of Phase 6/7 to date. |
| Why it survived | Gate closure was evidenced through other artifacts (validation reports, MEMORY_BANK narrative, DECISION_LOG entries) that satisfied the *spirit* of each gate's evidence requirement without ever populating the specific folder structure these two documents designed for that purpose. The gates closed correctly on the evidence that does exist; the originally-designed folder structure was simply never used. |
| Impact | LOW-MEDIUM. No capital or correctness risk — the capabilities work and are tested (2,282 tests). Risk is purely evidentiary/traceability: if this project is ever audited against its own ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md design, the literal evidence pointers fail even though the underlying claims are true. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02, during the same documentation-remediation pass that produced GG-074 — found while correcting ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md's blanket "PENDING" status column. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-06.** Path (a) chosen for `EVIDENCE/`: all 17 `EVIDENCE/<REQ-ID>/` folders (FR-01 through FR-09, NFR-01 through NFR-08) populated with a fresh `EVIDENCE.md` each, grounding every claim in real, currently-existing artifacts — actual test file names (cross-checked against `tests/`, not invented), live VPS artifacts (`logs/audit_mlb_t4.jsonl`), and two scans run fresh this session rather than merely re-asserted: (1) `bandit`/`detect-secrets` for NFR-05 (0 real hardcoded-credential findings; 1 confirmed false positive — `DIR_PASS="PASS"` bet-direction enum flagged by bandit's `B105` heuristic; 0 files with detected secrets) and (2) a full `pytest -q` run (2,375 passed / 6 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated data-freshness failure) underpinning NFR-01/NFR-04/NFR-07. FR-03's evidence and the corresponding ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md/VALIDATION_PLAN.md rows also carry forward EXP-017's 2026-07-06 result (n=151, DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE) and a terminology caveat surfaced by the concurrent GG-074 audit: CLAUDE.md/GG-004's "Tier 1/Tier 2" language is informal shorthand, not a threshold formally defined in ADR-0024's text — ADR-0024's actual sample-adequacy bar ("≥500 unique markets, OR ≥2 sports each n≥150") is not yet met (NBA n=0), so `G6_GATE_CHECKLIST.md`'s T0-A correctly stays OPEN/BLOCKING regardless of "Tier 1" phrasing. Path (b) was applied selectively to `TEST_CASES/`/`UAT_SIGNOFFS/`: left deliberately unpopulated rather than building a duplicate parallel per-TC-NNN case-file structure, since `TEST_STRATEGY.md`'s existing TC-001–TC-010 table already names the components under test and every `EVIDENCE.md` cites the real passing pytest file backing its claim directly; UAT sign-off isn't yet applicable since FR-08/live capital (G6) hasn't been reached. `ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA.md` and `VALIDATION_PLAN.md` header/table text updated in place to point at the now-populated folders and to remove the "(empty)" annotations. |

### GG-076 — T4 MLB One-Shot-Per-Ticker Dedup Structurally Conflicts With CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9's Documented Design Throughput

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-076 |
| Category | Architecture-vs-documentation conflict — a real, load-bearing design contradiction between the governing spec and the shipped implementation, not a bug in either taken alone. |
| What was missed | CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9 states T4 design throughput as "~1,000 evaluations/game... at a 2-3% fire rate, that produces 50-100 actual bets per game," derived from "18 batters x 2 thresholds x ~480 cycles per 3-hour game." This arithmetic implicitly assumes each 45s evaluation cycle is an independent shot at firing. The actual implementation (`already_fired_game` set in `run_t4_mlb_live.py::_run_cycle`) retires a ticker the instant it fires a position — one bet per market per game-day, permanently, for the rest of the game. The two cannot both be true: under a one-shot-per-ticker policy, the true ceiling on fired bets is bounded by (count of distinct tracked tickers that ever cross the edge threshold), not by (evaluation count x fire rate). Direct analysis of real production data (`exp_output/MLB/snapshots/t4_mlb_cycle_log_SNAP-2026-07-02-002.jsonl`, 2,393 cycle-evaluations, 2026-07-02) confirms this: only 63 tickers were tracked all day (3 games), 28 of them (44%) ever crossed net_edge>0 at any point, which is already close to the observed ceiling of 10-20 fired bets/day after C4's MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.04 gate and Kelly/budget filtering are applied on top. The system is not "too fresh" or malfunctioning — it is structurally incapable of reaching its own documented design target as currently built. |
| When it should have been caught | Should have surfaced when ADR-0039 (T4 MLB sub-band architecture, SESSION 109) or the original T4M-PROJ build (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`, ADR-0023) were designed — both post-date CLAUDE.md's Hard Rule 9 throughput language, and neither was checked against it for consistency at build time. |
| Phases it survived | Entire T4M-PROJ operational period (ADR-0023 to present) plus ADR-0039's Tier A build (T4M-ARITH/T4M-FLOW) — none of which revisited the dedup policy's interaction with documented throughput. |
| Why it survived | T4M-PROJ has been treated as "working as designed" because it correctly rejects contamination, correctly computes Bayesian edge, and correctly fires when net_edge clears the C4 gate — every individual mechanism is sound. Nobody had directly quantified fired-bet ceiling against Hard Rule 9's stated 50-100/game target until Earl noticed real daily volume (10-20/day, not per game) was an order of magnitude below the documented design and asked for a root-cause diagnosis (2026-07-02 session). |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH. Not a capital-safety issue (paper trading only, no live capital). Directly affects whether T4M-PROJ can ever produce the evidence volume needed to evaluate the T4 alpha thesis (market-latency edge) at the throughput the project's own design intended — a thin trickle of 10-20 bets/day makes commercial validation (EXP-017-equivalent for T4) slower and noisier to reach a confident verdict than the documented design anticipated. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-02, direct diagnostic session requested by Earl ("what the fuck is wrong with T-4... firing at only ~10-20 bets a DAY not even a game"). Root-caused via live production JSONL analysis plus source inspection of `run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `run_mlb_pregame.py`, and `c4_risk_engine/risk.py`. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-03 — dedup policy redesigned and shipped (T-225).** Earl confirmed 2026-07-02: CLAUDE.md's documented throughput target is authoritative — the dedup/position-management implementation is what needed to change, not the doc. **T-225 ruling (2026-07-03):** "allow re-entry per ticker" — `already_fired_game` changed from a permanent one-shot `set[str]` to a `dict[str, tuple[int,int]]` mapping ticker → `(h_so_far, ab_remaining)` at last entry; a ticker is now blocked from re-firing only when the current cycle's live state exactly matches its last-fired state, so re-entry tracks whether the state feeding `p_t4` has actually moved (new hit, new out, or `ab_remaining` decay). Rationale: the original one-shot guard was correct scaffolding while the Binomial math, contamination guards, kill switch, and C4 edge gate were unproven; those are now proven, and the real backstops against over-concentration (`max_single_event_exposure_usd`, drawdown kill switch, T4 band budget cap) supersede the blunt proxy. `PaperTrader.open_position()` gained `h_so_far_at_entry`/`ab_remaining_at_entry` kwargs for restart-safe reconstruction; `_reconstruct_fired_tickers()` renamed `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states()`. Verified no downstream code assumed ticker uniqueness (`run_resolve_mlb.py`, `PaperTrader.settle_position()` both key on `position_id`, not ticker). 11 new tests added; full suite 2293 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure — no regressions. Full rationale in DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-03 (3 entries). **T-226 update:** the "63 tickers/3 games" figure cited above as a possible co-binding constraint has been checked and does NOT hold as stated — it was an artifact of reading the T4 live cycle log (a ~69-minute snapshot window that only saw the day's 3 earliest-starting games in progress) rather than PIPE-06pre's full-day pregame fetch, which actually covered 6 of the 9 games scheduled 2026-07-02 (`markets_fetched=234`, `players_matched=51`). Real Kalshi KXMLBHIT venue coverage ≈ 6-7 of 9 games/day (60-70%), not ~3/9 (33%). Kalshi client cursor pagination (`kalshi_client.py:483-503`) verified correct — not silently dropping games. **Net effect: the addressable-market ceiling is a real but much smaller venue constraint than first assumed, and is NOT the primary explanation for the 10-20 bets/day observation — T-225's dedup policy is now confirmed as the dominant lever.** Full evidence trail in TODO_NEXT.md T-226 row and DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-02. **T-227 update:** the two session_ids / ~69-minute window that seeded T-226's original snapshot were checked directly against VPS `journalctl -u t4-mlb` for 2026-07-02 — both restarts (19:09:10-12 UTC, 19:14:41-43 UTC) log a clean `Deactivated successfully` systemd stop, with no exception/traceback/OOM signature. Both align (within ~30s-2min) with two manual code deploys that day — commit `086c66e` (the GG-068 fix, pushed 19:07:11Z) and commit `e3eb4bb` (T-220's extra-innings fix, pushed 19:14:08Z) — i.e., ordinary deploy-triggered `systemctl restart t4-mlb`, not a crash. GG-072 directly ruled out as a shared cause: GG-072 is PIPE-05/T1's HALT on `MLB_FEATURE_CSV_CONTRACT`; grepped `run_t4_mlb_live.py`/`t4_mlb_live_runner.py` for that contract/module — zero references, confirming T4-mlb's systemd service is architecturally isolated from GG-072's failure mode. No live coverage was lost beyond the ordinary cost of two brief deploy restarts. Full evidence trail in TODO_NEXT.md T-227 row. Tasks T-228/T-229 registered in TODO_NEXT.md, still open. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-03 — all five follow-on tasks (T-225 dedup redesign, T-226/T-227 sub-findings, T-229 Hard Rule 9 arithmetic reconciliation, T-228 vig/liquidity floor filter) now closed. See DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-03 entries and TODO_NEXT.md for full detail on each. |

### GG-077 — `fee_net_edge` Is Stored Always in the YES-Frame, Never Sign-Flipped for NO Bets (Corrupts Edge-Monotonicity Analysis; Possible CHECK 3 Gating Asymmetry)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-077 |
| Category | Data-contract / sign-convention bug, discovered while running T-184's EXP-017 MLB script against real data for the first time — same failure class as [[GG-037]]/T-177 (the anti-predictive edge-monotonicity guard), because this bug directly feeds that guard's input. |
| What was missed | `c5_execution/paper_trader.py::open_position()` computes `gross_edge` from the forecast record (always YES-frame per ADR-0016: `model_p - mid - vig/2`, never flipped for direction) and then `fee_net_edge = gross_edge - fee_per_contract` — also never flipped. The very next lines in the same function DO flip sign for direction when computing a sibling field, `fee_contaminated`, via `_directional_gross = gross_edge if direction == "YES" else -gross_edge` — proving the author was aware direction-flipping is required, but did not apply it to `fee_net_edge` itself. Net effect: the stored `fee_net_edge` field does not represent the actual, direction-effective edge of the bet placed for any NO-direction position — it represents the YES-side edge of the opposite bet. |
| When it should have been caught | Should have been caught the first time real MLB portfolio data was analyzed (T-184's `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py`, written SESSION 79 2026-06-23) — but the script was written and unit-tested against synthetic fixtures before any real positions existed, so the field's real-world sign behavior was never exercised until this session (2026-07-03), the first time the script ran against real data. |
| Phases it survived | Entire T1 MLB paper-trading run to date (49 resolved positions) — every one of them carries this field with the bug already baked in. `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py`'s 99-test suite (T-184) also passed the whole time, because its fixtures never exercised the "stored value present AND direction=NO" real-world case with a sign mismatch. |
| Why it survived | The T-184 test suite had a test (`test_uses_stored_fee_net_edge_when_present`, now replaced) that explicitly asserted the loader should trust a stored `fee_net_edge` value as-is — encoding the (at-the-time reasonable, pre-real-data) assumption that whatever the production system stored was correct by construction. No test exercised the NO-direction + stored-value-present combination against real sign expectations. |
| Impact | **Confirmed impact (analysis-only, current scope):** corrupted the Edge Monotonicity / Terciles check inside `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py` — before the fix, `edge>0 n=2 / edge<=0 n=47` exactly matched the YES/NO direction split (an artifact, not a finding about edge magnitude), which would have produced a false or meaningless verdict on the GG-037/T-177 anti-predictive guard once the portfolio reaches statistical scale. Did NOT affect EV/win-rate/P&L/threshold-breakdown figures (those use `pnl`/`outcome` directly). **Open, unresolved, higher-severity question (flagged here, NOT fixed this session — requires operator/DS review before touching live code):** `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`'s own production `edge` value (the one that feeds C4 `risk.py` CHECK 3, `abs(edge) >= MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE`) is computed the same way — YES-frame `gross_edge` minus the Kalshi platform fee, never flipped for direction — and `abs()` of a YES-frame quantity is NOT equal to the true direction-effective edge for NO bets once vig/half-spread and the fee term are both accounted for (derivation: true NO edge = `-gross_edge - fee`, vs. the system's `abs(gross_edge - fee)`; these differ by a term on the order of the fee, roughly ≤2% of stake for typical mid-range prices). Since 47 of 49 real MLB T1 positions (96%) are NO-direction, this is a systematic, small-magnitude mis-gating that has been present in every live NO bet since PIPE-05 went active. This does **not** invalidate the DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE EXP-017 result (the bot still made money), but it means the CHECK 3 approval gate has not been screening NO bets against the threshold it was designed to enforce, with unknown-but-nonzero effect on which bets were approved vs. rejected at the margin. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-03, while running T-184's `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py` against the first-ever real MLB portfolio download (49 resolved positions) for GG-004/EXP-017 commercial validation. Noticed the edge>0/edge<=0 split exactly matched the YES/NO direction count, traced through `_load_portfolio()` to `paper_trader.py:380-460`, and confirmed root cause by reading the `_directional_gross` flip applied to `fee_contaminated` but not to `fee_net_edge`. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-05 — production-side fix shipped, operator-authorized.** Analysis-side fix shipped 2026-07-03 (see prior text below); T-232 magnitude quantification run 2026-07-05 (analysis-only, unchanged, retained below). Operator explicitly instructed "proceed with GG-077" 2026-07-05, providing the operator/DS review sign-off this finding's production-side fix was withheld pending. Two code changes shipped, both direction-flip-before-fee, matching the already-correct pattern `paper_trader.py::open_position()` used for `fee_contaminated`: (1) `paper_trader.py:419-421` — `fee_net_edge` now computed from `_directional_gross` (gross_edge flipped for NO direction) instead of the raw YES-frame `gross_edge`; `fee_contaminated` simplified to `bool(fee_net_edge < 0.0)`. (2) `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` — new pure helper `_directional_edge(direction, gross_edge, kalshi_fee)`; direction is still assigned off the raw YES-frame `edge` against symmetric +/-0.02 thresholds (unaffected by GG-077), but `edge` is recomputed via the helper immediately after direction assignment, before it flows into CHECK 3 (`abs(edge) >= MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE`, risk.py), the stored forecast record, and `open_position()`'s approval dict — closing the false-approval mechanism T-232 quantified (12/56 positions, 21.4%, approved below the 0.04 policy floor via a deterministic `2 x fee_per_contract` overstatement). Regression tests added: `tests/test_paper_trader.py` (`test_fee_net_edge_is_direction_effective_for_no_bet`, `test_fee_net_edge_matches_gross_edge_for_yes_bet`); `tests/test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py` (`TestDirectionalEdge`, 3 tests, including a cross-check that the two independent flip implementations agree on identical NO-bet inputs). Full suite: 2,370 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (`test_feature_csv_freshness`). **Scope note:** fixes live, cron-active T1/T2/T3 MLB betting logic (PIPE-05) going forward only — does not retroactively correct any of the 56 already-closed positions T-232 characterized (their P&L is settled fact); prevents the same false-approval mechanism from recurring on new bets. Full detail: DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-05. --- *(Prior analysis-side text, retained for history:)* Analysis-side fix shipped 2026-07-03: `scripts/run_exp017_mlb.py::_load_portfolio()` no longer trusts any stored `fee_net_edge` value under any circumstance — it always recomputes via the direction-aware, already-tested `compute_fee_net_edge(direction, model_p, mip)`. Two new regression tests added; full suite 102/102 passing. Re-running EXP-017 MLB against real data showed `edge>0 n=49 / edge<=0 n=0` (no longer an artifact of direction), Terciles all positive, Monotonicity INCONCLUSIVE at that sample size. **T-232 magnitude quantification, run 2026-07-05 against the live VPS ledger (2 independent datasets):** (1) all 56 real closed T1/T2/T3 MLB positions in `paper_portfolio_mlb.json` (54 NO / 2 YES), recomputing true direction-effective edge as `(-gross_edge if NO else gross_edge) - fee_per_contract` against the stored, buggy `fee_net_edge` that CHECK 3 actually gated on at trade time; (2) the independent `logs/audit_mlb_edge_decisions.jsonl` sink (T-213/GG-060, 63 scored records, both APPROVED and REJECTED, back-deriving `gross_edge` from the logged `edge`+`market_implied_p` since fee is a deterministic function of mid-price). Both datasets agree exactly: **12 flips, all in the same direction — false approvals, zero false rejections.** Algebraic proof of why the asymmetry is always one-directional for NO bets with `\|gross_edge\| > fee` (true for every real position observed): `abs(stored) = abs(gross_edge - fee) = \|gross_edge\| + fee`, `abs(true) = abs(-gross_edge - fee) = \|gross_edge\| - fee`, so `abs(stored) - abs(true) = 2 * fee_per_contract` exactly, always positive — CHECK 3 can only ever overstate a NO bet's true edge, never understate it (YES bets are unaffected — no flip needed, stored == true). Measured overstatement across the 12 flipped positions: mean 2.82 percentage points (range 1.79-3.50pp), consistent with `2 * fee_per_contract` (observed fees 0.009-0.016). Of the 56 closed positions, **12 (21.4%) were approved with a true edge below the 0.04 `MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE` floor** (true edge as low as 0.0165-0.0399 vs. the 0.04 policy minimum) — real bets the risk engine's own stated policy should have rejected, waved through solely because the YES-frame computation inflated their apparent magnitude past the floor. Economic impact on this sub-population: $82.50 total stake (12.4% of the book's $664.69), +$3.48 combined P&L (win rate 7/12), vs. the full 56-position book's +$94.80 P&L (win rate 30/56). **Does not change any settled P&L** and does not retroactively invalidate EXP-017's DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE verdict. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-05 — analysis-side fix RESOLVED 2026-07-03; magnitude precisely quantified 2026-07-05 (T-232, analysis-only); production-side code fix shipped 2026-07-05, operator-authorized ("proceed with GG-077"). |

### GG-078 — T4 MLB's Real ~341-Bet/Day Cadence Means Kill-Switch Fate Is Decided Within ~15 Days Regardless of True Edge Sign; Long-Horizon Projections for T4 Are Not Meaningful at Any Stake Cap

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-078 |
| Category | Modeling/simulation scope gap, discovered while extending [[GG-071]]'s forward-compounding Monte Carlo (T-233) to include the T4 leg per direct operator correction — the original T-233 pass modeled T1 only. |
| What was missed | The T-233 Monte Carlo initially covered T1 (26 bets/active-day, verified real inputs) only, on the judgment call that T4's 13 resolved positions were too thin to calibrate a reliable win rate. Operator correctly flagged this as the wrong call to make unilaterally — T4 is real, live, cron-active, and per CLAUDE.md's own throughput design fires roughly 13x more often than T1 (~341 bets/day derived from ~50-55 bets/game × ~6-7/9 games/day, vs. T1's real observed 21-26/day). Omitting it left the simulation silent on the dominant-volume leg of the actual system. |
| When it should have been caught | Should have been part of the original T-233 scope, not a follow-up correction — CLAUDE.md's own Hard Rule 9 explicitly states the T4 throughput design is a first-class architectural fact ("Design throughput is thousands of bet evaluations per game... any implementation that only makes sense at low throughput is architecturally wrong"), which should have prompted including T4's real cadence from the start rather than excluding it for data-thinness reasons. |
| Phases it survived | One full artifact rebuild cycle (T-233's v1/v2 passes) before being caught by direct operator review. |
| Why it survived | Reasonable-sounding but ultimately wrong risk-averse judgment call: T4's 13 resolved bets genuinely can't support a reliable win-rate estimate, and the instinct to exclude a thinly-evidenced input rather than fabricate one is generally sound practice — but applied here, it silently dropped an entire leg of the real system instead of including it with explicit, labeled uncertainty (the correct handling of a low-n input, per this project's own "mark unsupported as Assumption" discipline). |
| Impact | Running T4 at its real derived cadence under two explicit win-rate scenarios (46.34% breakeven — statistically consistent with T4's real +$1.59-on-13-bets result; 53.06% T1-comparable — an explicit unproven upper case) produced a structural finding independent of which scenario is closer to truth: **T4's outcome is almost entirely decided within the first ~15 days, regardless of edge sign.** At breakeven, 29% of the 100,000 simulated paths hit the 20% drawdown kill switch on day 1 alone, 96.5% by day 5, ~100% by day 15 — a driftless (zero-edge) random walk at ~341 bets/day will hit any fixed drawdown barrier almost immediately; this is a real mathematical property of high-frequency zero-edge processes under a hard stop, not a simulation artifact. At T1-comparable edge, breach probability plateaus at 15.4% by day ~15 (vs. T1's own day-~150 plateau) — same $500-stake-cap-escape mechanism as T1, compressed ~10x by the higher frequency — and the surviving-path median explodes to $8,851,815 by day 365, an even less credible number than the T1-only $484,087 finding this whole exercise started from. **This means no tuning of individual constants (stake cap, starting bankroll, which legs are included) fixes the "ludicrous long-horizon number" problem — it is structural to projecting any persistent edge forward at real observed frequency for a full year with no capital/liquidity ceiling.** The corollary for capital planning: if T4 truly has near-zero edge, the drawdown kill switch will reveal that fact fast (within ~2 weeks) and cap the loss at roughly -11.6% of T4's $1,000 book before going fully dark for the remainder of any period modeled — a real, actionable, and previously undocumented characterization of T4's near-term risk profile. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-03, T-233 v3 pass, direct operator feedback: "Why is the MC still not scripted properly to include the full bot... Where is T-4? ~1000s of bets PER GAME not even PER DAY is still not being simulated?" |
| Resolution | OPEN. T4 leg added to the Monte Carlo (`mc_t4_leg.py`) with both scenarios; new artifact `t1-t4-mlb-mc-combined` presents the combined finding. Not resolved: (a) T4's true edge remains unknown pending T4M-ARITH/FLOW's own gating experiments (EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01, both currently INCONCLUSIVE at n=0 resolved per prior sessions); (b) no capital/liquidity-ceiling model has been built for either leg — the recommended fix (a shorter, ~30-60 day trustworthy planning horizon, or a real market-depth-constrained model per GG-045) has not been implemented, only recommended in the artifact. |
| **2026-07-12 reconciliation (T-350)** | [[T-342]] (2026-07-11) added a `stake_mode="flat"` path to `mc_forward_projection.py --from-ledger` to model T4M-PROJ's *observed* near-constant-dollar stake behavior (~$0.57/bet, not visibly scaling with bankroll over a short ~6-day window). Running that flat-mode report live against the real clean_n=412 ledger (T-350, 2026-07-12) found its risk shape is **structurally different** from this gap's original finding: FLOOR-scenario (breakeven) P(kill) accumulates gradually across the full year — 0.00% at 30d, 4.18% at 182d, 17.92% at 365d — rather than being decided within ~15 days. This is expected, not a contradiction: this gap's "~15-day decision" finding was derived under T4's real Kelly-**proportional** sizing (`mc_t4_leg.py`, ~341 bets/day theoretical cadence, since superseded ~9-19x downward by [[GG-080]]'s empirical measurement), where compounding stake-vs-bankroll dynamics under a hard drawdown barrier force fast resolution. A **flat** dollar stake never grows with bankroll, so it does not inherit that early-decision property — it spreads ruin risk gradually across the full horizon instead. Critically, T4M-PROJ's live code is still Kelly-proportional (`KELLY_T4=0.10`) — the flat-stake behavior is an *empirical regime property* of a tiny, tightly-clustered edge over a short observed window (per T-342's own DECISION_LOG framing), not a coded guarantee. If real stakes begin visibly compounding with bankroll as more data accrues, this gap's original fast-decision dynamic is the operative risk model for T4M-PROJ again, not the flat-mode gradual one. A GG-078 cross-reference caveat was added directly to `build_ledger_report()`'s T4M-PROJ/flat-mode output (alongside the existing GG-105 caveat) so this distinction is visible in every report run, not just in this log. This reconciliation does not resolve either open item from the original finding (T4's true edge is still unknown; no capital/liquidity-ceiling model exists) — it clarifies that the flat-mode long-horizon numbers describe a distinct, conditional sizing regime, not a refutation or supersession of the original ~15-day finding. |
| Status | OPEN — finding registered and evidenced; no code/architecture change made or required by this finding alone. The 2026-07-12 reconciliation (above) confirms the original ~15-day proportional-sizing finding stands unmodified for T4's real live sizing mechanic; it does not transfer to T-342's flat-mode hypothetical, which is now explicitly caveated in the tool's own output. |

### GG-079 — Failed Kalshi Orders (HTTP 410) Remain Counted in `open_positions`, Likely Inflating Deployed-Exposure Calculations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-079 |
| Category | Data-integrity gap in position lifecycle state, discovered incidentally while re-checking [[T-230]]'s status (2026-07-04 CONTINUATION, item 2 of this session's sequenced work — "backlog hygiene"). |
| What was missed | While re-verifying T-230's `_reconstruct_fired_ticker_states` gap against the live `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json`, found that 3 of the 07-01 positions sitting in that same gap carry `kalshi_order_status: "failed: HTTP 410"` — meaning the order was never actually accepted/executed by Kalshi (the market had gone (HTTP 410 Gone) by the time the order was submitted) — yet all 3 remain present in the `open_positions` list rather than a rejected/failed bucket. Any downstream code that sums `open_positions` by `pool_isolated`/`game_date` (deployed-exposure math, kill-switch proximity checks, C8 dashboard funnel stats) would count these 3 non-executed orders as real open capital, when in fact no capital was ever actually placed with the venue for them. |
| When it should have been caught | Should have been caught at the point `paper_trader.py::open_position()` (or its caller) receives a non-2xx order-placement response from Kalshi — a failed order should route to a distinct status/bucket at write time, not rely on a downstream reader to notice the `kalshi_order_status` string. |
| Phases it survived | At least since these 3 positions were opened (2026-07-01) through this session (2026-07-04) — three days, un-surfaced, until an unrelated T-230 re-check happened to inspect the same JSON rows. |
| Why it survived | No existing session-start check, stub scan, or governance-gap scan inspects `kalshi_order_status` values inside portfolio JSON files — Steps 1-4 and the Q1-Q6 adversarial checklist in CLAUDE.md check code, docs, and cron/log health, but not position-level data integrity inside the portfolio state files themselves. |
| Impact | **Not yet quantified this session** (registration only, per this task's explicit "status re-check, do not fix" scope) — magnitude of exposure-math distortion is currently unknown; likely small in dollar terms (3 positions) but the pattern (failed orders silently retained as "open") could recur at any volume if the root cause in the order-placement path is not fixed. Does not affect realized P&L (no real capital was ever placed for these 3), but could affect kill-switch/exposure-cap proximity calculations that assume `open_positions` reflects real deployed capital. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-04, CONTINUATION session, item 2 (T-230 backlog-hygiene status re-check) — direct query of `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` on the VPS while counting the true scope of T-230's entry-state gap. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-05.** Root cause fixed in `paper_trader.py::open_position()`: the Kalshi-submission branch now checks `order.ok` — on failure (non-2xx), the position is stamped `status="REJECTED"`, `kalshi_order_status=f"failed: {order.error}"`, appended to a new `rejected_positions` list (added to `portfolio_schema.py` as `F_REJECTED_POSITIONS`/`STATUS_REJECTED`, validated by `validate_portfolio_state()`), and the method returns `(ok_save, {})` — the same empty-dict-on-no-op contract every T1/T2/T3/T4 caller (MLB and NBA) already gates its fired/placed/deployed-capital counters on via `if pos:`, so no caller-side changes were needed. `_load_or_init()`/`_reload_locked()` both `setdefault("rejected_positions", [])` for backward compatibility with pre-existing portfolio files. 5 new regression tests added in `tests/test_paper_trader.py::TestFailedKalshiOrderRouting`, including one that mirrors `_reconstruct_t4_ledger()`'s exact summation logic to confirm rejected orders are excluded from deployed-exposure math. Live VPS `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` migrated (backup taken first): the 3 real `POS-599B86503F`/`POS-AF86537673`/`POS-996BF383AC` HTTP-410 positions moved from `open_positions` to `rejected_positions`, `status` corrected to `REJECTED`, validated clean via `portfolio_schema.validate_portfolio_state()`. `t4-mlb` systemd service restarted post-deploy to pick up the code change. T-234 closed. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-05 — code fix + tests + VPS data migration + service restart, all verified. |

### GG-080 — Hard Rule 9's "~8,600 Slots/Game" Theoretical Ceiling Has No Clean Per-Game Empirical Referent; Real Rows/Game and Bets/Game Are 9-19x Lower

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-080 |
| Category | Documentation-arithmetic gap in CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9 / T4 alpha thesis worked example, discovered while re-deriving [[T-229]]'s throughput math against a second full day of data (2026-07-04 CONTINUATION, item 4 of this session's sequenced work). |
| What was missed | T-229 (RESOLVED 2026-07-03, commit `ce0979c`) corrected Hard Rule 9's internal arithmetic inconsistencies using exactly one day of real data (2026-07-02: 3,697 rows, 23 qualifying, 5 assumed games), producing "18 batters x 2 thresholds x ~240 cycles/game = ~8,600 slots/game... ~50-55 bets/game." Re-running the same methodology against 07-02 **and** the now-available 07-03 full day surfaced two things T-229 could not have caught with n=1: **(1)** `cycle_id` in `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` is a single shared counter for the whole `t4-mlb` service process across all concurrently-live games, not a per-game counter — direct query shows 07-02's 4 real games (not 5) shared 209 total cycles for the entire day, and 07-03's 3 games shared 246 total cycles for the entire day. The "~240 cycles per 3-hour game" term in the Hard Rule 9 formula therefore does not have a clean empirical referent as a *per-game* quantity in the actual running architecture. **(2)** Even setting (1) aside, empirical rows/game is far below the theoretical 8,600 ceiling: 07-02 = 924 rows/game (3,697/4 games, ~9.3x below); 07-03 = 464 rows/game (1,393/3 games, ~18.5x below). Real bets/game: 07-02 = 5.75 (23/4), 07-03 = 3.67 (11/3) — both ~9-14x below the documented "~50-55 bets/game." The observed fire-rate *percentage* (0.62%, 0.79%) is consistent with what T-229 found — the gap is entirely in the opportunity-volume terms (slots, rows), not the fire rate. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-229's original resolution (2026-07-03), if a second day of data or a direct rows-per-game empirical check (rather than a theoretical batters x thresholds x cycles formula) had been available/run at the time. T-229 explicitly flagged itself as "n=1-day" at the time, so this is an expected, not a surprising, follow-on gap — not a defect in T-229's own methodology given the data it had. |
| Phases it survived | One day (2026-07-03, T-229's resolution) before this session's re-derivation with a second full day of data. |
| Why it survived | No standing check re-validates a "RESOLVED" documentation-arithmetic task against newly-accumulated production data once the task closes — CLAUDE.md's Hard Rule 9 figures are treated as settled once corrected, with no scheduled re-derivation trigger as more real game-days accumulate. |
| Impact | Documentation-accuracy only — does not affect any live risk control, kill switch, or capital decision (Hard Rule 9 is a design-intent/architecture-framing section, not a governing parameter consumed by `risk.py` or any executing code). Impact is on operator/DS understanding of true T4 MLB throughput when reasoning about capacity, load, or future architecture decisions. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-04, CONTINUATION session, item 4 (T-229 throughput re-derivation, explicitly scoped as present-only/no-CLAUDE.md-edit since T-229 is DS-owned) — direct query of `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` on the VPS across 07-02 and 07-03 full days. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-04 (SESSION 118 continuation).** Operator authorized the supersession explicitly ("proceed with all 3/4... full permission to proceed"). CLAUDE.md's T4 alpha section (the "~8,600 evaluation slots" paragraph) and Hard Rule 9 both rewritten to: (1) drop the theoretical `18 × 2 × ~240 cycles` derivation as the headline figure, noting `cycle_id`'s global-not-per-game nature as the reason it can't be cleanly derived; (2) state the empirical figures directly — 924 rows/4.6 bets per game-day (2026-07-02), 464 rows/3.7 bets per game-day (2026-07-03); (3) preserve the underlying architectural point (per-cycle/per-bet continuous evaluation, not batched pre-scheduled bets) since that holds regardless of the corrected magnitude; (4) flag n=2 game-days as not yet a stable long-run estimate, to be re-derived as more data accumulates. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-04 — CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9 and T4 alpha section rewritten with empirical throughput figures, T-235 closed. |
