### GG-095 — DECISION_LOG T-094's Documented Brier Drift Ladder Does Not Match `monitor.py`'s Shipped Implementation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-095 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — a decision's documented derivation and its shipped code silently diverged with no reconciliation step. |
| What was missed | DECISION_LOG.md's T-094 entry (logged 2026-06-02) documents a 4-level **absolute** Brier drift ladder: baseline ~0.245, L1=0.255 (watch), L2=0.265 (warning), L3=0.275 (alert), L4=0.280 (kill switch). Direct inspection of `src/prediction_market_bot/c7_monitoring/monitor.py` (lines 66-79) shows the actually-shipped scheme is structurally different: `BRIER_BASELINE=0.2416` plus **relative deltas** (`BRIER_L1_DELTA=0.02`, `BRIER_L2_DELTA=0.05`, `BRIER_L3_DELTA=0.10`), only **3** levels (L1=watch, L2=alert→ALT-001, L3=pause+kill→ALT-002) — not 4. Neither the level count, the absolute/relative framing, nor most of the numeric values match what T-094 recorded as the derivation. |
| When it should have been caught | Whenever `monitor.py` was implemented or last modified from the delta-based scheme — the implementation should have been checked against T-094's own documented derivation at that time, or T-094 should have been corrected to match the as-shipped design. |
| Phases it survived | Since T-094 was logged (2026-06-02) through today — over a month of live C7 monitoring running on thresholds that don't match their own decision record. |
| Why it survived | `MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md` (the structured grounding doc for this component) was itself stale per the same GG-074 audit ("Placeholders pending G4 evidence" when the scheme was actually live) — nothing was cross-checking the decision log against the shipped constants because the intermediate document that should have flagged the mismatch was not being read. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. `monitor.py`'s actual thresholds are *tighter* in the watch/alert bands (baseline+0.02=0.2616 vs T-094's stated L1=0.255) but the real kill-switch trigger point differs materially in structure (L3/ALT-002 in the shipped 3-level scheme vs. T-094's L4=0.280 in a 4-level scheme) — this is not simply "the same math with different rounding," it is two different designs. Until reconciled, no one can say with confidence which one is the intended, DS-approved design: the logged decision or the running code. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-06, GG-074 background audit agent surfaced the discrepancy while correcting `MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md`; independently re-verified by the calling session via direct `grep` of `monitor.py`'s constants against the T-094 DECISION_LOG text before registering. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-07, T-247.** Operator direction (2026-07-07): "Proceed with L4 design - this was the intended design that had not been built out initially." Direct inspection of `monitor.py`'s own module docstring (lines 27-39, unchanged since authorship) resolved the (a)/(b) ambiguity: the docstring already documented `drift_alert_l3_pause (ERROR) — Brier > baseline + 0.10 (ALT-002)` as its own intended design — a 3-tier Brier ladder (L1 watch/L2 alert/L3 pause) that stops short of a full kill, with the real kill switch (L4, in the MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md ladder-table sense) driven independently by `risk.py`'s `MAX_DAILY_LOSS_PCT`/`max_drawdown_pct` (confirmed live and already firing `trigger_kill_switch()` correctly at `risk.py:216,219,563` — this mechanism was never broken). The shipped bug was narrower than either (a) or (b): `get_drift_snapshot()`'s Brier ladder (line ~370) assigned `alert_level = ALERT_L4_KILL` directly at the `BRIER_L3_DELTA` crossing — a straight implementation error where the wrong constant was assigned — meaning `ALERT_L3_PAUSE` was fully wired in `check_and_alert()`'s `severity_map`/`event_map` (ERROR severity, `drift_alert_l3_pause` event) but structurally unreachable, and every L3-level Brier breach was auto-firing a full, undocumented kill switch. **Fix applied:** `get_drift_snapshot()` now sets `ALERT_L3_PAUSE` (not `ALERT_L4_KILL`) at the `BRIER_L3_DELTA` crossing; `check_and_alert()`'s L4 auto-kill branch is retained (for a possible future integration reflecting risk.py's independent kill state into a unified `DriftSnapshot`) but is no longer reachable from this module's own Brier computation, so a Brier-drift breach alone can never again auto-fire the kill switch. `BRIER_L3_DELTA`'s constant comment and a new code comment at the L4 branch both document that real L4/kill is intentionally external to this module. T-094's absolute/4-level numeric scheme (2026-06-02) is superseded by this entry — `monitor.py`'s relative-delta/3-level design (plus risk.py's externally-driven L4) is confirmed as the correct, intended design; T-094 is not corrected in place (immutable log entry) but is superseded by this GG-095 resolution and this session's DECISION_LOG row. **What was NOT built:** MONITORING_DRIFT_PLAN.md's ladder table describes L3 as auto-halting new position opens specifically (not merely a differently-labeled log line); no such enforcement mechanism (e.g., RiskEngine consulting C7's alert_level before approving a forecast) exists anywhere in the codebase today — confirmed by grep across `risk.py`/`paper_trader.py` (no `pause`/`halt_new`/`block_new` hits). Building that cross-component (C4↔C7) wiring is a distinct architectural decision beyond this fix's scope and was not built unilaterally; flagged to the operator as the next open decision point. Tests updated in `tests/test_c7_monitor.py`: `test_l3_pause_when_brier_above_l3` (was `test_l4_kill_when_brier_above_l3`), `test_error_log_on_l3_pause` (was `test_critical_log_on_l4`), `test_l3_pause_does_not_auto_trigger_kill_switch` (was `test_l4_auto_triggers_kill_switch`, inverted), `test_l4_auto_triggers_kill_switch_if_ever_set` (was `test_l4_does_not_double_trigger`, now exercises the dormant L4 branch directly via a patched snapshot so its idempotency logic stays covered). Full suite: 2,374 passed / 7 skipped / 1 pre-existing unrelated failure (feature CSV staleness, untouched by this fix). |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-07 — T-247. `monitor.py` L3/L4 ladder bug fixed; kill switch can no longer auto-fire from Brier drift alone; real L4 confirmed already correctly driven by `risk.py`. Open follow-up (not a blocker, no task assigned yet): whether L3_PAUSE should gain real "halt new positions" enforcement via a new C4↔C7 wire, or whether a correctly-logged ERROR-severity audit event is sufficient — operator decision, not built this session. |

Both rows added to the "Completed Tests" table. ### GG-098 — MLB T1 Aggregate EV Metrics Are Cited as Milestones While Still Variance-Dominated (Small-n Subset Structure Masked; Aggregate Sign-Flipped n=151→169)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-098 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — a headline commercial-validation metric (EXP-017 Tier-1 +$0.1009/bet) that is not internally decomposed can mask a losing sub-strategy inside a "positive" aggregate. |
| What was missed | EXP-017 reported a single aggregate EV/bet and a monotonicity guard, but never split the book by bet DIRECTION. Decomposition (EXP-017B) shows all positive edge is NO-side (n=140, +8.71%/staked, +$125.50) while the YES sub-book (n=11) is −62.11%/staked (−$110.27), erasing ~88% of NO profit. The aggregate +$0.1009/bet is bootstrap-insignificant (95% CI [−$1.86,+$2.13], P(EV>0)=53%). |
| When it should have been caught | At the EXP-017 Tier-1 read (2026-07-06). A Tier verdict on an aggregate should have been paired with a direction/threshold/edge-bucket decomposition before being cited as evidence of edge. |
| Phases it survived | MLB paper-trading G6 pursuit (49→151 resolved). The EXP-017 script has a monotonicity guard (GG-037/T-177 class) but no direction split. |
| Why it survived | No internal-validity companion existed for EXP-017; the aggregate + monotonicity guard were treated as sufficient. EXP-017B/C/D were built this session to fill that gap. |
| Impact | MEDIUM. Does NOT invalidate the Tier-1 milestone, but materially qualifies it: the edge is NO-only, non-monotonic in realized profit, tail-concentrated, and aggregate-insignificant. EXP-017C shows the model is nonetheless well-calibrated (slope CI includes 1) — so the shortfall vs expected is variance, not miscalibration. Risk is over-crediting the aggregate when reasoning about G6/live readiness. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-07, edge-strategy decomposition session (EXP-017B/C/D), via direct decomposition of the live cached book (`logs/.vps_portfolio_cache_mlb.json`, n=151). Headline reproduced to 4 dp before decomposing. |
| Resolution | (1) EXP-017B/C/D registered as the standing internal-validity companion to EXP-017 (re-run as n grows). (2) ADR-0042 — **corrected 2026-07-07**: the original proposal to quarantine YES was REJECTED by the operator as over-calibration on n≈11; ADR now monitors YES and keeps collecting. (3) A/B harness given a `MIN_REMOVED_N`=30 guard so category-removal filters return `UNDERPOWERED_REMOVAL` not DEFENSIBLE on tiny removed sets. (4) T-250 reframed to monitor-and-investigate-at-higher-n; T-251 re-runs the decomposition as n grows. Does NOT close GG-004. |
| Status | OPEN (watch item, by design) — registered 2026-07-07. The core gap (aggregate metrics cited as milestones while variance-dominated) is reinforced by the aggregate sign-flipping +$15.23 (n=151) → −$44.76 (n=169) in 18 bets. YES underperformance is variance-consistent (Binomial P(≤0)=0.073 on the k=2 0/7 run; model under-predicts k=2 at threshold level). Pre-registered monitor: revisit YES only at n_YES ≥ 40 with Fisher p<0.05 AND an identified mechanism. Watch item — no quarantine, no stop-work. **Citation-hygiene verification pass, 2026-07-12 (T-349):** audited every "milestone"/EV-citation surface for an unhedged claim — `CLAUDE.md` § "commercial validation gap", `07_TEST_VALIDATION_EVIDENCE/EVIDENCE/FR-03/EVIDENCE.md`, `exp_output/EXP-017B-DECOMP/FINDINGS.md`, `MEMORY_BANK.md` — all four already carry the direction-split/bootstrap-insignificance/"milestone not proof" caveats this gap calls for; none found requiring correction. This gap stays OPEN as its own pre-registered watch item (the n_YES≥40 trigger above), not because a doc-hygiene action remains outstanding. |

### GG-099 — Anti-Predictive / Edge-Monotonicity Guards Are Not Wired Per-Band; the ACTIVE T4M-PROJ Band Has No Resolved-Outcome Join, So Its Edge Signal Cannot Be Checked at All

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-099 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — a load-bearing safety check (the GG-037/T-177 anti-predictive / edge-monotonicity guard) exists for one band but is absent from, or unmeasurable for, the others — most critically the active, highest-throughput one. |
| What was missed | The anti-predictive guard (does the edge>0 cohort win MORE than edge≤0, or inversely?) lives only in offline scripts pointed at the T1 pregame book (`run_mlb_calib_monitor.py`, `run_exp017_mlb.py`, EXP-017B/C/D). It is NOT wired per-band. Verified by grep: `run_t4_mlb_live.py` (T4M-PROJ, ACTIVE) and `t4_mlb_execution_engine.py` (T4M-ARITH/FLOW) contain ZERO anti-predictive/monotonicity/fisher logic. `c7_monitoring/monitor.py` has none either (its only `monotonic` tokens are `time.monotonic()`). |
| When it should have been caught | At T4M-PROJ activation (ADR-0023) — a band going live should not be exempt from the same predictive-integrity check its T1 sibling has. The GG-037 NBA finding (anti-predictive edge in an efficient market) is the precedent that makes this check mandatory for every live band. |
| Phases it survived | T4M-PROJ's entire live history. **Corrected 2026-07-08 (T-254)** — the original text here cited `t4_position_trajectories.jsonl` (3,097 decision records, 1,532 `FIRE_BAYESIAN` actions) as T4M-PROJ's history. Direct inspection found that file is **100% NBA data** (ticker prefixes `KXNBAPTS`/`NBA_TEST_00[12]`/`NBA_EXIT_TES`, all summing to 3,097) — it belongs to the dark/frozen NBA T4 band (`t4_execution_engine.py`), not the ACTIVE MLB T4M-PROJ band (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`). This is the exact GG-061 NBA/MLB-T4-conflation failure mode recurring. None of T4M-PROJ's real closed positions have ever been evaluated for anti-predictiveness — that part of the original claim stands — but the evidentiary file named was wrong. |
| Impact | HIGH (deferred severity). Three compounding holes, item 1 corrected 2026-07-08: (1) **CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (T-254)** — the original claim "T4M-PROJ has no resolved-outcome join at all" is **factually wrong**. `run_resolve_mlb.py`'s shared `_resolve_pool()` helper already resolves the isolated T4 MLB pool (`paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json`) exactly like the T1/T2 pool, and every `closed_positions` record already carries `outcome`/`pnl`/`model_p`/`mid_price_at_entry`/`vig_at_entry` (273 resolved positions as of 2026-07-08: win=153, loss=120, total P&L=+$16.0432 — matches the portfolio's own `total_realized_pnl` exactly). The real, narrower gap: that resolved data was never extracted into a stable, analysis-ready artifact independent of the live-mutating portfolio JSON, and (per GG-077) the stored `fee_net_edge`/`gross_edge`/`t1_edge` fields are YES-frame-only and wrong-signed for NO bets. **Closed via T-254**: `scripts/build_t4_resolved_ledger.py` reads `closed_positions`, recomputes edge direction-aware via the audited `run_exp017_mlb.compute_fee_net_edge()` helper (GG-077-safe, not reimplemented), tags every row `band=T4M-PROJ`, and writes `logs/t4_mlb_resolved_ledger.jsonl` — verified against the live VPS book (273/273 rows extracted, win/loss/P&L totals match source exactly; spot-checked edge recomputation against a known sample position). This does **not** resolve items 2/3 below. (2) The MLB book is **not band-tagged** (169 closed = 167 `FCST-M` + 2 `T2-MLB`, no `band` field), so even the T1 monitors cannot separate bands — still open, T-253's scope. (3) **RESOLVED 2026-07-07 (T-256)** — `run_t177_edge_monitor.py` defaulted to the **stale legacy NBA portfolio** (`logs/paper_portfolio.json`), not the live MLB book. Fixed: default now resolves via new `--sport {mlb,nba}` (default `mlb`) to the same VPS-synced `.vps_portfolio_cache_<sport>.json` cache path the session-start governance hook already uses; every report prints an explicit band-mix caveat since it remains pool-level, not per-band (true per-band split is still T-255's scope). **Running the corrected default against the live MLB book for the first time surfaced a new, material finding — see GG-100.** Capital risk is currently paper-only, but this must be closed before any T4 band informs live capital. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-07, code audit prompted by operator question ("do we have anti-predictive structures for ALL decision trees, especially T4?"). Direct grep of src/scripts + inspection of `t4_position_trajectories.jsonl` schema and the MLB book's `forecast_id` tags. **Item 1 correction discovered 2026-07-08**, T-254 implementation session: ticker-prefix analysis of `t4_position_trajectories.jsonl` (100% NBA), grep of `run_t4_mlb_live.py`/`t4_mlb_execution_engine.py` (zero references to that file or to `FIRE_BAYESIAN`), and direct inspection of `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` + `run_resolve_mlb.py` (which already implements the resolved-outcome join the original claim said was missing). Surfaced before any code was written against the wrong premise; operator confirmed the re-scope via explicit choice before implementation proceeded. |
| Resolution | Iteration task set registered in TODO_NEXT, now all DONE: T-253 (band-tag the resolved-bet stream) — DONE. T-254 (T4M-PROJ resolved-outcome ledger, `scripts/build_t4_resolved_ledger.py`) — DONE 2026-07-08. T-255 (per-band anti-predictive + calibration monitor, `scripts/run_band_monitor.py`, 19/19 tests) — DONE 2026-07-08. T-256 (fix `run_t177_edge_monitor` default + make band-aware) — DONE 2026-07-07. T-257 (schedule the per-band monitor weekly, cron installed live on the VPS with a `--pull`→`--portfolio` bug found and fixed in the process) — DONE 2026-07-08. T-258 (unit tests incl. the GG-100-inversion-immunity guardrail) — DONE 2026-07-08. Full narrative of the T-255/T-257 build and the T-257 VPS-install bug fix is recorded under GG-100's Status field below (cross-referenced there since it was written during that same investigation thread). See ADR-0042 D2 (harness-as-gate) — this operationalizes it for live bands. |
| Status | **RESOLVED — 2026-07-08.** All six sub-items (T-253 through T-258) are code-complete, tested, and live in production: every band (T1/T2/T3/T4M-PROJ) now has anti-predictive/calibration coverage, gated `MIN_N=30` (GG-098), scheduled weekly (Mondays 14:20 UTC) and confirmed actually installed + dry-run-verified on the live VPS crontab (not just committed to the repo). No band currently alarms. This was the prerequisite before any T4 band could be cited as edge evidence or inform live capital — that prerequisite is now met structurally; it does not by itself constitute a positive edge finding (see GG-004 for the separate, still-open commercial-sample-size question). |

### GG-100 — T1 MLB book flips ANTI-PREDICTIVE for the first time once the edge monitor is pointed at live data (T-256 discovery)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-100 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — the correct monitor existed (`run_t177_edge_monitor.py`, T-177/GG-037 methodology) but had never been run against the live MLB book, because its default portfolio path was wrong (GG-099 item 3 / T-256). The finding below was invisible until that default was fixed. |
| What was missed | Once `run_t177_edge_monitor.py --sport mlb` (T-256 fix) is run against `logs/.vps_portfolio_cache_mlb.json` (n=169 resolved, pool-level — T1 dominant, 2 T2-MLB positions mixed in per GG-099 item 2), the Fisher edge-monotonicity test returns **ANTI-PREDICTIVE**: p=0.0236 (< alpha=0.10), win rate for `fee_net_edge > 0` positions is LOWER than for `fee_net_edge <= 0`. Fisher OR=0.350. Tercile P&L is non-monotonic and inverted at the extremes: low-edge tercile +$177.38, mid −$58.31, high-edge tercile −$163.83. This is the same qualitative pattern that triggered GG-037 (NBA anti-predictive finding, ADR-0027) — now appearing in the ACTIVE MLB pool that G6 is being pursued against. |
| When it should have been caught | The moment the MLB book crossed a sample size where the edge≤0 comparison group became non-trivial (per the 2026-07-06 SESSION 122 log, at n=151 the comparison group was still n=3/INCONCLUSIVE). By n=169 it resolved to a real, significant signal — but nothing was watching, because the only monitor capable of catching it (`run_t177_edge_monitor.py`) was pointed at the wrong book (GG-099 item 3) until T-256 fixed it today. |
| Phases it survived | All of G5/G6 paper trading to date — this is the first session this check has ever run correctly against live MLB data. |
| Why it survived | Compounding of two gaps: (1) GG-099 item 3 (monitor defaulted to the stale NBA book, so it was structurally incapable of seeing the MLB book at all), and (2) GG-004's own tracked commercial-validation narrative (SESSION 122, n=151, DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE, EV=+$0.1009/bet) was the only lens on this book's health, and EV-per-bet alone does not surface an edge-monotonicity inversion — a book can show positive aggregate EV while the *edge signal itself* is inverted, if the base rate/vig capture is doing the work instead of the edge condition. |
| Impact | **HIGH — this is the single most consequential thing found this session.** If real, it means the T1 MLB model's `model_p > MIP + vig` edge condition is not just noisy but actively anti-correlated with outcome on this book: the bot is winning *more* on bets it thought had *less* edge. This directly threatens the G6 commercial case (GG-004) — a positive aggregate EV built on an inverted edge signal is not evidence the methodology works, per the exact reasoning that produced GG-037/ADR-0027 on the NBA side. Confounds/caveats that must be resolved before treating this as confirmed, not just registered: (a) **GG-077** — the `fee_net_edge` sign-convention bug for NO-direction bets; found this session that `fee_contaminated` is `False` for all 169 positions despite 140 having negative `fee_net_edge`, meaning `run_t177_edge_monitor.py`'s `exclude_fee_contaminated=True` default filter is currently a complete no-op on this book — the ANTI-PREDICTIVE verdict has NOT been screened for fee-sign contamination. Split-by-entry-date sanity check (pre/post the reported 2026-07-05 GG-077 production fix) shows the inverted pattern in both subsets (pre-fix n=99: wr_pos=0.250 vs wr_neg=0.516; post-fix n=70: wr_pos=0.286 vs wr_neg=0.531) — suggestive that this is not purely a GG-077 sign-bug artifact, but this is not a rigorous independent significance test on the post-fix subset alone. (b) **GG-099 item 2** — the book is not band-tagged; 2/169 positions are T2-MLB, not T1, so this is a pool-level, not strictly T1, verdict. (c) The GG-004 SESSION 122 baseline (n=151) is now stale — this session's fetch shows n=169 with aggregate P&L sign-flipped negative (+$15.23 → −$44.76); GG-004 itself needs a fresh entry reflecting this, separate from this gap. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-07, immediate downstream consequence of running the T-256 fix (`run_t177_edge_monitor.py --sport mlb`, no `--portfolio` override) for the first time against the live VPS-synced MLB cache. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED-AS-ARTIFACT 2026-07-08 (GG-099 iteration, Terminal 1).** Confound (a) — the GG-077 fee-sign bug — is the entire explanation; confound (b) turned out not to matter (see below). Root cause confirmed directly: `run_t177_edge_monitor.py`'s loader read the **stored** `fee_net_edge` field verbatim (`p.get("fee_net_edge")`), which is YES-framed and wrong-signed for NO-direction bets (GG-077). The live book is 157/169 NO, so ~93% of positions were mis-cohorted into the wrong edge bucket, and the Fisher test on that mis-cohorted data produced the false ANTI-PREDICTIVE result. Fix: the loader (`_load_portfolio`/`_load_csv`) now recomputes `fee_net_edge` direction-aware via the audited `run_exp017_mlb.compute_fee_net_edge(direction, model_p, mip)` helper (same helper T-254 uses for the T4M-PROJ ledger) whenever a position carries `model_p`/`mid_price_at_entry` — true of every real production record. The stored field is used only as a fallback for legacy/synthetic records missing those two fields, and every fallback use is now counted and surfaced as an explicit warning (never silent). Reproduction, filed at `exp_output/GG-100-DIAGNOSIS/FINDINGS.md`: stored-edge method reproduces GG-100 exactly (edge>0 n=29 wr=0.276 vs edge≤0 n=140 wr=0.521, Fisher p=0.0236, ANTI-PREDICTIVE); direction-aware method gives edge>0 n=166 wr=0.488 vs edge≤0 n=3 wr=0.000, Fisher p=0.2468, **INCONCLUSIVE** — the cohort-size collapse (166→29 under stored edge) is the fingerprint of the sign bug, since the bot only fires when it sees edge, so almost every position has true edge>0. Live re-run after the fix (`python3 scripts/run_t177_edge_monitor.py --sport mlb`) confirms: n=169, verdict **INCONCLUSIVE (p=0.2468)**, not ANTI-PREDICTIVE. Confound (b) (band mixing) was not the driver — T-253's band-tagging landed independently and the book already carries a `band` field, but the sign bug alone fully explains the false alarm without needing to invoke it. Regression coverage: 3 new tests in `tests/test_t177_edge_monitor.py` (`TestGG100SignSafeRecompute`) reconstruct the exact win/loss pattern that produces this inversion — one proves the fixed loader is NOT anti-predictive on it (in fact MONOTONIC), one proves the raw-stored-edge path (the pre-fix behavior) DOES reproduce ANTI-PREDICTIVE on identical underlying data, one covers the legacy fallback path. All 32 tests pass (29 original + 3 new), no regressions. **Explicitly NOT resolved by this fix, and must not be conflated with it:** the underlying aggregate P&L is still negative (n=169, −$44.76, sign-flipped from n=151's +$15.23) — that is GG-004's variance/sample-adequacy question, tracked separately below, and remains open. Not-anti-predictive ≠ positive alpha; this resolution only retracts the false claim that the edge signal itself is inverted. |
| Status | **RESOLVED (artifact) — 2026-07-08.** The ANTI-PREDICTIVE finding was a measurement bug (GG-077 sign inversion in the monitor's loader), not a real signal-inversion in the model. Do not escalate to DS as a no-alpha/monotonicity-guard finding — there is nothing to escalate. The separate, real, still-open question is GG-004 (aggregate EV variance at n=169), which this resolution explicitly does not close. **Per-band coverage closeout (2026-07-08, Terminal 2, T-255/T-257/T-258):** the standing structural gap this whole GG-099 chain was opened to fix — no per-band monitor, T4M-PROJ zero coverage — is now code-complete. `scripts/run_band_monitor.py` runs the same GG-077-safe `compute_fee_net_edge`/`edge_monotonicity` plus calibration-slope/CI/ECE per band (T1/T2/T3 from the band-tagged book, T4M-PROJ from the T-254 ledger), gated by `MIN_N=30` (GG-098: T2 n=2 and T3 n=0 both correctly return `UNDERPOWERED`, not a verdict). Live run: T1 n=167 INCONCLUSIVE p=0.2462 (consistent with this entry's p=0.2468), T4M-PROJ n=273 INCONCLUSIVE p=0.2582 — **first-ever per-band read for T4M-PROJ**, no alarm. 19 tests in `tests/test_band_monitor.py`, including a synthetic NO-majority fixture proving the monitor is immune to the exact stored-edge inversion documented above (never flags `E_ANTI` on data a naive YES-frame formula would invert) — this is the concrete guard closing GG-054's "never fired in the wild" concern for this monitor specifically. Scheduled weekly (Mondays 14:20 UTC, `deploy/cron.conf`) in `--ci` warn-only mode (exit 1 on any band alarm, captured in cron log only, no auto-kill — GG-098). **VPS install closeout (2026-07-08, same session):** installing the committed cron line surfaced a real bug before it could fire — both `build_t4_resolved_ledger.py --pull` and `run_band_monitor.py`'s bare defaults assume a *local dev machine* SSH-pulling a portfolio snapshot from the VPS; running that same convention ON the VPS (where the cron job executes) crashes with `FileNotFoundError`, since `--pull` shells out to `ssh prediction-market-vps` from inside the VPS itself and the default cache path never exists there. Fixed in `deploy/cron.conf` (commit `418c78c`) to use explicit `--portfolio logs/paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` / `--portfolio logs/paper_portfolio_mlb.json` flags. Installed onto the live VPS crontab via append/diff-verify-replace (preserved PIPE-08's dynamically-adjusted lines; not a wholesale overwrite). Dry-ran the exact installed command on the VPS: `EXIT_CODE:0`, T1 n=167 INCONCLUSIVE, T4M-PROJ n=331 INCONCLUSIVE, no alarm. GG-099's full chain (T-253 through T-258) is now code-complete, tested, and live in production — this gap is fully closed, no residual action. |

### GG-101 — GG-082/SESSION_CLAIMS.md makes concurrent sessions visible but does not isolate their working trees (T-254 session discovery)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-101 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — process gap in a process (GG-082) that was itself built to close a process gap. |
| What was missed | GG-082's SESSION_CLAIMS.md protocol (Step 0 of the mandatory session-start sequence) makes concurrent sessions *visible* to each other and instructs re-diffing shared nav files against `HEAD` before writing — but it does not isolate concurrent sessions' working directories. Two sessions (this one, T-254; and a concurrent one, T-256) were confirmed operating in the **same physical git working directory** at the same time. Mid-session, the T-256 session ran `git stash push` scoped to files it did not own (my uncommitted edits to `TODO_NEXT.md`, `DECISION_LOG.md`, `GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`, plus its own T-253-adjacent WIP in `PLAN.md`/`MEMORY_BANK.md`/several `src/` files) in order to commit its own change cleanly — a considerate, non-destructive choice on that session's part (confirmed via `git stash list`/`git reflog`: no destructive `restore`/`reset`, content fully recoverable), but the *mechanism* that made this necessary — a shared, non-isolated working tree — is a real risk regardless of how carefully any one session behaves. A less careful concurrent operation (`git checkout -- <file>`, `git reset --hard`, `git clean -fd`, or even a differently-scoped `git stash` that got popped/dropped by mistake) would have caused genuine, permanent data loss with no recovery path. |
| When it should have been caught | When GG-082 was originally designed (2026-07-06) — the fix addressed *narrative conflation* (one session's work attributed to another in shared docs) but implicitly assumed sequential, not concurrent-in-the-same-tree, sessions. The SESSION_CLAIMS.md file's own existence proves concurrent sessions were anticipated; working-tree isolation between them was not. |
| Phases it survived | G5/G6 paper trading to date; every multi-terminal session since GG-082 (2026-07-06) ran in this same shared-tree configuration without the risk being surfaced, because no prior collision happened to touch the same uncommitted files at the same moment. |
| Why it survived | GG-082's fix was scoped to the specific failure it was built to catch (narrative conflation caught 2026-07-03) rather than the broader class of shared-mutable-state risk that a non-isolated git working tree represents. Nothing in the session-start protocol checks *how* concurrent sessions are invoked (same directory vs. `git worktree`) — that is an operator/tooling-launch decision outside any single session's control, so no session-side check could have caught it, only the launch configuration itself. |
| Impact | Demonstrated near-miss, not yet a realized loss: this session's `TODO_NEXT.md`/`DECISION_LOG.md` edits were briefly stashed by the concurrent session and were fully recovered (see T-254's 2026-07-08 DECISION_LOG entry) — verified byte-for-byte via `git stash show -p` before trusting the recovery. But recovery depended entirely on the concurrent session choosing `stash` over a destructive operation, and on this session catching the anomaly via `git status`/`git reflog` rather than assuming its own edits were simply never applied. A future collision under a less careful sequence (or a session that doesn't independently verify file state before committing) could silently and permanently lose committed-quality work. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-08, this session (T-254), via `git status --short` showing unexpected file churn immediately before an intended commit, followed by `git log`/`git reflog`/`git stash list` diagnosis. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-08 (corrected 2026-07-08 SESSION 127 — the original text here, drafted 2026-07-07, claimed a CLAUDE.md Step 0 rewrite and a new `.claude/commands/session-start.md` that were never actually written; caught during session-end file-state verification before commit, not by a separate review — see Hard Rule 10/Q6 discipline. Protocol wiring landed subsequently and is confirmed present in the current tree.)** Operator selected defense-in-depth: isolation + cohesion, not a warning-only fix. The tooling layers are real and built: **(Isolation)** `scripts/new_session_worktree.sh` launches each concurrent session in a dedicated `git worktree` + short-lived `session/<utc>-<slug>` branch checked out from a freshly-fetched `origin/main`; `scripts/end_session_worktree.sh` retires it (refuses removal if uncommitted work remains). Two sessions launched this way have physically separate checkouts — one session's `stash`/`reset --hard`/`checkout --`/`clean -fd` cannot reach another's files. **(Cohesion)** `scripts/claim_session.sh` serializes session claims through `origin` (commit the claim row alone → push → on non-fast-forward, rebase and retry), which defeats the 2026-07-07 T-247 double-claim race; `.gitattributes` sets `merge=union` on the strictly-append-only `SESSION_CLAIMS.md` so simultaneous claim appends auto-merge instead of colliding; `.claude/hooks/session_guard.sh` (read-only) reports branch/worktree status, HEAD-vs-`origin/main` drift, other sessions' recent claims, and any foreign uncommitted changes before you write or commit. `merge=union` was deliberately confined to `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`: the other shared logs (DECISION_LOG/GG_LOG/TODO_NEXT/MEMORY_BANK) contain in-place edits where union merge would silently retain both stale and new lines, so they are protected by isolation + path-scoped commits + the diff-against-`HEAD` rule instead. **Protocol wiring confirmed done:** CLAUDE.md's Step 0 (lines 160-181) now mandates worktree-first launch via `scripts/new_session_worktree.sh`, requires the atomic claim via `scripts/claim_session.sh`, and Hard Rule 10 was strengthened to reference both; `.claude/commands/session-start.md` exists and wires Step 0b into one command. A session following CLAUDE.md as written today is instructed to launch isolated. See DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-08 (GG-101 correction row). |
| Status | **RESOLVED — tooling shipped 2026-07-07/08 (scripts + hook + gitattributes) and protocol wiring confirmed live in CLAUDE.md Step 0 + `.claude/commands/session-start.md`.** The isolation guarantee binds by default: the mandatory session-start sequence instructs sessions to launch via `new_session_worktree.sh` before doing other work. |

### GG-102 — deploy.yml dirty-tree guard regressed to blocking on any untracked file (GG-073 whack-a-mole, plus 3 tracked files that should never have been tracked)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-102 |
| Category | Incremental architecture — a guard's implementation drifted from its stated invariant as the system it protects grew. |
| What was missed | `deploy.yml`'s dirty-tree guard (built for T-171) ran `git status --porcelain` with no `--untracked-files` flag, so it aborted on **any** untracked file on the VPS, not just conflicting tracked ones. Every new pipeline component that writes a new `exp_output/<EXP-ID>/` dir, cache, or scratch file re-triggers the failure — GG-073 "fixed" this 2026-06-24 by gitignoring the specific offending paths, but that is whack-a-mole against an open-ended set of future output paths, not a fix to the guard's logic, and it regressed again by 2026-07-08 (email trigger: run `28908956151`, failed in 7s). Separately, once the guard was corrected to `--untracked-files=no`, it surfaced a second, real, independent bug: 3 files (`exp_output/EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-ARITH-MLB-01_results.json`, `exp_output/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01_results.json`, `exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/band_monitor_results.json`) were git-tracked despite being rewritten by live VPS processes (the weekly `run_band_monitor.py --ci` cron for the third; ad-hoc reruns for the first two) — the exact `paper_portfolio.json`/T-171 shape, just never caught for these specific paths. |
| When it should have been caught | GG-073's 2026-06-24 fix should have addressed the guard's root logic (block on tracked-file drift only) instead of enumerating specific untracked paths to ignore; the untracked-files gap was structural and guaranteed to resurface with the next new pipeline component. The 3 mistracked results files should have been caught whenever they were first added to git — the same T-171 pattern (a file periodically rewritten by a running process should never be tracked) was already established precedent by the time these were committed. |
| Phases it survived | Live since 2026-06-24 (GG-073's original fix); regressed silently until the 2026-07-08 failed deploy email surfaced it. The 3 mistracked results files survived from their original commits (EXP-ARITH-MLB-01/EXP-FLOW-MLB-01 added ~2026-07-05/06, band_monitor_results.json added 2026-07-08 alongside T-255/T-257) through several deploy runs before the guard's own fix exposed them. |
| Why it survived | The guard's failure mode (deploy fails) is loud but its *cause* (untracked-vs-tracked distinction) was never inspected — GG-073's fix treated symptoms (specific paths) rather than the check's flag semantics. The 3 results files were never flagged because the guard itself was broken in a way that masked them (it failed on unrelated untracked scratch files first, so the deploy job never got far enough to detect the tracked-file drift underneath). |
| Impact | Deploy pipeline was fully blocked (100% failure) from the regression until this fix landed — no code changes could reach the VPS via the automated path during that window. Manual `git pull` on the VPS remained available as a workaround (not exercised here) but defeats the purpose of the automated deploy gate. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-08, operator-forwarded GitHub Actions failure email (run `28908956151`), root-caused by a prior sandbox session (T-261 claim in SESSION_CLAIMS.md) which drafted but did not commit the fix; completed and pushed this session after resolving an index.lock false alarm (stale lock from the sandbox's own interrupted commit, confirmed via `ps`/`lsof` no live git process) and two live `gh workflow run` verification cycles. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-08/09.** (1) `deploy.yml`'s guard changed to `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` (blocks only on modified tracked files, the actual T-171 invariant) and `git pull` switched to `--ff-only` for a cleaner failure mode — commit `41e46b2`. (2) The 3 live-regenerated results files untracked via `git rm --cached` + `.gitignore` entries, scoped to exactly those 3 paths (every other `exp_output/**/*_results.json` file is a one-time, non-regenerated snapshot per a commit-count check across all 26 tracked results files, and stays tracked as an audit trail) — commit `9b06a31` (note: the first attempt, `b918f4b`, was a no-op due to `git commit <pathspec>` re-staging the files' on-disk content and silently undoing the preceding `git rm --cached`; caught via `git ls-tree HEAD` verification before declaring done, corrected in the same session). VPS-side reconciliation (backup live content → `git checkout --` to clean → `git pull --ff-only` → restore live content as now-untracked) preserved all 3 files' current data byte-for-byte (verified via `md5sum` before/after). End-to-end verified via `gh workflow run deploy.yml` → run `28984294745` completed `success`, confirmed the guard passed and `git pull` executed (not just skipped). |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-09. |

### GG-103 — `.last_session_checkpoint` is a single unprotected file, not concurrency-safe like `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-103 |
| Category | New infra introduced without the concurrency protection its sibling mechanisms already have (GG-101/GG-102 pattern: a new file/mechanism added under time pressure, protected against the single-session case, not audited against the concurrent-session case at introduction time). |
| What was missed | T-300 (A3 residual, 2026-07-09) wired `04_EXECUTION/.last_session_checkpoint` — a single-line file holding one git SHA — into `session-start.md` (read) and `session-end.md` (overwrite) so `session_delta.sh` can diff since the last checkpoint instead of a full Tier 1 read. Unlike `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`, this file has no `.gitattributes` `merge=union` rule and is not append-only — it is a full overwrite on every session-end. If two sessions are concurrently active in separate worktrees (the GG-101-mandated pattern) and both reach session-end before either pushes, whichever pushes second silently overwrites the first session's checkpoint update with its own HEAD SHA — not a merge conflict, since git resolves single-line-file overwrites cleanly. This does not corrupt data (worst case: the "losing" session's checkpoint-to-HEAD span is simply skipped by the next session's delta, which then must rely on Hard Rule 10's fresh-diff/re-read discipline to still catch it) but it is a silent staleness gap the mechanism itself cannot detect. |
| When it should have been caught | At A3-residual design time (this session) — the concurrent-session isolation work (GG-101, `scripts/claim_session.sh`, `.gitattributes` `merge=union` on `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`) was already-known precedent for exactly this class of file when `.last_session_checkpoint` was created a few hours earlier in this same session; the asymmetry wasn't noticed until this A5 close-out review. |
| Phases it survived | N/A — new in this session (created 2026-07-09, wired into session-start/session-end same session); has not yet been exercised under real concurrent load. |
| Why it survived | The file was designed to solve a single-session sequencing problem (A3's stated residual: "nothing invokes `session_delta.sh` automatically yet") and the concurrency angle wasn't part of that problem statement — it was found only by deliberately re-applying the adversarial "are we compromised" lens to newly-written infra before closing out the window, not by any test or review step that would have caught it otherwise. |
| Impact | Zero capital risk, zero live-system risk — this is session-bootstrap tooling only, not part of C1-C8. Worst case is a slightly stale delta on the affected session's next start, fully recoverable via Hard Rule 10 (which already governs every write regardless of retrieval path) or a manual full read. Not a blocker to anything; logged so it isn't silently forgotten. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-09, T-300/Window A A5 close-out review (this session), immediately after wiring the checkpoint file into session-start/session-end. |
| Resolution | OPEN — no fix implemented yet. Candidate fix (not yet built): apply the same `merge=union` `.gitattributes` treatment used for `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`, reformatted as an append-only log (`checkpoint<TAB>timestamp<TAB>session-slug` rows) with session-start reading the last line rather than the whole-file content — mirrors `claim_session.sh`'s already-proven pattern instead of inventing a new one. Deferred rather than built now because it is zero-capital-risk and Hard Rule 10 already covers the failure mode; register a T-NNN if/when Window A resumes or another session picks this up. |
| Status | OPEN. |

### GG-109 — git worktree isolation (GG-101/T-259) covers git operations only; Python import resolution can silently fall through to the main tree

**Renumbered from GG-103 during Window A/Window B merge reconciliation (2026-07-09/10)** — Window A independently registered its own GG-103 (`.last_session_checkpoint` concurrency gap, above) in `origin/main` before this branch merged; both were assigned the same next-available ID by their respective isolated sessions, a direct instance of the numbering-race class of problem GG-101/`claim_session.sh` exists to prevent for T-/GG-/ADR- IDs but did not fully close (the claim file reserves an ID *range*, not a live-synchronized counter). Content below is unchanged from Window B's original registration; only the ID changed. All in-file cross-references (`TODO_NEXT.md` T-282, `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-09 B4 entry, `SESSION_HANDOFF_20260709_WindowB_B4partial.md`, `tests/test_t280_cross_implementation_invariants.py`'s guard comment) are being updated to GG-109 as part of this same reconciliation. **Second renumbering, merge-time (2026-07-11):** this gap's ID (GG-104, assigned during the Window A/Window B reconciliation above) collided with an independently-assigned, unrelated GG-104 already merged to `main` from the Window A/T-320-T-321 track (`deploy.yml` never restarts persistent systemd services) — a second, live recurrence of the exact numbering-race class GG-101 targets, this time surfaced at merge time rather than pre-emptively. Renumbered to **GG-109** throughout (`GOVERNANCE_GAP_LOG.md`, `DECISION_LOG.md`, session handoffs, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_t280_cross_implementation_invariants.py`) as part of this merge; content unchanged, ID only. Origin/main's own, unrelated GG-104 (deploy.yml gap) is preserved below, untouched.

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-109 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — a safety mechanism (worktree isolation) was assumed to cover import resolution because it covers everything else about a session's working state, but it operates at the git layer, not the interpreter layer. |
| What was missed | All worktrees share one global Python 3.11 environment. `prediction_market_bot` is bound via a single static `.pth` file in that environment's site-packages (`/usr/local/opt/python@3.11/.../site-packages/*.pth`, literal contents: the **main tree's** absolute `src/` path). `scripts/new_session_worktree.sh` creates a git worktree + branch only — no worktree-local venv, no editable reinstall, no `PYTHONPATH` export. Once `prediction_market_bot` is bound in `sys.modules` (whichever tree's `src/` wins the race for the first import in a given process), every subsequent bare `from prediction_market_bot...` import in that process resolves against that binding's `__path__`, regardless of the importing file's own location. Some test files guard against this explicitly (`tests/test_c4_risk.py`: `sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).parent.parent / "src"))` before its `prediction_market_bot` import); others do not. `tests/test_t280_cross_implementation_invariants.py` (this session's own B2 output) was one of the latter — it happened to work in full-suite runs only because `test_c4_risk.py` collects alphabetically first and wins the binding race, not because the file was actually worktree-safe. |
| When it should have been caught | At B2 (this session), when `test_t280_cross_implementation_invariants.py` was first written — it should have followed the `test_c4_risk.py` guard pattern from the start. More structurally, at GG-101's original resolution (2026-07-08): worktree isolation was verified for git operations (stash/reset/checkout/clean) but never verified for Python import resolution, which is a distinct isolation boundary. |
| Phases it survived | Introduced with `test_t280_cross_implementation_invariants.py` in B2 this session; not caught until B4 (same session) when a new file created only in the worktree (`src/prediction_market_bot/analytics/calibration_diagnostics.py`) broke the implicit assumption that main-tree and worktree copies of every imported module were byte-identical. |
| Why it survived | The full-suite run ("2518 passed, 30 skipped") never surfaced it because collection order (alphabetical) happened to always bind `prediction_market_bot` via a guarded file (`test_c4_risk.py`, sorts early) before any unguarded file needed it — so the suite's green result was real for that run, but fragile: it depended on collection order and on main-tree/worktree byte-identity for every unguarded file's dependencies, not on an actual isolation guarantee. Standalone/subset runs of an unguarded file are the failure mode, and B4's `ModuleNotFoundError` on a worktree-only new module is what exposed it. |
| Impact | Any worktree session's standalone or partial test runs touching an unguarded file could have been silently validating **main-tree** code instead of the worktree's actual (possibly diverged, possibly uncommitted) code, without any error — when the two trees' relevant files happen to be byte-identical, which is the common case except exactly when it matters most (mid-session, uncommitted worktree-only changes). No evidence found this session that it caused a false-positive test result to date (the one concrete case found, B4, failed loudly with `ModuleNotFoundError` rather than silently passing wrong code) — but the silent-pass case is real and unproven-absent for prior worktree sessions. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-09, this session (WINDOW B / T-280 / B4), while debugging a `ModuleNotFoundError` after lifting `calibration_diagnostics.py` into a new worktree-only module; root-caused via two throwaway probe files (`/tmp/test_which_src.py`, `tests/test_zzz_which_src_probe.py`, both deleted after use) plus a `risk.py` main-vs-worktree diff confirming a real committed divergence (`_MIP_EPSILON`, B1) that an unguarded import would have silently missed. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-10 (T-282).** Audited every file under `tests/` (70 files) for a bare `prediction_market_bot` import: 25 already carried the `test_c4_risk.py`-style `sys.path.insert` guard, **31 did not** — a materially larger surface than the single file found in B4, confirming this was a real systemic gap, not a one-off. Rather than patching 31 files individually, added `pythonpath = ["src"]` to `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` in `pyproject.toml` — pytest's native (core, no plugin) mechanism for prepending a rootdir-relative path to `sys.path` before test collection begins. Because `pythonpath` resolves relative to the config file's own directory, each worktree's own `pyproject.toml` makes its own `src/` win the import race unconditionally, closing the gap for all 31 previously-unguarded files (and making the 25 existing per-file guards redundant but harmless — left in place, not removed). Verified the fix is real, not just plausible: wrote a throwaway probe test (`tests/test_probe_gg104.py`, deleted after use) asserting `risk._MIP_EPSILON` (a confirmed worktree-only symbol, absent from the main tree per B1 — the same divergence class GG-109's original discovery hinged on) is present when imported via a bare `import prediction_market_bot`, with no local guard; passed, and printed the resolved module path as the worktree's own `src/`, not the main tree's. Full suite re-run post-fix: 2,541 passed / 30 skipped / 0 failed (identical to the pre-fix count — confirms the fix closes a real risk without changing any test's actual behavior, i.e. no prior false-positive was masked by it). |
| Status | RESOLVED. |

### GG-104 — `deploy.yml` never restarts persistent systemd services; code changes to `t4-mlb`/`pmbot-web-dashboard` silently do not take effect until manually caught

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-104 |
| Category | Deploy-pipeline gap — `deploy.yml` (T-171/GG-073/GG-102) only runs `git pull --ff-only`; it never restarts any systemd service. Cron-invoked scripts (T1/T2/T3/T4-NBA MLB pipelines — fresh process per cron fire, confirmed via `crontab -l` on the VPS) pick up new code automatically on next fire, but `t4-mlb.service` and `pmbot-web-dashboard.service` are long-running processes that import their modules once at startup and keep running the in-memory version indefinitely. |
| What was missed | Discovered while closing out T-320/T-321 (this session, 2026-07-10): after merging and deploying the `logic_epoch` write-path fix (`paper_trader.py`), `pmbot-web-dashboard`'s `/api/data` endpoint still returned `current_logic_epoch: None` post-deploy — the running process had been up since 2026-07-08, well before the merge. Checked `t4-mlb.service` next (`systemctl show --property=ActiveEnterTimestamp` = 2026-07-08 06:01 UTC, vs. deploy at 2026-07-10 15:56 UTC) and found the same: the live T4M-PROJ evaluator was running stale `paper_trader.py`, meaning new T4M-PROJ positions opened between the merge and the discovery would NOT have had `logic_epoch`/`approval_commit` stamped — directly undermining the T-320 exit criterion ("every newly opened position carries logic_epoch") for the one band where it matters most (T4M-PROJ is the highest-volume band under CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9). |
| When it should have been caught | At `deploy.yml`'s original authorship (T-171) or at either service's `systemd` unit creation — any deploy pipeline serving a long-running process needs an explicit restart step; this is a well-known class of gap (the "deploy succeeded but old code is still running" bug), not specific to this project. |
| Phases it survived | Every deploy since `t4-mlb.service`/`pmbot-web-dashboard.service` were first installed as systemd units — an unknown but plausibly large number of merged fixes to `paper_trader.py`, `t4_mlb_execution_engine.py`, or `web_dashboard.py` may have silently not taken effect on the live T4M-PROJ evaluator until the next incidental manual/crash restart. Not retroactively auditable without correlating each fix's merge timestamp against `t4-mlb.service`'s restart history — out of scope for this entry, but a real historical-data-quality question worth being aware of if a future session is auditing T4M-PROJ bet quality bug-by-bug. |
| Why it survived | `deploy.yml`'s "Verify HEAD" step only checks `git log -1 --oneline` on the VPS filesystem — it confirms the *code on disk* updated, not that the *running processes* are executing it. No prior session's deploy verification distinguished those two things. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH for T4M-PROJ specifically (highest-volume, ACTIVE band): any write-path fix merged to `paper_trader.py`/`t4_mlb_execution_engine.py` is silently inert on live T4M-PROJ evaluation until the next `t4-mlb.service` restart (manual, crash, or VPS reboot) — with no error, warning, or deploy-log signal that this happened. LOW for `pmbot-web-dashboard` (read-only monitoring surface, no capital/trading consequence, but can show stale/misleading state to the operator). Zero impact for T1/T2/T3/T4-NBA (fresh cron process each run). |
| Discovery | 2026-07-10, this session, T-320/T-321 close-out verification — caught by directly curling `pmbot-web-dashboard`'s `/api/data` and independently checking `t4-mlb.service`'s `ActiveEnterTimestamp` against the deploy timestamp, rather than assuming "deploy succeeded" meant "new code is live everywhere." |
| Resolution | **Immediate/manual (2026-07-10 session):** `systemctl restart t4-mlb` and `systemctl restart pmbot-web-dashboard` run directly, confirmed both processes now report current state. **Permanent fix (T-328, this session, 2026-07-11):** `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` now has an unconditional `systemctl restart t4-mlb pmbot-web-dashboard` step immediately after `git pull --ff-only`, and "Verify HEAD" was extended to read each service's `ActiveEnterTimestamp` and fail the workflow (exit 1) if either timestamp is more than 2 minutes older than the pull — so a future silent-staleness case now fails the deploy pipeline itself instead of requiring manual `curl`/`journalctl` inspection to catch. Restarting `t4-mlb` mid-deploy is safe by existing precedent (position state persists to `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json`, not in-memory-only; the service has been manually restarted repeatedly before, e.g. 3x in short succession on 2026-07-08, with no reported ill effect) — a 45s-cycle evaluator resuming after a few seconds of downtime is within its own designed staleness tolerance. Not yet exercised against a real push (verification is the deploy that lands this very commit) — treat as newly-live, not battle-tested, until at least one real deploy cycle confirms the freshness check passes end-to-end. |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-07-11, T-328) — permanent fix implemented; first live validation is the deploy that ships this change. |

---

### GG-105 — T4M-PROJ (ACTIVE, highest-volume band) shows a statistically significant, tercile-monotonic ANTI-PREDICTIVE relationship between its own computed edge and realized win rate, on clean data (Workstream B / T-322/T-323/T-324 discovery)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-105 |
| Category | Strategic/edge-sign finding — not a code bug, not a write-path contamination artifact (both ruled out; see Resolution). A live signal-quality question about the T4M-PROJ model itself. |
| What was missed | GG-004's commercial-validation narrative (most recently updated 2026-07-10 re: `clean_n`/`raw_n`) tracks T4M-PROJ's positive aggregate EV (+$0.047/bet on clean_n=271) as directionally encouraging, but has never separately checked whether the band's own edge computation *rank-orders* win probability correctly. It does not: `scripts/run_band_monitor.py` (T-322), run on `clean_n` only (CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH=3, GG-077/GG-087 contamination excluded), found T4M-PROJ's edge-monotonicity Fisher test significant at p=0.024 (252 positive-edge bets @ 63.9% win vs. 19 negative-edge @ 89.5% win, odds ratio 0.208) — and, more importantly, a tercile breakdown across three balanced ~90-count bins shows a clean monotonic decline (74.4%→64.4%→58.2% win rate as edge magnitude rises from tercile 1→3), ruling out the obvious thin-cell-noise objection to the Fisher result alone. T1, by contrast, is INCONCLUSIVE (not negative) on the same clean-data methodology — its Fisher test is structurally near-degenerate (64:1 positive:negative edge split, since T1 only bets on computed-positive edge by construction) and its raw aggregate EV/win-rate are positive. Full analysis: `05_RESEARCH_EXPERIMENTS/RESULTS_SUMMARIES/T324_T1_VS_T4_DIVERGENCE_MEMO.md`. |
| When it should have been caught | Ongoing — this is the first session the edge-monotonicity + tercile methodology has been run specifically against `clean_n` (post-GG-077/GG-087 write-path fixes) per band. Prior runs (e.g. GG-100) used the pool-level, not band-separated, book and were also confounded by the since-fixed sign bug. |
| Phases it survived | T4M-PROJ has been ACTIVE and accumulating clean_n since the epoch-3 boundary (2026-07-06/07); this pattern was present in the data the entire time but not measured this way until T-322's clean-only band monitor existed. |
| Why it survived | GG-004's tracked metric (aggregate EV/win-rate) is a coarser signal than edge-rank-ordering; a band can show positive aggregate EV while its edge computation actively anti-predicts within that same positive-EV population. No prior monitor decomposed by edge tercile on a clean, band-separated, correctly-signed cohort. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH, capital-track relevant. T4M-PROJ is the sole band with real live-poll evaluation cadence (CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 9, highest-volume design point) and the band closest to further gate progress. If Kelly sizing scales with computed edge magnitude (per ADR-0010/ADR-0039 design), this anti-predictive relationship — if it persists — means the band is currently sizing up into its statistically worst bets, which could be masking a materially worse size-weighted P&L picture than the unweighted aggregate (+$0.047/bet) suggests. Not yet a stop-work-level finding: n=271 is below T-323's own computed n*=836 for 95%-power detection of the *aggregate* edge>0/=0 question (a related but distinct question from this rank-ordering finding), and the clean epoch is only ~4 days old. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-10, this session (Workstream B, T-322/T-323/T-324), via `scripts/run_band_monitor.py`'s Fisher test plus a tercile breakdown added during T-324's investigation to check whether the Fisher result was thin-cell-dependent — it is not. |
| Resolution | Not yet resolved — this entry registers the finding per the project's standing invariant (gap registered independently of any fix). T-324's memo recommends three diagnostic steps: (1) a size-weighted re-run to check whether Kelly sizing amplifies the inversion's P&L impact; (2) segmenting the tercile finding by live game state (inning, score differential, ab_remaining); (3) re-running as clean_n grows past its immature-cadence window (<14 days). **Diagnostic (1) run this session (T-329, `scripts/run_t329_size_weighted_diag.py`, 2026-07-11 — same-day continuation, clean_n still n=271, tercile split and win rates reproduce T-324's table exactly as a correctness check).** Result is the *opposite* of the compounding-risk hypothesis this diagnostic was built to test: avg stake *falls* monotonically with edge tercile ($0.69→$0.57→$0.47, not rises), and $-weighted return *rises* monotonically with edge tercile (+3.91%→+6.32%→+16.59% pnl/staked) even as win rate falls (74.7%→64.4%→57.8%) — overall T4M-PROJ clean_n cohort returns +8.12% pnl/staked ($12.64 on $155.69 staked), well above what the unweighted EV/bet figure (+$0.047/bet) alone would suggest, and the largest-edge tercile is the *most* profitable per dollar staked despite having the lowest win rate. Not driven by a single outlier (tercile 3's $6.95 total P&L is spread across 90 bets averaging ~$0.47 stake each). **This rules out candidate (b) (sizing artifact / compounding risk) as this session's read of GG-105's Impact field describes it** — T4M-PROJ is not currently sizing up into its statistically worst bets; if anything the opposite. Plausible mechanical explanation (not yet verified): larger fee-net-edge readings likely coincide with more extreme MIP price points, where a correctly-functioning edge/payout relationship means a lower win probability can still carry a larger expected dollar return — i.e. this may be confirming the edge formula tracks $-EV correctly even though it does not rank-order win-rate correctly, which are different properties. **This diagnostic does not touch candidate (a) (real signal / game-state) at all** — the mechanistic question of *why* edge magnitude inversely ranks win rate (T4's core latency thesis: are large edges concentrating in structurally noisy game states rather than genuine market-latency situations?) remains fully open and is now the highest-value remaining diagnostic (T-324 recommendation (2), unscoped/unbuilt — needs live game-state fields joined into the resolved ledger, not yet done). Diagnostic (3) (re-run as clean_n grows) remains a standing, passive re-check, not a discrete build. Two known contamination boundaries (GG-077, GG-087) were explicitly ruled out as the cause — this analysis used `clean_n` only and independently recomputed edge via the audited `compute_fee_net_edge()` helper rather than trusting any stored field, the same defense-in-depth GG-100 established. **Do not fold this finding silently into GG-004's existing DIRECTIONAL_POSITIVE narrative** — it is a materially different and more specific concern (edge rank-ordering, not sample size) and should be surfaced at the next G6/gate discussion directly, now including this session's size-weighted result. **Diagnostic (2) run this session (T-332, `scripts/run_t332_gamestate_diag.py`, 2026-07-11 — same-day continuation, clean_n still n=271).** Method: joined every clean_n resolved position to its originating live-poll evaluation cycle in `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` via `forecast_id`'s embedded `cycle_id` (validated 271/271, 100% join rate, 0 key collisions across 19,275 cycle-log rows), then re-ran the T-324 tercile/Fisher methodology inside buckets of `inning` (early 1-3 / mid 4-6 / late 7+) and `ab_remaining` (0 / 1 / 2+) — the two of the memo's three proposed dimensions this data actually supports (see data-gap note below). Result: the inversion is **not concentrated in a single game-state slice**. Both buckets large enough to reach `MIN_N=30` and carry the bulk of the cohort reproduce ANTI-PREDICTIVE independently — `inning=early_1-3` (n=136, tercile win rates 76.1%→53.3%→60.0%) and `ab_remaining=2+` (n=213, the majority bucket, tercile win rates 78.9%→62.0%→57.7%). The `mid_4-6` (n=133) and `ab_remaining=1` (n=58) buckets are directionally the same (win rate falls from tercile 1 to tercile 3 in both) but do not clear the Fisher significance bar on their own — INCONCLUSIVE, not a reversal. `late_7+` is UNDERPOWERED (n=2, GG-098 discipline: descriptive only, no verdict). Zero buckets returned MONOTONIC (i.e., zero buckets showed edge correctly rank-ordering win rate). Point-biserial correlations of each game-state variable against edge magnitude and win outcome were all weak (|r|≤0.13) — no single game-state variable individually explains meaningful variance in either. **Two new data-quality findings surfaced in the process, not previously logged:** (i) `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` — T4M-PROJ's own live-poll trail — carries no score-differential field at all (only `inning`, `ab_remaining`, `ab_so_far`, `h_so_far`, `batting_slot`, `ba_l10`); CLAUDE.md documents a `score_diff`-never-wired gap for T4M-FLOW specifically, this confirms the identical gap exists in T4M-PROJ's actual schema too, so "score differential" — one of the memo's three proposed segmentation dimensions — could not be tested at all, by data absence rather than by choice. (ii) `batting_slot` is populated as `0` in 19,202/19,275 (99.6%) of all cycle-log rows system-wide (checked outside the clean_n join, across the full log) — effectively a dead/near-constant field in current production logging, not a usable signal; this is why its correlation computed as `nan` (zero variance) rather than a small number. **Conclusion: candidates (a) game-state noise and (b) sizing artifact (T-329) are both now weighed against as full explanations** — the inversion reproduces uniformly enough across the analyzable game-state buckets that it does not look like a structural noise source specific to one slice. This leaves the edge computation itself (T4M-PROJ's `p_t4` / MIP / fee-net-edge formula, or an upstream feature feeding it) as the leading unexplained factor, and is the natural focus for diagnostic (3) or a fresh, targeted follow-on once clean_n grows past its immature-cadence window. Full script + raw JSON: `scripts/run_t332_gamestate_diag.py`, `exp_output/T332-GAMESTATE-DIAG/gamestate_diag_results.json`. **T-333 Component B (edge-formula audit) run this session (2026-07-11, `scripts/run_t333_edgeformula_diag.py`, clean_n grown to n=338 from T-332's n=271 — natural growth, `logic_epoch` distribution `{1: 122, 2: 122, 3: 338}`, `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH=3`).** Joined the clean_n cohort to its cycle-log inputs (338/338, 100% join rate, reusing T-332's `forecast_id`→`cycle_id` infrastructure verbatim) and tested all five H1-H5 hypotheses from T-333's scope doc. **H2 (confirmed by direct code read, no backtest needed):** `ctx["p_t1"]` (line 343) feeds only `store.stage_order()` (line ~1088) for audit persistence; it never appears in `p_hits_gte_threshold()` or the `gross_edge`/`net_edge` computation (lines 945-954) — T4 structurally discards T1's calibrated estimate at exactly the step GG-105 finds the inversion. **H1 (suggestive, not conclusive):** tercile 3 (highest edge, n=112) has lower mean `ba_l10` (0.224, stdev 0.089) than terciles 1/2 (~0.255-0.258, stdev 0.06-0.08); `corr(|ba_l10-0.25|, edge)=+0.195`, `corr(|ba_l10-0.25|, win)=+0.115`, `corr(ba_l10, edge)=-0.166` — weak-to-moderate, directionally consistent with H1's noisy-extreme-`ba_l10` mechanism but not independently conclusive. **H3 (weighs against as primary driver):** default-fill (`ba_l10==0.25` exact) is rare, 8/338 (2.4%) overall, below `MIN_N=30` in every tercile subgroup — GG-098 discipline: descriptive only, no verdict, but too rare to be the main driver of a pattern spanning all 338 rows. **H4 (suggestive, mechanistically linked to H1):** `batting_slot==0` confirmed near-universal in the clean_n cohort too (97.9%, consistent with T-332's system-wide 99.6%); `ab_remaining` distribution shifts modestly across terciles (tercile 3 has proportionally more `ab_remaining=3` rows, 54/112 vs 33/113 in tercile 1) — a plausible compounding link with H1 (more remaining at-bats amplifies `ba_l10` sampling noise in the binomial tail calculation) that has not yet been formally tested via an interaction model. **H5 (headline finding — a real methodological gap in the analysis tooling, not the live system):** `e17.compute_fee_net_edge()` — the function backing every GG-105 diagnostic's `_fne` field to date via `bm.load_t4_ledger()` (T-324, T-329, T-332, and this task's own backtest-formula terciles) — computes only `model_p - mip - kalshi_fee(mip)`, with **no vig term at all**. The live decision path (`run_t4_mlb_live.py:952-954`) DOES subtract vig (`gross_edge = p_t4 - mip - vig`) before the fee. Every GG-105 result to date was computed on a formula narrower than what the live system actually gates/sizes on. This audit re-derived a direction-aware "live-parity" edge — `model_p - mip - vig - fee` (YES) / `mip - model_p + vig - fee` (NO), mirroring `compute_fee_net_edge()`'s own YES/NO sign convention and `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s actual `gross_edge`→`directional_gross_edge` flip logic exactly (vig's sign flips with direction; fee is always subtracted, never flipped) — after self-catching and fixing a first-draft implementation bug that applied `-vig` unconditionally regardless of direction (transparency note: caught before this write-up by re-deriving the sign convention directly from the live code rather than trusting the initial draft). **A structural consequence, not a bug:** because every resolved position was, by definition, live-gated on this exact vig-inclusive formula clearing a positive threshold, the corrected live-parity edge is non-negative for every one of the 338 rows (`n_neg=0`) — there is no negative-edge population in a live-execution-only ledger to run the audited pos/neg-split Fisher test against. `e17.edge_monotonicity()` correctly returns `INCONCLUSIVE` (p=1.0) on this input rather than forcing a verdict on a degenerate split (GG-098 discipline working as designed) — the test's pos/neg-split design assumes a backtested candidate universe including both accepted and rejected edges, which a live-gated resolved-only ledger structurally cannot supply. The backtest formula (no vig) remains the only one of the two for which this Fisher test is meaningfully applicable here, and its verdict is unchanged and reconfirmed: **ANTI-PREDICTIVE, p=0.0563** (n_pos=317/n_neg=21, wr_pos=0.637/wr_neg=0.857 — essentially T-324/T-329/T-332's same figures, the larger n=338 vs their n=271 being natural clean_n growth, not a re-derivation difference). Descriptively (not Fisher-tested, since the pos/neg split doesn't apply to this formula), the vig-inclusive live-parity tercile win rates still decline monotonically as edge rises — 69.0%→63.7%→62.5% (n=113/113/112) — the same qualitative direction as the backtest formula's 75.2%→62.8%→57.1% split, just compressed; 35.2% of positions (119/338) change tercile-membership between the two sorts, so the two formulas are not interchangeable and any future GG-105 diagnostic should state explicitly which one it used. **Conclusion: T-333 Component B does not overturn GG-105's anti-predictive finding — it reconfirms it under both the audited backtest formula (statistically) and the corrected live-parity formula (descriptively)**, while surfacing one real tooling gap (recommend `e17.compute_fee_net_edge()` be extended to accept a vig parameter in a future task — a tooling-correctness fix, not a capital-affecting change, so it doesn't require a DS sign-off/ADR under Hard Rule 3, but per T-333's own scope §2.5 no code change is made in this task) and confirming H2 structurally while leaving H1+H4 jointly (not individually conclusive) as the leading open mechanistic hypothesis for a future targeted diagnostic. Full script + raw JSON: `scripts/run_t333_edgeformula_diag.py`, `exp_output/T333-EDGEFORMULA-DIAG/edgeformula_diag_results.json`. **T-340 (N-05) checked this session (2026-07-11, Window 2, ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening_v2.md) — Component A (diagnostic (3), tercile/Fisher re-run at calendar maturity) remains gated, not yet met.** `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH` (epoch 3) began 2026-07-05 22:45:38 UTC; live check this session (`date -u` → 2026-07-11 23:00:42 UTC) puts epoch age at ~6.0 days against the ~14-day maturity threshold this diagnostic requires — corroborated independently by `scripts/run_power_calc.py`'s own `cadence_immature=true` flag on T4M-PROJ (T-338, same session, clean_n=338/n*=787). No new diagnostic run this pass; standing passive re-check per this entry's own established pattern, not a discrete build. Re-check target: ~2026-07-19/20. See `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-11 T-340 entry. |
| Status | OPEN — diagnostics (1), (2), and Component B of the T-333 follow-on are complete; candidates (a) game-state, (b) sizing artifact, and the H1-H5 edge-formula hypotheses have all been tested. **Net result: the anti-predictive finding is robust** — it survives game-state segmentation (T-332), is not a sizing artifact (T-329), is not explained by default-fill masking (H3) or by the offline analysis formula's vig omission (H5 — the live formula already correctly includes vig; the gap was in the analysis tooling only, and the pattern persists under the corrected formula too). The leading open explanation is a joint H1 (noisy `ba_l10` as a fixed per-AB Bernoulli parameter) + H4 (`ab_remaining` estimator compounding that noise) mechanism, not yet formally tested via an interaction model, plus H2's structural fact that T1's calibrated estimate is discarded rather than blended in. **Component A** (diagnostic (3), re-run tercile/Fisher methodology once clean_n crosses ~14 days epoch age or ~n=540) remains a standing watch item, not yet triggered (currently n=338, epoch age ~5-6 days as of this update). **Any future work escalating H1/H2/H4 into an actual live-formula change (e.g., blending `p_t1` into `p_t4`, shrinking `ba_l10` toward a prior) requires a fresh, explicit DS sign-off and a superseding ADR per Hard Rule 3 / ADR-0039 Tier B precedent — this task authorized investigation only, per T-333's own scope.** Not remediated (by design — no remediation was in scope). **T-341 confirmation (2026-07-11, separate worktree, C7-wiring task):** end-to-end dry run of the newly-built `scripts/run_band_monitor.py` C7 wiring against real VPS data independently reproduced this exact finding (n=338, Fisher p=0.0563, `winrate_pos=0.637`/`winrate_neg=0.857`, calibration `b=0.729` CI `[0.52, 0.985]` excludes 1.0) — this was momentarily mis-registered as a new gap (draft GG-111) before being traced back to this entry and corrected; noted here so a future session doesn't repeat the duplicate-registration mistake. Two genuinely new facts from this pass: (i) the local/VPS `t4_mlb_resolved_ledger.jsonl` artifact was stale (dated 2026-07-09, predating the T-320 backfill), initially showing a false `clean_n=0`, until rebuilt via `build_t4_resolved_ledger.py` — the ledger-build step is not yet on any freshness check, so a future session pulling it without rebuilding could silently see 0 instead of 338 and wrongly conclude the check is clean; (ii) GG-105's anti-predictive verdict now fires as a live `band_monitor_anti_predictive_alarm`/`band_monitor_calibration_alarm` WARNING-severity C7 audit event on every scheduled run of the T-255/T-322 cron job (`20 14 * * 1`) going forward, i.e. this finding is no longer something a session has to remember to re-check by hand — C7 will surface it automatically (warn-only, GG-098 discipline, no auto-halt) for as long as it persists. **T-344 decomposition (2026-07-12, same-day continuation, fresh VPS pull, clean_n grown to n=412 from T-341's n=338 — natural growth) formally decomposes the four candidate mechanisms named in this task's scope (a real signal-inversion, b fee/edge-frame artifact, c flat-sizing artifact, d model miscalibration) against current data, per explicit instruction not to wait for further sample.** Reconfirms the pattern at larger n and *sharper* significance (`run_band_monitor.py` this session: fisher_p=0.0464 vs T-341's 0.0563, n_pos=389/n_neg=23, calibration b=0.600 CI [0.419,0.812] excludes 1.0). **New headline finding: a real, previously-undiscovered vig sign-flip bug in `run_t4_mlb_live.py`'s NO-direction gating math, escalated as its own gap, GG-113 (OPEN, see below)** — distinct from T-333's H5 (a tooling-parity question about whether the *offline analysis tool* matches the live formula) because this is a new question about whether the *live formula itself* is economically correct, tested against the codebase's own pre-existing vig convention in `run_exp017.py`. **Critically, correcting GG-113's bug does NOT resolve GG-105 — it makes the anti-predictive pattern more significant** (Fisher p: 0.046→0.0094 under the corrected symmetric-vig formula, n_neg growing from 23→137 as previously-hidden negative-true-edge NO bets are revealed) — this rules out mechanism (b) as GG-105's cause, even though the underlying bug is real and independently actionable via GG-113. Compositional (YES/NO mix) artifact also ruled out this session: the tercile-monotonic decay reproduces within the NO-only subgroup alone (n=337, 82% of book) under the corrected formula (70.5%→68.8%→61.1%), so this is not merely a base-rate mix effect. **Verdict as of T-344: (a) ruled out as primary driver (win rate stays >50% at every tercile — a decay/overconfidence signature, not a sign-inversion); (b) ruled out as cause despite a real bug found (GG-113); (c) ruled out (T-329, reconfirmed orthogonally); (d) model miscalibration/overconfidence remains the leading mechanism**, converging with T-333's H1/H2/H4 and this session's fresh calibration-slope measurement. No recalibration ADR proposed this window, per explicit scope ("diagnose, do not fix"). Full memo: `05_RESEARCH_EXPERIMENTS/RESULTS_SUMMARIES/T344_GG105_DECOMPOSITION_MEMO.md`; script + JSON: `scripts/run_t344_vig_sign_diag.py`, `exp_output/T344-VIG-SIGN-DIAG/vig_sign_diag_results.json`. **T-345 (GG-020 Kalshi fee-rate verification, run in parallel this session) found no new evidence** — network attempts to Kalshi's fee documentation were blocked (429/302), consistent with GG-020's prior finding — but clarifies that GG-105's driver is vig-handling (GG-112), not the fee *rate* itself (0.07), which remains a separate, orthogonal, still-open concern under GG-020. **T-359 (2026-07-15, Window 2 escalation, run only after independently re-verifying GG-113's deploy) builds the formal H1×H4 interaction model T-333/T-344 both flagged as the next missing step.** Run against the frozen epoch-3 clean_n cohort (grown to n=666; explicitly hardcoded to `logic_epoch==3` rather than `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH`, now 4 post-GG-113 with clean_n reset to 0 — raw inputs `ba_l10`/`ab_remaining`/`h_so_far` are unaffected by GG-113's downstream vig-sign bug, so the historical cohort remains valid for this test), 666/666 join rate to `t4_mlb_cycle_log.jsonl` via T-332's join method, k=0 exactly reproduces stored `model_p`. **H1 (empirical-Bayes shrinkage of `ba_l10` toward the cohort's own observed mean, an internal data-derived prior, k∈{0,20,40,80,160} pseudo-count sweep) shows a clean, monotonic dose-response**: Fisher p rises from <0.0001 (k=0) → 0.0045 (k=40) → 0.0797 (k=80) → 0.1552/INCONCLUSIVE (k=160), with the pos/neg-edge win-rate gap compressing in step (0.530/0.716 → 0.563/0.619) — direct, testable support for H1 (unshrunk `ba_l10` noise) as a real contributing mechanism, and for a shrinkage-based remediation. **H4 could not be independently isolated**: only 12/666 (1.8%) of the cohort has real known-`batting_slot` data (far below `MIN_N=20`, UNDERPOWERED/descriptive-only per GG-098), so the fallback-path segment (n=654, 98.2%) is nearly the whole cohort and an H1×H4 interaction sweep restricted to fallback-only reproduces the full-cohort H1 sweep almost exactly — confirms H1's effect isn't a slot-known-subset artifact, but H4's distinct marginal contribution remains untested this session, an honest limitation rather than a forced verdict. Script + JSON: `scripts/run_t359_gg105_interaction_model.py`, `exp_output/T359-GG105-INTERACTION-MODEL/interaction_model_results.json`. **Formal remediation proposal (ba_l10 shrinkage, sized for its own DS sign-off per Hard Rule 3) not yet drafted — session paused at context limit; this is the immediate next step for whichever session picks this up.** |
