### GG-106 — `TestT1RegressionAnchor` pins a tight numeric tolerance to the live-mutating VPS portfolio cache, drifts stale as the book grows (found during T-322/T-323/T-324 post-merge verification)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-106 |
| Category | Test-design gap — a "regression anchor" test (`tests/test_band_monitor.py::TestT1RegressionAnchor`, authored during the GG-100/T-255 investigation) asserts `fisher_pvalue == pytest.approx(0.24615828583796262, abs=0.02)` by loading `REAL_PORTFOLIO` — the live, VPS-synced `.vps_portfolio_cache_mlb.json` — rather than a frozen fixture. The magic number was correct when the anchor was written against a smaller T1 raw_n; it has no mechanism to stay correct as the live book accumulates more resolved bets. |
| What was missed | Discovered running the full test suite after merging T-322/T-323/T-324 to `main` (post fast-forward, this session): `test_t1_band_reproduces_direction_aware_verdict` fails — `fisher_pvalue` obtained 0.3536 vs. expected 0.2462±0.02. Not a code regression: the test's own qualitative assertions (`verdict == E_INC`, `verdict != E_ANTI`, calibration CI includes 1, `not b_excludes_1`) all still pass — only the tight point-estimate tolerance on the Fisher p-value has drifted, consistent with T1's raw_n growing (169 at anchor-authoring time → 214 as of this session's own clean_n work, per CLAUDE.md's commercial-validation section). |
| When it should have been caught | At the anchor's original authorship — any test that loads a live, cron-mutated production data file and asserts a tight numeric tolerance on a value derived from it will drift as that file grows; this needed either a frozen fixture snapshot or a qualitative-only assertion (verdict class, not exact p-value) from the start. |
| Phases it survived | Present since the GG-100/T-255 session (2026-07-08); not exercised by CI in a way that surfaced the drift until this session's full-suite run. |
| Why it survived | No CI job runs the full test suite against a scheduled cadence that would have caught the drift as the live book grew between writes; it was only caught incidentally during this session's post-merge verification. |
| Impact | LOW — test-suite-only, no production/capital impact. Does affect trust in "test suite green" as a signal, since a future session could see this exact failure and either (a) correctly diagnose it as data drift (as done here) or (b) incorrectly assume a real regression and spend time chasing it, or worse, loosen the tolerance without understanding why it drifted. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-10, this session, running `pytest tests/test_power_calc.py tests/test_band_monitor.py -q` as a sanity check after fast-forward-merging T-322/T-323/T-324 into `main`. |
| Resolution | **FIXED 2026-07-11 (T-336/N-01).** Applied a hybrid of both options this entry proposed, not just option (b) alone: (a) froze `REAL_PORTFOLIO`'s T1 slice (n=230 T1 positions, the live book at fix time) into a static registered fixture — `tests/fixtures/SNAP-2026-07-11-001_t1_regression_anchor_mlb_portfolio.json`, registered in `06_CODE_EXECUTION_REPRODUCIBILITY/DATA_SNAPSHOTS_INDEX.md` as `SNAP-2026-07-11-001`/alias SNAP-007, sha256 `47938bd44ff762a37d557c2551a633e0eafd0a92a1e9050fbfdb114b9a85924d` — so the anchor is reproducible independent of live data growth, closing this entry's root cause (a test depending on a live-mutating file at all); **and** (b) loosened the assertion from an exact `pytest.approx(0.246, abs=0.02)` to a verdict check (`E_INC`, not `E_ANTI`) plus a wide tolerance band (`0.10 < fisher_pvalue < 0.60`) plus the calibration-CI-includes-1 check, so a future re-freeze of the fixture doesn't require hand-tuning a new magic number as long as the qualitative result is unchanged. Verified by deleting the live cache from the worktree entirely and re-running: 49/49 passed in `test_band_monitor.py`+`test_power_calc.py`, confirming full decoupling. Swept both files plus every other test referencing "vps" for the same anti-pattern — no other instances found. Committed `5a4f074`; DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-11 ("T-336 (N-01) DONE"). |
| Status | **RESOLVED — 2026-07-11, T-336/N-01.** |

### GG-107 — Untracked, never-claimed iteration-plan proposals sitting in shared `main` for 3+ days; their own task-ID reservation collided with real work before anyone noticed

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-107 |
| Category | Process gap — shared-tree hygiene / narrative-conflation precursor (GG-082/GG-101 family) |
| What was missed | Two planning documents (`ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening.md`, `ITERATION_PLAN_context_and_math.md`), both dated 2026-07-08 and both self-described as "PROPOSAL... not yet committed to SESSION_CLAIMS.md" / "not yet task-registered," were left untracked (`git status` `??`) in the shared `main` working tree with no committing session, no claim row, and no expiry mechanism. `ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening.md` explicitly reserved task IDs **T-320–T-331** for its own (never-started) work. This session's real, separately-scoped Workstream E brief was assigned T-330/T-331 by the operator without cross-checking that file (which was never committed or claimed, so it did not show up in any of the normal claim/collision checks) — the collision was only caught because this session's own Step 0c/0e re-read surfaced it, not because any structural check would have caught it. |
| When it should have been caught | At authorship (2026-07-08): per CLAUDE.md Step 0b, any task-ID reservation should be committed to `SESSION_CLAIMS.md` atomically at the moment it's made, specifically to prevent exactly this — a second session picking the same range days later with no visibility into the first reservation. |
| Phases it survived | 3+ days (2026-07-08 → 2026-07-11) sitting untracked in shared `main`, surviving at least one intervening session's Step 0c "check for other live sessions" scan (which only reads `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`, not untracked working-tree files) without being claimed, committed, or flagged. |
| Why it survived | `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`-based collision detection (Step 0c) only sees claims that were actually committed. A task-ID reservation that exists only as prose inside an untracked local file is invisible to every other session's Step 0 protocol, no matter how carefully that protocol is followed — the protocol has no mechanism to detect "a reservation exists but was never registered," only "two registered reservations collide." |
| Impact | LOW-MEDIUM, realized this session as a same-day renumbering (T-330/T-331 → T-334/T-335, logged in DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-11) rather than a real collision, because this session's re-read caught it before any commit. Had a second session started real implementation work under T-330/T-331 before this one, this would have been a genuine GG-082-class ID collision requiring after-the-fact reconciliation, not a clean pre-emptive renumber. Untracked files aging in shared `main` with no owner are also a standing risk under Hard Rule 10/0d — any future session's broad-scoped git operation (even a permitted, path-scoped commit that grows sloppy) is one `git add -A` away from silently absorbing or destroying them. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-11, this session (Workstream E / T-334/T-335), while renumbering task IDs and investigating why T-330/T-331 felt pre-used; confirmed via direct read of both `ITERATION_PLAN_*.md` files' own headers, which explicitly self-report as uncommitted proposals. |
| Resolution | Partially remediated 2026-07-11 (same-day, separate session, Window B/context_and_math reconciliation): `ITERATION_PLAN_context_and_math.md`'s Window B track was formally claimed, merged to `main` via PR #7, and the file retired — the file this entry originally warned about is gone. `ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening.md` and `ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening_v2.md` remain deliberately untouched per explicit operator instruction (owned by a separate live terminal session) — this portion of the gap is intentionally still open, not overlooked. **This gap's warned-about failure mode was independently confirmed to occur for real the same day**, in a different form than originally described: not an ID collision between an untracked proposal doc and real work, but a GG-ID collision between two properly-committed-and-merged branches (Window B's PR #7 registering GG-109 for worktree-isolation, and the T-336/T-337 branch's PR #6 independently registering its own GG-109 for a SemanticFloorViolation finding) that forked before either could see the other's registration, with GitHub's squash-merge finding no textual conflict because the two additions landed in different regions of this file. Resolved by renumbering the fresher/OPEN entry to GG-110 (see that entry's header note) — but the structural point stands: two independent, well-behaved sessions each following protocol correctly still produced a silent collision, this time on GG- IDs rather than T- IDs, via branch-fork-timing rather than an untracked file. The proposed structural fix (extending Step 0c to surface unregistered reservations, and by extension to pre-flight-check the *next available* GG-/T- ID against `origin/main` at merge time rather than at branch-fork time) is still not built. **File-hygiene portion completed 2026-07-12 (T-348):** both remaining files (`ITERATION_PLAN_calibration_hardening.md`, `_v2.md`) — whose T-320–T-343 workstreams were by then substantively executed (per `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-08/07-11 entries) — retired: retirement-note header prepended to each summarizing what closed and what remains genuinely open (v1: fully closed; v2: T-340/T-343 carried forward, still calendar-/adequacy-gated), then `git mv`'d from repo root into `11_MEMORY_HANDOFF/` as historical artifacts. A third, active plan (`ITERATION_PLAN_v3.md`, which reserved T-344–T-350 and itself triggered this remediation as one of its own Window 4 tasks) stays in root, untouched, until its own workstreams close. **A second real-world instance of this gap's exact risk class was also confirmed 2026-07-12**, independent of the GG-110 instance above: a GG-111 ID was independently assigned by two concurrent sessions (the T-341/T-342 branch, for an unrelated already-resolved test-fragility fix; and a separate G6-status-review worktree, for the clean_n dashboard-inconsistency finding that became GG-112) — caught only at rebase time and resolved by renumbering the fresher/OPEN entry to GG-112, per the same convention used for GG-109→GG-110 (see `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-12, row citing "GG-104->GG-109/GG-109->GG-110 precedent"). Two independent real collisions in as many weeks, both caught by manual rebase/re-read rather than by any structural check, is direct evidence the Step 0c extension proposed above is not optional hardening — it is addressing a recurring, not hypothetical, failure mode. The structural fix itself remains **not built**. |
| Status | **RESOLVED 2026-07-14 (T-355, UNWIND_PLAN.md Window B).** The structural fix flagged as missing above is now built: `scripts/lib/check_id_collision.sh` (a pure, offline, no-git-network function) greps every T-/GG-/ADR- token in a proposed claim range against `TODO_NEXT.md`, `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`, and `DECISION_LOG.md`, and `scripts/claim_session.sh` now sources it and refuses (exit 1, before any commit/push) to claim an ID already found in any of the three. Proven both by a unit test suite (`tests/test_claim_session_collision.py`, 7 cases — rejects an ID already in each of the three registries individually, allows a genuinely fresh ID, treats a no-ID range as a no-op pass, and does not false-positive on substring matches e.g. `T-3520` vs `T-352`) and by a live smoke test this same session: this exact gap recurred a *third* time in real-time while this fix was being built — UNWIND_PLAN.md (authored 2026-07-14 by a prior/concurrent session) assigned T-352/T-353 to Window B without checking they were already registered (2026-07-12) for unrelated content (SECRETS_HANDLING.md rotation cadence, `t4-mlb.service` hardening parity) — caught manually and renumbered to T-354/T-355 before this fix existed, then independently reproduced by running the finished `claim_session.sh` against the now-taken `T-354` (correctly rejected: `COLLISION: T-354 already appears in 04_EXECUTION/SESSION_CLAIMS.md`). `session-end.md` Step 8 (new) additionally enforces push+merge before session close, addressing the "unmerged branch sits invisible overnight" half of this gap's causal chain. Residual: the check is a hard grep on ID tokens, not a semantic check that the *content* under a re-used ID matches — a session deliberately re-confirming its own still-open task under the same ID will also be (correctly, conservatively) rejected and must pick a fresh ID or edit the existing registry entry in place instead. |

### GG-108 — CLAUDE.md Step 4's NBA operational health check grepped a log filename that no longer existed on the VPS, silently returning empty on every run

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-108 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — protocol/interface drift |
| What was missed | CLAUDE.md's mandatory Step 4 command grepped `/opt/prediction-market-bot/logs/paper_cron.log` for NBA T1 health. That file does not exist on the VPS — NBA's single-window paper-trade cron was split into two windows at some unlogged point (`paper_early_cron.log` at 18:00 UTC, `paper_late_cron.log` at 01:30 UTC), and the Step 4 command was never updated to match. `grep ... nonexistent_file 2>/dev/null` returns empty with no error, which renders identically to "0 games scheduled, nothing to report" — the intended reading whenever NBA is off-season/dark. |
| When it should have been caught | At the moment the early/late cron split shipped (filename rename), whichever session made that change should have grepped CLAUDE.md itself for the old filename and updated all references, per the same "fix stale claims in place" discipline the file's own header mandates. |
| Phases it survived | Unknown number of sessions since the early/late split — the split predates this session and no DECISION_LOG entry cross-referenced updating Step 4's command. Every session that ran Step 4 in that window and saw empty NBA output plausibly read it as "confirmed dark" without it actually confirming anything. |
| Why it survived | The check has no negative-space assertion — an empty result reads as a pass whether the file exists and truly has nothing, or the file doesn't exist at all. Nothing in the protocol distinguishes "grep found zero matching lines" from "grep found zero because the target was wrong." |
| Impact | LOW today — independently confirmed via the correct files (`paper_early_cron.log`/`paper_late_cron.log`) that NBA is in fact correctly dark for 2026-07-11 (`No KXNBAPTS markets available. No games today or pre-market.`), and NBA is tabled/frozen per standing operator directive regardless, so no live capital or paper-trading decision was made on the faulty check's output. The risk was structural: had NBA's cron actually broken (not just gone quiet for the season), this check would have shown the same empty output and reported a false "healthy/dark" read, the same failure shape as GG-049 (which this Step 4 check was built specifically to prevent). |
| Discovery | 2026-07-11, session-start protocol run (isolated worktree `session/20260711T073538Z-session-start-audit`). Step 4's NBA grep returned empty; cross-checked by `ssh`-ing directly to `ls logs/*cron*.log` and `crontab -l`, which showed the real filenames and confirmed the cron itself is alive and firing on schedule. |
| Resolution | Fixed same session — CLAUDE.md Step 4's command now greps both `paper_early_cron.log` and `paper_late_cron.log`, and also matches the literal `No .* markets available` off-season line so a genuinely healthy dark day produces visible confirming output instead of silence. Logged in DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-11 per Hard Rule 7. |
| Status | RESOLVED 2026-07-11 |

### GG-110 — Fresh CRITICAL SemanticFloorViolation fired in production T1 MLB (RESOLVED: actually game_date 20260711, not 20260710 — see Resolution), zero players matched despite 16 games fetched

**Renumbered from GG-109 during main fast-forward reconciliation (2026-07-11)** — this gap was independently registered as GG-109 by the T-336/T-337 branch, which forked from `main` before Window B's merge (PR #7, commit `388c56f`) landed and therefore could not see that Window B had already registered its own, unrelated GG-109 (git worktree isolation vs. Python import resolution, RESOLVED via T-282). GitHub's squash-merge of PR #6 did not flag a textual conflict — the two additions landed in different regions of this file — so the ID collision was silent, exactly the failure class GG-107 describes. Since Window B's GG-109 is RESOLVED with extensive cross-references already baked into immutable historical `DECISION_LOG.md`/session-handoff entries, and this entry is fresh/OPEN/uninvestigated with far fewer references, this entry was renumbered rather than the other. Content unchanged, ID only. Cross-references updated in `TODO_NEXT.md`, `DECISION_LOG.md`, `SESSION_HANDOFF_20260711_1800_T336_T337.md`.

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-110 |
| Category | Runtime signal failure — T1 MLB pipeline (PIPE-05) |
| What was missed | `paper_mlb_cron.log` on the VPS shows a `CRITICAL semantic_floor_violation` fired for the `[DONE] Report: logs/paper_mlb_20260710.json` run: `games_fetched=16 but matched_players=0`. This is the exact condition CLAUDE.md Step 4's semantic-oracle check exists to catch (GG-054's guard, RESOLVED 2026-07-06, confirmed the guard fires correctly end-to-end in tests — this is that guard firing for real in production for the first time observed). Log ordering places this event immediately after the 20260710 report and immediately before the `[PIPE-05] MLB Paper trading — 20260711` section header; file mtime is 2026-07-11 11:00 UTC, so this fired during today's cron execution while generating/reconciling yesterday's (20260710) report, not during a stale historical run. |
| When it should have been caught | Immediately, by whichever session's Step 4 health check ran closest to the 11:00 UTC cron execution — a prior session claimed at 2026-07-11T07:35:47Z ("TBD — session-start protocol run") logged "zero SemanticFloorViolation" in TODO_NEXT.md, which was accurate at 07:35 but predates this 11:00 event. |
| Phases it survived | Not yet triaged as of this session's Step 4 check (2026-07-11, ~18:00 UTC claim) — roughly 7 hours between firing and first documented notice. |
| Why it survived | No alerting/paging on this counter — it is only visible to a session that happens to run Step 4's grep after the event and before the counter is reset or buried by subsequent log growth. No session ran Step 4 between 11:00 and this one. |
| Impact | UNKNOWN/MEDIUM — one day's (20260710) T1 MLB report shows 0 matched players despite 16 games fetched, meaning T1 MLB placed zero bets off real market data for that report cycle even though games were live. Per CLAUDE.md's own guidance this is a signal failure requiring investigation (naming-convention mismatch, wrong ticker, markets settled before pipeline ran, or stale pregame context file) — likely causes are enumerated in the violation message itself but not yet diagnosed. Not yet known whether this is a one-off (e.g. a transient Kalshi/ESPN outage) or a recurring ticker/name-matching regression. Does not affect T4M-PROJ (separate evaluator, confirmed active via systemd) or capital safety (paper trading only, no live capital pending G6). |
| Discovery | 2026-07-11, this session (T-336/T-337, isolated worktree), CLAUDE.md Step 4 operational health check — grepped `paper_mlb_cron.log` for `SemanticFloorViolation`, found count=1 where the immediately preceding session (07:35 claim) had found 0. |
| Resolution | **Investigated and closed 2026-07-11 (follow-up session, isolated worktree `20260711T190308Z-gg110-…`).** **Date correction first:** the "report date 20260710" attribution in this entry's title/body was a reasonable misread of log ordering but is wrong — the raw audit record (`audit_mlb.jsonl`, `event_id EVT-ACA5759B5876`) is unambiguous: `session_id: "PT-MLB-2CD75D5F"`, `payload.game_date: "2026-07-11"` — the violation belongs to **today's (20260711) run**, not 20260710's. It only *looks* like it precedes the `20260711` header in `paper_mlb_cron.log` because the CRITICAL line is emitted via the logger (a different, unbuffered stream) while the header is a plain `print()` (stdout-buffered) — the two interleave non-chronologically in the redirected file. The 20260710 run itself (session `PT-MLB-332B5B04`) was fully healthy — 37 players matched, 19 bets placed — and is not implicated at all. **Code-path finding:** exactly one call site (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py:730-761`; guard = `SemanticFloorGuard.assert_floor()` in `c7_monitoring/semantic_contracts.py:331-402`, firing when `games_fetched>0 and matched_players==0`). `matched_players` = Kalshi markets filtered by `status=="active" and has_live_quotes and _ticker_to_game_date(m.ticker)==GAME_DATE_ISO` (`:719-728`); `games_fetched` = MLB Stats API scheduled-game count in `Preview` state (`:733`, excludes `Final` games by design). **Root cause, confirmed by direct comparison:** at the 11:00 UTC PIPE-05 fire time on 2026-07-11, Kalshi had only 55 total KXMLBHIT markets (44 quoted) — versus a live re-fetch the same afternoon showing 383 total / 317 quoted, correctly tickered `26JUL11...` and parsing cleanly to `2026-07-11` via `_ticker_to_game_date` (verified directly against live tickers — the regex/parsing logic itself is not broken). Kalshi had simply not finished publishing the day's markets by 11:00 UTC; none of the 55 early markets present at fetch time were tickered for today, so all were correctly filtered out and `matched_players` legitimately computed to 0. This contradicts the timing assumption documented in `deploy/cron.conf`'s PIPE-05 comment block ("Kalshi publishes same-day KXMLBHIT markets by ~01:00 UTC ... at 11:00 UTC all ET-dated games are in Preview state") — true on 2026-07-10 (130 markets already live at 11:00 UTC) but false on 2026-07-11. **This is a genuine, reproducible-class defect, not a one-off data blip or a matching/parsing bug:** T1 MLB silently places zero bets for the entire day whenever Kalshi is slow to finish publishing that day's markets ahead of the fixed 11:00 UTC fire time, with no retry. **Impact for 2026-07-11 specifically:** T1 MLB placed 0 bets today; T4M-PROJ (separate systemd-managed evaluator polling independently every 45s) is unaffected since it isn't gated to a single snapshot. **Follow-up registered:** T-343 in `TODO_NEXT.md` — add a bounded retry/backoff to PIPE-05's market-fetch step (re-fetch after N minutes if `matched_players==0 and games_fetched>0`, before declaring the violation) or move the fire time later; implementation deferred to a future session. **T-343 implemented 2026-07-11 (same-day follow-up session, isolated worktree `20260711T231206Z-t343-pipe05-retry-backoff`):** `run_mlb_paper_trade.py`'s Step 5 now wraps the fetch+group step in a bounded linear-backoff retry loop (3 attempts default, 300s/600s/900s delays, both env-overridable), gated on `_count_scheduled_mlb_games(GAME_DATE_ISO) > 0` so a genuine 0-games day still breaks immediately with no wasted retry. Full detail in `TODO_NEXT.md`'s T-343 entry and `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-11. |
| Status | **RESOLVED — root cause identified AND code fix shipped.** T-343 closed same-day (retry/backoff implemented, tested — 2648 passed/29 skipped/0 failed — and merged). |

### GG-111 — Local-vs-UTC date mismatch in T-343's own new test makes it fail depending on time-of-day the suite is run

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-111 |
| Category | Test fragility — time-drift dependent test, silent pass/fail based on wall-clock timing |
| What was missed | `tests/test_run_mlb_paper_trade.py::TestMainSemanticFloorViolationFiresLive::test_retry_recovers_when_later_fetch_matches_players` (introduced by T-343's own merge, `bd4a395`, GG-110's fix commit) computes `today_iso = date.today().isoformat()` (line 638) — the **local** wall-clock date — and feeds it into a mocked `_ticker_to_game_date` used to simulate a Kalshi market ticker that recovers on retry. Production code (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py:116-124,721`) deliberately computes `GAME_DATE_ISO` from `datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc)`, by explicit design (comment at :116-120: cron runs 11:00 UTC / 7am ET so UTC-basis keeps today's ET games in `Preview` state). When local and UTC calendar dates diverge — i.e., any time after roughly 5pm PDT / 8pm EDT through midnight local — the test's mocked "recovered" market's date never matches the production `GAME_DATE_ISO` it's compared against, the intended-to-match retry never matches, the test's 2-item `fetch_all_markets.side_effect` list is exhausted by an unplanned extra retry attempt, and `StopIteration` is raised instead of the intended assertions running. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-343's own merge, by running the full suite at a UTC-vs-local boundary time (evening Pacific), or by code review noticing the test used `date.today()` while the code under test explicitly documents a UTC basis two lines away in its own module. |
| Phases it survived | Landed in `main` via T-343 (`bd4a395`) same-day as GG-110's resolution; survived until this session's (T-341/T-342, `20260711T225758Z-...`) mandatory pre-merge full-suite regression check, run in the evening Pacific / next-day UTC window, which is exactly when it fails. |
| Why it survived | T-343's own merge session evidently ran the suite (or wasn't caught by CI) at a time-of-day where local and UTC dates coincided, so the bug was latent and invisible until a session ran the suite during the local/UTC date-boundary window. No CI check runs the suite repeatedly across different times of day, so this class of fragility has no structural catch mechanism. |
| Impact | LOW/test-only — the production `run_mlb_paper_trade.py` UTC-basis logic is correct and unaffected; only this one test's mocked date source is wrong. Confirmed via direct comparison against the clean `main` worktree at `967beec` (pre-dating this session's changes) that the identical failure reproduces there too — this is a pre-existing defect on `main`, not introduced by T-341/T-342's branch. No other of the file's 3 other `date.today()` call sites (lines 391, 508, 773) are affected — those populate a feature CSV `game_date` column for T-193 freshness-guard purposes only and are self-consistent regardless of local/UTC divergence. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-11 (into 2026-07-12 UTC), this session (T-341/T-342), via CLAUDE.md/session convention's mandatory full-suite pre-merge health check after rebasing onto `origin/main` (which had independently advanced via T-343). System clock at time of discovery: local `Sat Jul 11 22:03:29 PDT 2026` vs UTC `Sun Jul 12 05:03:29 UTC 2026`. Root-caused via: reading the failing test's full source, confirming production's deliberate UTC basis via its own code comments, and reproducing the identical failure against the separate clean `main` worktree at `967beec` to rule out this session's own rebase as the cause. |
| Resolution | Test-only fix: change line 638's `today_iso = date.today().isoformat()` to a UTC-based equivalent (e.g. `datetime.now(timezone.utc).date().isoformat()`) matching the production code's own basis, applied only to this one test. Fixed same-day in this session (T-341/T-342 branch) immediately following this registration. |
| Status | RESOLVED [2026-07-11/12] — fixed same-day, in this session, immediately after registration. |

### GG-112 — Governance-scan dashboard reports two different `clean_n` figures for T4M-PROJ within the same run

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-112 |
| Category | Monitoring/dashboard self-consistency — `.claude/hooks/governance_scan.sh` |
| What was missed | The session-start governance scan prints two separate lines that both claim to report T4M-PROJ's `clean_n` (bets resolved under `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH`), and they disagree. In a single invocation (2026-07-12, ~09:30 UTC): `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE` line reported `MLB T4M-PROJ: raw_n=300 clean_n=300`; the `SAMPLE ADEQUACY` line two sections later, same script run, reported `[T4M-PROJ] clean_n=412/n*=3059`. `raw_n` itself differs (300 vs. a separately-observed 656 from a live `run_power_calc.py` pull moments later) — this is not just a clean/raw definitional gap, the two code paths see different total row counts for the same band. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-338 (2026-07-11), when the `SAMPLE ADEQUACY` line was added to the same script that already had the `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE` line — a self-consistency check between the two new/existing lines was not run before merging. |
| Phases it survived | Shipped and merged same-day as T-338/T-339/APR-0024 (2026-07-11/12) without anyone diffing the two lines against each other. |
| Why it survived | The two lines look like independent, unrelated dashboard facts (one labeled "provenance," one labeled "sample adequacy") rather than two measurements of the same underlying quantity, so a reader has no visual cue to cross-check them against each other. |
| Impact | MEDIUM — `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE`'s `_band_counts()` (governance_scan.sh) filters the T4M cache's `closed_positions` by `band=='T4M-PROJ'` directly and trusts each row's stored `logic_epoch` field, refetching the VPS cache only if >600s stale. `SAMPLE ADEQUACY` instead rebuilds via `scripts/build_t4_resolved_ledger.py --portfolio "$CACHE_T4M"` (also a `logic_epoch` passthrough, confirmed by direct code read — not a recompute) before handing off to `run_power_calc.py`. Root cause not yet diagnosed — candidates include cache-staleness skew between the two code paths' fetch timing, or a `closed_positions` vs. ledger-rebuild row-selection mismatch upstream of the `logic_epoch` filter — but whichever line is wrong, it has been feeding numbers into governance artifacts: **APR-0024's own published T4M-PROJ figures (clean_n=338, 2026-07-11 pull) came from the `run_power_calc.py`/ledger-rebuild path**, i.e. the same lineage as the higher `SAMPLE ADEQUACY` number, not the `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE` line's lower one — so APR-0024's numbers are probably self-consistent, but any future session that cites the `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE` line alone (the one explicitly labeled "G6 progress language should cite clean_n, not raw_n") gets a different, lower figure than APR-0024's own methodology would produce today. No capital risk (paper trading only), but a real risk of two sessions citing two different "clean_n" numbers for the same band in the same week without either being flagged as wrong. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-12, session-start protocol + user-directed G6/GG-004 status review, isolated worktree `20260712T093443Z-g6-adequacy-status-review`. Found while reconciling live figures to answer a status-review request; not part of a scheduled audit. |
| Resolution | RESOLVED same-session (T-344). Root cause pinned by direct inspection of the live T4M cache (2026-07-12): `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` has 656 `closed_positions`, of which 356 are legacy rows with no `band` field at all (predating that field being written) and 300 carry `band=="T4M-PROJ"` explicitly. `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE`'s `_band_counts()` required an exact `p.get('band') == 'T4M-PROJ'` match, silently dropping all 356 unlabeled legacy rows (raw_n=300, clean_n=300 — every tagged row happened to be on the current epoch). `build_t4_resolved_ledger.py`'s `build_ledger()` never filters by `band` at all — it treats every row in this T4M-dedicated file as T4M-PROJ by construction, which is the correct assumption for this file — so it captured the full 656 (raw) / 412 (clean) universe, matching `SAMPLE ADEQUACY`'s figure exactly (300 clean tagged + 112 clean untagged legacy = 412, confirmed by direct row count). Fix: consolidated `.claude/hooks/governance_scan.sh` so `CLEAN-LOGIC PROVENANCE` and `SAMPLE ADEQUACY` share one ledger build (`build_t4_resolved_ledger.py`, now run once) and T4M-PROJ's raw_n/clean_n in both sections is now computed by the same functions (`run_band_monitor.load_t4_ledger` + `filter_clean`) that `run_power_calc.analyze_band_power` already used internally — T1/T2 counting (`_band_counts()` on the raw MLB portfolio) is untouched since `run_band_monitor.load_pool_by_band` already used the identical band-field filter, so T1/T2 were never affected by this bug. Verified post-fix: both sections now report `MLB T4M-PROJ: ... clean_n=412` and `[T4M-PROJ] clean_n=412/n*=3059` in the same run. Full existing test suite for the touched dependency modules (`tests/test_band_monitor.py`, `tests/test_power_calc.py`) re-run clean: 49 passed, 0 failed — no source-code behavior changed, only the shell hook's row-counting path. No dedicated test harness exists for `.claude/hooks/*.sh` files in this repo (no prior convention to extend), so no new test file was added for the hook itself. |
| Status | RESOLVED [2026-07-12] — fixed same-session (T-344), verified against live VPS cache data, no regressions in dependent test suites. |

### GG-113 — `run_t4_mlb_live.py` flips vig's sign (cost→credit) for every NO-direction bet, contradicting the codebase's own established vig convention

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-113 (renumbered from a session-local GG-112 — collided with a concurrent session's own GG-112, the dashboard `clean_n` self-consistency bug directly above, both discovered independently 2026-07-12) |
| Category | Fee/edge-frame correctness bug — live gating math, not a storage-layer or tooling-parity issue |
| What was missed | `run_t4_mlb_live.py:952` computes `gross_edge = p_t4 - mip - vig` (vig baked in as a YES-frame cost). For NO-direction bets (`gross_edge < 0`), line 1032 sets `directional_gross_edge = -gross_edge`, negating the *entire* expression including the already-subtracted `vig` term. Algebraically this converts vig from a cost into a credit for NO bets: `-(p_t4 - mip - vig) = (mip - p_t4) + vig`. This contradicts the codebase's own pre-existing, documented vig convention (`run_exp017.py:41`, `"kalshi_vig: full spread (ask - bid)"`; its bet-simulation logic uses `half_vig = vig/2.0` and adds it as a cost to `price_paid` on *either* side — YES: `mip + half_vig`, NO: `(1-mip) + half_vig`). Vig is a bid-ask spread cost; it should never flip sign with direction. |
| When it should have been caught | GG-087's fix (which this exact code block's comment cites — "designed to avoid reintroducing" GG-077's sign-convention bug) negated the whole `gross_edge` expression to flip direction correctly for the `(p_t4-mip)` term, but did not separately account for the fact that `vig` was already folded into that same expression with its own fixed sign — the fix solved the direction-flip problem for the signal term while accidentally re-introducing an analogous problem for the cost term. |
| Phases it survived | Live in production (T4M-PROJ, `t4-mlb` systemd service) since GG-087's fix. Not caught by T-333's H5 (2026-07-11), which built a "live-parity" formula that deliberately *mirrors* this exact behavior for offline/live parity-testing purposes — a tooling-parity question, not an economic-correctness one — and so did not evaluate whether the live behavior itself is correct. |
| Why it survived | No test asserts vig's sign independent of direction; T-333's H5 explicitly checked "vig is used consistently... never sign-flipped... across the YES/NO direction split" but validated consistency-with-the-live-code, not consistency-with-the-project's-own-established-vig-semantics (`run_exp017.py`). Two different bars, and only the weaker one was checked. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH, capital-track relevant. 337/412 (81.8%) of T4M-PROJ's clean_n resolved book is NO-direction; every one of those bets was gated on an edge inflated by `+2×vig` relative to the correct symmetric-cost treatment. Recomputing with the corrected (symmetric-vig) formula shows 137/412 (33.3%) of clean_n positions have true (corrected) edge ≤ 0 — i.e., a third of the currently-approved book would not have cleared `MIN_EDGE_TO_APPROVE=0.04` gating under a correct vig treatment. **This does NOT explain GG-105** (T-344 decomposition, same session: correcting this bug strengthens the anti-predictive edge/win-rate pattern rather than resolving it — see `05_RESEARCH_EXPERIMENTS/RESULTS_SUMMARIES/T344_GG105_DECOMPOSITION_MEMO.md`) but is independently a real correctness bug affecting which bets the live system approves. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-12, T-344 (GG-105 decomposition task), via direct code read of `run_t4_mlb_live.py:952-1037` cross-referenced against `run_exp017.py`'s pre-existing documented vig-as-symmetric-cost convention, then empirically confirmed via `scripts/run_t344_vig_sign_diag.py` against the live VPS-pulled clean_n=412 ledger. |
| Resolution | **RESOLVED 2026-07-14, Window 1 (worktree `gg113-rebase-clean`), DS sign-off received (operator, Earl Ignacio — see DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-14 entry, quoted verbatim: "stop accumulating based on biased formulas... it's so that our process and formulaic design is stress-tested and held to a relentless standard of precision without letting it cut corners").** Superseding ADR-0043 (ACCEPTED) documents the fix, the accepted `clean_n`/logic-epoch reset, and the explicit scope boundary (sign only, not magnitude; T4M-PROJ only, not T1/T2). Fixed both independent instances of the bug: `run_t4_mlb_live.py` (live gating decision, commit `fb13166`) — NO-direction edge is now recomputed directly from the signal term, `(mip - p_t4) - vig`, rather than algebraically re-deriving it from the vig-inclusive `gross_edge` via a plain negation; `paper_trader.py::open_position()` (paper-trade write path, committed alongside ADR-0043 this session) — `_directional_gross = gross_edge` for YES, `-gross_edge - 2.0 * vig` for NO **only when `band == "T4M-PROJ"`**, else the pre-GG-113 `-gross_edge` formula unchanged. **Self-correction, same session, pre-merge:** the first draft of the `paper_trader.py` fix applied the corrected formula unconditionally — since `open_position()` is shared by T1/T2/T4M-PROJ, this would have silently changed T1's and T2's stored `fee_net_edge` ledger values without authorization, contradicting the DS sign-off's T4M-PROJ-only scope. Caught before merge/deploy via `tests/test_approval_path_invariants.py::TestFeeSignInvariance`'s fail-closed gate (asserts `paper_trader.py` and `run_mlb_paper_trade.py::_directional_edge()` stay in agreement; failed loudly once one path was fixed and the other wasn't), then corrected to the band-gated form above — no unconditional version was ever merged to `main`. Both fixed paths now match `run_exp017.py`'s established symmetric-vig convention, scoped correctly to T4M-PROJ alone. Regression tests added: `tests/test_run_t4_mlb_live.py::TestRunCycle::test_no_direction_vig_is_symmetric_cost_not_credit` (pins the economically correct `fee_net_edge` against a live `_run_cycle()` call, not merely internal consistency — the exact bar T-333's H5 missed); `tests/test_paper_trader.py::TestOpenPosition::test_fee_net_edge_is_direction_effective_for_no_bet` (updated — previously pinned the pre-fix/buggy value as "expected," now explicitly scoped with `band="T4M-PROJ"`); `tests/test_paper_trader.py::TestOpenPosition::test_gg113_fix_is_band_gated_to_t4m_proj_only` (new — parametrized over `band in {T1, T2, T3, None}`, locks in that every non-T4M-PROJ band keeps the pre-GG-113 formula). Full suites green: `test_run_t4_mlb_live.py` + `test_paper_trader.py` 226 passed combined, `test_approval_path_invariants.py` 6 passed (source pin updated to match the live `(mip - p_t4) - vig` block). `logic_epoch.py` epoch advanced 3→4 (commit `a67545c`, boundary references `2569c1f` — the later of the two fix commits, since both files are part of the same write path and the corrected behavior is only fully consistent once both land); `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH` verified reporting 4 live, and `tests/test_backfill_logic_epoch.py` updated (new `_POST_GG113` constant) so its "current epoch" assertions track the new boundary instead of silently pinning the now-stale epoch-3 timestamp — 13/13 passed, 17/17 on the broader `-k logic_epoch` sweep. `tests/test_power_calc.py`'s `_rec()` fixture default was also updated to import the live `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH` constant rather than a hardcoded `3`, since the epoch bump silently broke 8 of its "clean" fixtures — same failure class as the `test_backfill_logic_epoch.py` fix, now guarded against recurring on the next epoch bump. T4M-PROJ's `clean_n` resets to 0 under epoch 4 as an accepted, deliberate cost per the DS sign-off (not a failure) — APR-0024's per-band `n*` ETA for T4M-PROJ restarts from zero. **Deferred, not resolved by this fix:** the separate full-vig-vs-half-vig magnitude question (registered below) remains open and unauthorized for change under this sign-off. **Explicitly out of scope:** T1 MLB's and T2 MLB's parallel instances of this bug pattern (`run_mlb_paper_trade.py::_directional_edge()`; T2's local `run_t2_mlb_sameday.py` formula) — registered separately as **GG-119** (OPEN, deferred), since T1 is the active/primary G6 book and both require their own fresh, explicit DS sign-off before any live-formula change (Hard Rule 3 / ADR-0039 Tier B precedent) — this sign-off was scoped to T4M-PROJ only, and the band-gating self-correction above is what keeps `open_position()`'s shared code path from leaking the fix into T1/T2 regardless. **VPS deploy + live ledger re-pull, executed and verified (2026-07-14, same session, close-out):** PR #21 merged (`3d1268c`), CI green, auto-deploy fired and succeeded (including the GG-104 persistent-service-restart step — `t4-mlb.service` confirmed freshly restarted post-deploy, `ActiveEnterTimestamp` matching deploy time), VPS `HEAD` confirmed at the merge commit. Note for a future reader: the boundary's `commit_sha`/`commit_utc` fields were corrected post-merge (same session) from an initial, inaccurate citation of `2569c1f`/`a67545c` (a self-corrected-away draft that never ran in production) to `91111b7` (the actual band-gated commit that landed) — see `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-07-14 addendum and `logic_epoch.py` itself for the full self-correction narrative; this does not change the fix's substance, only which commit SHA the epoch boundary points to. Re-pulling `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` and rebuilding `t4_mlb_resolved_ledger.jsonl` confirms **`clean_n` genuinely reset: raw_n=910, clean_n=0** (zero closed positions yet carry `logic_epoch=4`, as expected minutes after deploy with no new resolutions). One transient false-alarm worth recording: `governance_scan.sh` run immediately post-merge from this session's separate, un-pulled `main` working directory (`Prediction Markets/`, distinct from the `gg113-rebase-clean` worktree the fix was built in) reported `clean_n=666` — investigated and confirmed to be an artifact of that directory's stale `CURRENT_LOGIC_EPOCH=3` (pre-merge) matching 666 genuinely-epoch-3 positions, not any bug in the fix, the backfill script, or a mass-restamping event. Resolved by fast-forwarding that directory to `origin/main` and re-running the scan, which then reported the correct `clean_n=0`. No corrective code change was needed. Window 1 exit criteria (ADR ACCEPTED, fix merged/deployed/verified, GG-113 → RESOLVED, new epoch confirmed live) are now met without qualification. |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-07-14) — see ADR-0043. Full-vig-vs-half-vig magnitude question and GG-119 (T1/T2 MLB parallel bug) remain separately OPEN, unauthorized for change under this sign-off. |

### GG-114 — A session can push a fully-completed branch to origin, self-declare "closed" in its own commit messages/handoff docs, and never open a PR — leaving the work invisible to `gh pr list` and unmerged for days

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-114 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — session-closure verification gap, distinct from GG-101/GG-082 (which are concurrent-session *collision* failures, not closure-verification failures) |
| What was missed | `session/20260710T151127Z-workstream-b-edge-sign` (Workstream C, T-325/T-326/T-327) was worked correctly per Hard Rule 10 — isolated worktree, committed, pushed to `origin` on 2026-07-10 — and its own commit messages and `SESSION_HANDOFF_20260710_1600.md` explicitly described all three tasks, including T-327 (the fail-closed pre-deploy CI gate on approval-path files), as fully complete. No step in that session's close-out, nor any later session's start-up protocol, checked whether a PR had actually been opened for the branch. It had not. The branch — and the safety gate it built — sat on origin, unmerged and inert, for 2 days. |
| When it should have been caught | Any session-end protocol step that verifies closure should confirm the work actually reached `main`, not just that it was committed and pushed. Any later session's Step 0c ("check for other live sessions" via `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`) reads recent claims but does not cross-check open branches against open PRs. |
| Phases it survived | Two full days and at least 3 subsequent sessions' worth of session-start protocols (each of which reads `SESSION_CLAIMS.md`/`TODO_NEXT.md` but does not run `gh pr list --state all` against unmerged branches) before an unrelated worktree-hygiene pass in this session stumbled onto it. |
| Why it survived | Git history alone (`git log`, commit messages, even a self-authored SESSION_HANDOFF file) is not evidence of integration — a branch can look 100% complete from its own history while being completely absent from `main`. No existing protocol step distinguishes "committed and pushed" from "merged." `SESSION_CLAIMS.md` records that work was *claimed*, not that it was *landed*. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH for this instance specifically: the inert artifact was a fail-closed correctness gate (blocks deploy on a red approval-path test run) — its 2-day dormancy meant the live system had zero enforcement of that gate for 2 days despite the tests themselves existing and passing. Generalizes to any safety-relevant branch, not just this one. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-12, this session, while investigating remaining `ITERATION_PLAN_v3.md` scope: found the branch listed in `git branch -r` but absent from `gh pr list --state all --search "head:<branch>"`; confirmed via `git branch --contains <sha> \| grep main` (empty) and a direct grep of live `main`'s `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (no `approval-path-tests` job present) that the gate was genuinely not live. |
| Resolution | This instance: rebased the branch onto current `main`, re-verified the full test suite (2685 passed/29 skipped/0 failed), opened PR #15, confirmed CI green, merged (`381ddac`). Systemic fix not yet implemented — recommended follow-on: add a session-start or session-end check that runs `gh pr list --state all` (or equivalent `git branch -r --merged main` diff) against any branch referenced in the last N `SESSION_CLAIMS.md` rows, flagging any claimed-and-pushed-but-unmerged branch older than some threshold (e.g. 24h). Not scoped or implemented this session — registering the gap per the standing invariant that registration is independent of remediation. |
| Status | RESOLVED (this instance, PR #15/`381ddac`) — the underlying systemic gap (no automated stale-unmerged-branch detection) remains OPEN, unscoped |

### GG-116 — Leaked Kalshi API key ID + private-key filesystem path in a tracked session-handoff document

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-116 |
| What was missed | `11_MEMORY_HANDOFF/SESSION_HANDOFF_20260531_2100.md`, a session-handoff artifact written 2026-05-31 during the original ADR-0008 (Kalshi demo order execution) debugging work, contained the real live Kalshi API key ID (`[REDACTED-2026-07-13-GG-116 — see T-351]`) in plaintext at line 48, and the private-key filesystem path (`/Users/earlignacio/.ssh/kalshi_bot.pem`) at line 50 — both inside prose describing the then-open T-111 auth blocker ("the bot signs requests correctly... correct key ID `[REDACTED-2026-07-13-GG-116]`"). This is a direct violation of Hard Rule 5 / NFR-05 ("Zero hardcoded secrets. Vault/env-var protocol only"), which the project otherwise enforces well: `AuditLogger` has a working automated `_REDACTED_KEYS` scrubber (`c6_audit_logger/audit_logger.py:65-77`) for structured log payloads, but that control only covers `AuditLogger.log()` payloads — it has no reach into narrative markdown files like session handoffs, which are written freehand and never scanned for secret-shaped strings before being committed. |
| When it should have been caught | At authorship time, 2026-05-31 — any pre-commit secret-scanning hook (none exists in this repo as of this writing) or a human proofread specifically for NFR-05 compliance before committing a handoff document. Also should have been caught by any of the many subsequent CLAUDE.md session-start "ARE WE COMPROMISED?" Q1-Q6 passes since — none of the six watchdog questions specifically directs a scan of `11_MEMORY_HANDOFF/` prose for leaked secrets; the closest, Q1, scans `risk.py` hard limits, not handoff narrative. |
| Phases it survived | Original commit `dce071e` (2026-05-31) through discovery on 2026-07-12 — roughly 6 weeks, spanning the G5 close, the start of Phase 6/7 live paper trading, and dozens of intervening sessions' worth of session-start protocol runs, none of which caught it. |
| Why it survived | The project's NFR-05 enforcement is real but narrow: it protects structured, code-mediated paths (`AuditLogger` payload redaction, `.env`/vault-only secret loading in `kalshi_client.py`) but has no control at all over freehand prose written into tracked markdown files. Session-handoff documents are, by their nature, written under time pressure at context-limit boundaries (see CLAUDE.md Hard Rule 8 — "stop implementation, run /session-end... commit and push") — exactly the condition least conducive to a careful secrets review, and the resulting document then sits in a widely-read directory (`11_MEMORY_HANDOFF/`) that future sessions are explicitly instructed to read at every session start, multiplying exposure rather than containing it. |
| Impact | MEDIUM-HIGH as a disclosure event, LOW as a live-exploitation risk today: the leaked credential is a **demo-environment** Kalshi API key ID (T-111's own context makes clear this was pre-live, paper-trading-era auth debugging), not a live-capital credential — no live capital has ever existed on this system (Hard Rule 2). The API key ID alone is not sufficient to authenticate (RSA signing requires the paired private key, which was referenced only by filesystem path, not leaked in content) — but the combination of a real key ID plus its private-key file's exact path is real reconnaissance value if this repository, or any fork/clone/backup of it, were ever exposed. The private key itself was never in the tracked file (correctly excluded from VCS per NFR-05 — see the handoff doc's own "Key Constraints to Remember" #6). Git history at `dce071e` and every commit since retains the original plaintext value regardless of this session's in-place redaction — the exposure is not fully closed until a history scrub and/or key rotation is separately decided and executed by the operator. |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — a real credential identifier and filesystem secret path were committed to a tracked, non-`.env` document and remained there undetected across roughly 6 weeks and dozens of subsequent sessions. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-12, this session (T-347/GG-093 threat-model grounding research) — found incidentally while reading `SESSION_HANDOFF_20260531_2100.md` for threat-model context, not via any dedicated secret-scanning pass. A full-worktree grep for the leaked key ID string was run immediately after discovery and confirmed no other tracked file contains it. |
| Resolution | **2026-07-12 (this session, non-destructive):** the plaintext key ID and private-key path were redacted in place in `SESSION_HANDOFF_20260531_2100.md` (commit `c7be13a`) with an explicit note pointing to this gap and to `THREAT_MODEL.md` TH-02. A second live-tree exposure — the same key ID sitting in plaintext in this GG-116 entry's own "What was missed" narrative — was found and redacted 2026-07-13. **2026-07-13 — credential rotated (operator-executed, per Hard Rule: Claude may not rotate credentials).** Operator revoked the leaked key ID in the Kalshi demo dashboard and generated a replacement (new key ID `ff1a9c1b-3c1d-4617-8c60-9d78b0408259`). Claude executed the mechanical cutover once the operator supplied the new key material: backed up old key files (local `~/.ssh/kalshi_bot.pem.bak-20260713`, VPS same path), installed the new private key + updated `VENUE_API_KEY` in both local and VPS `.env` files, restarted `t4-mlb.service`, and empirically verified the new key authenticates (`KalshiMarketClient.from_env().get_balance()` → `AUTH OK {'balance_usd': 995.05, ...}`). The old (now-revoked) key ID is inert going forward regardless of git history. **2026-07-13, later same session — git-history scrub decided and executed.** Operator explicitly authorized proceeding ("Yes - proceed with your recommendation. Keep the clean hygiene - no need to defer."), then flagged 4 concurrent terminal sessions active (this session, a GG-113 fix worktree, a hardware test-suite terminal, a crash-residue cleanup terminal) and directed proceeding "with caution" to avoid stranding any of their work. Claude re-verified worktree state first (`git worktree list` — confirmed only `origin/main` had any remote-tracked ref requiring force-push; other sessions' branches were local-only and their uncommitted diffs are immune to history rewrite/force-push by construction), took a precautionary backup of the GG-113 worktree's uncommitted diff (`gg113-worktree-uncommitted-backup-20260713T221817.patch`, technically unneeded but zero-cost insurance), then executed: `git clone --mirror` to an isolated scratchpad clone (captured `main` + all 20 `refs/pull/N/head` refs), `git filter-repo --replace-text` against a prepped mapping (`863c33ff-...==>[REDACTED-GG-116-ROTATED]`), verified zero occurrences of the leaked key ID across all refs post-rewrite (5 hits pre-scrub → 0 post-scrub), and force-pushed only `refs/heads/main` to `origin` (`df50835` → `47096dd`) — the read-only `refs/pull/N/head` synthetic refs were deliberately not targeted since GitHub does not accept force-pushes to them. Propagated the new history to both clones under direct management: local main working tree (`git fetch && git reset --hard origin/main` → `47096dd`, untracked files preserved) and the VPS deploy clone (same sequence → `47096dd`; `t4-mlb.service` confirmed still `active` post-reset, no tracked-file drift). No other session's branch, worktree, or uncommitted work was touched, reset, or force-pushed over. **Known residual limitation, disclosed not hidden:** GitHub's synthetic `refs/pull/N/head` refs (20 existing PRs on this repo) are read-only and were captured in the mirror clone's history but could not be force-pushed over — old commits containing the original (now-revoked) key ID may remain viewable via GitHub's PR diff/commit UI or API for those PRs even though `main` itself is clean. Since the leaked credential is already revoked (rotation above), this is a residual-disclosure/hygiene gap (dead key ID visible in old PR views), not an active-credential risk — closing it fully would require either closing/deleting the affected PRs or contacting GitHub support for cache purge, neither attempted here as out of scope for this remediation. Tracked as **T-351** (`TODO_NEXT.md`), now DONE. |
| Status | **RESOLVED (2026-07-13).** Both original exit criteria met: (1) key rotated and verified authenticating under the new key ID; (2) history-scrub decision made (proceed) and executed — `origin/main`, the local working tree, and the VPS deploy clone are all synced to the scrubbed history (`47096dd`), verified zero occurrences of the leaked key ID across all refs. Residual limitation (GitHub PR-ref cached views, see Resolution) is disclosed above and does not block closure — it is a hygiene footnote on an already-revoked credential, not an open exposure of a live one. |

### GG-117 — `band_monitor_summary.json` tracked-but-should-be-gitignored, recurring deploy-blocker (GG-102's sibling file, missed)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-117 |
| What was missed | GG-102 (2026-07-xx) gitignored `exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/band_monitor_results.json` because it is rewritten in place by the weekly VPS cron (`run_band_monitor.py --ci`, Mondays) and tracking it dirties the VPS tree, blocking `deploy.yml`'s fail-closed dirty-tree guard. `band_monitor_summary.json` — the same cron run's aggregate rollup output, sitting in the same directory, rewritten by the exact same job — was never added to the same `.gitignore` pattern. It stayed git-tracked and kept getting rewritten by every cron run. |
| When it should have been caught | At GG-102's own authorship — the fix should have covered both sibling output files from the same job in one pass; a `git ls-files exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/` at that time would have shown both. |
| Phases it survived | From GG-102's fix (exact date not re-derived here) through 2026-07-13 — at least one full weekly cron cycle, confirmed to have actually fired and blocked a deploy. |
| Why it survived | GG-102's fix was scoped to the file that was under investigation at the time, not audited against its sibling in the same directory/job. No test or CI check asserts that VPS-cron-regenerated `exp_output/` files are fully covered by `.gitignore`. |
| Impact | MEDIUM — directly caused a live deploy failure: PR #18's "Deploy to VPS" GitHub Actions run (`29293727602`, 2026-07-13) failed because `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` on the VPS showed `M exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/band_monitor_summary.json`, tripping `deploy.yml`'s designed safety guard (`git pull --ff-only` aborted rather than risk clobbering a dirty tracked file). No data loss or corruption — the diff was confirmed benign (regenerated monitor stats only: T1 n 81→110, T4M-PROJ n 412→666) — but it silently blocks every subsequent deploy until manually diagnosed and cleared, which is exactly the failure shape GG-102 was written to prevent. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-13, this session, while investigating why PR #18's auto-triggered "Deploy to VPS" run failed ahead of PR #14's merge-triggered deploy run. `gh run view 29293727602 --log-failed` surfaced the guard trip; `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no` + `.gitignore` grep on the VPS confirmed the sibling-file gap directly. |
| Resolution | Same-session: discarded the benign dirty diff on the VPS (`git checkout -- exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/band_monitor_summary.json`), unblocking PR #14's deploy run, which then completed successfully (VPS HEAD confirmed at `b2577dd`, matching `origin/main`). Root cause fixed in the repo: extended GG-102's `.gitignore` pattern to also cover `exp_output/T255-BAND-MONITOR/band_monitor_summary.json` and untracked the file (`git rm --cached`) so future cron runs no longer dirty the VPS tree. |
| Status | RESOLVED — `.gitignore` fix + untrack committed this session; VPS confirmed unblocked and current. |

### GG-115 — The `approval-path-tests` fail-closed CI gate (T-327) failed on its own first live run: install step bypassed the project's locked-requirements convention and omitted `nba_api`

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-115 |
| Category | CI configuration bug — the safety gate registered as GG-114's resolution was itself broken, on `main`, for the ~8 minutes between PR #15's merge and this fix |
| What was missed | `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`'s `approval-path-tests` job installed dependencies via `pip install -e ".[dev]"` — resolving only `pyproject.toml`'s floating dependency list. `tests.yml` (the project's other, pre-existing CI workflow) instead installs via `pip install -r 06_CODE_EXECUTION_REPRODUCIBILITY/DEPENDENCY_LOCKS/requirements-dev.txt` (which chains to `requirements-base.txt`), the project's established locked-dependency convention (`tests.yml`'s own comment explains why: pinned versions, not floating `pyproject.toml` ranges). `nba_api` is pinned only in `requirements-base.txt`, never declared in `pyproject.toml` at all — so the new job's install step could never resolve it. Every test importing `run_mlb_paper_trade` (which imports `c1_signal_ingestion` → `ingestion.py` → `nba_api` at module load, unconditionally, even for MLB-only test paths) failed collection with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'nba_api'`. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-327's own authorship (2026-07-10): the job's install step should have mirrored `tests.yml`'s pattern, which sits ~20 lines away in the same `.github/workflows/` directory and is explicitly commented with the reason it exists. Also should have been caught by actually exercising a push-to-`main` before merge — but PR CI only runs `tests.yml` (a `pull_request`-triggered workflow); `approval-path-tests` is `push`-to-`main`-only by design (see GG-114/T-327 rationale — deliberately not path-filtered), so no PR-stage CI run ever could have caught this; the first real exercise is structurally always a live push to `main`. |
| Phases it survived | Shipped 2026-07-10 on the orphaned branch; survived GG-114's own recovery rebase and merge (PR #15, `381ddac`, 2026-07-12 17:01 UTC) because the local full-suite verification run during that rebase used the *local* Python environment (`nba_api` already installed there from a prior session), not a clean CI-equivalent install — so the bug was invisible to every local check performed during GG-114's recovery. |
| Why it survived | Two independent causes stacked: (1) the job's install step was never actually run in anger before this merge, since its trigger (`push: branches: [main]`) cannot fire during PR review; (2) local pre-merge verification (`python3 -m pytest -q`) ran against an already-populated local venv, which is a strictly weaker check than a clean-environment CI install for exactly this class of bug (missing dependency declaration). |
| Impact | HIGH for the ~8-minute window it was live: every future push to `main` would have failed `approval-path-tests`, and since `deploy` job has `needs: approval-path-tests`, **every deploy to the VPS would have been blocked outright** until this was fixed — including deploys of genuinely unrelated, correct changes. No live capital or paper-trading impact (VPS `t4-mlb`/cron services continue running whatever was already deployed; a blocked deploy just means the next update can't ship), but it is a self-inflicted availability gap in the exact gate built to prevent silent regressions. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-12, ~17:05 UTC, this session, while confirming the `approval-path-tests` job's first live run (triggered by PR #15's own merge) actually passed — `gh run view <run-id> --log-failed` surfaced the `ModuleNotFoundError` directly. |
| Resolution | Fixed same-session: `approval-path-tests`'s "Install dependencies" step changed to `python -m pip install --upgrade pip && pip install -r 06_CODE_EXECUTION_REPRODUCIBILITY/DEPENDENCY_LOCKS/requirements-dev.txt && pip install -e .`, matching `tests.yml`'s pattern exactly. Verified locally: both gate suites pass (13 passed) and full suite re-confirmed zero regressions (2685 passed/29 skipped, unchanged from pre-fix baseline). PR opened to merge this fix; see DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-12 for the merge record. |
| Status | RESOLVED — fix merged same-session (see DECISION_LOG.md for commit/PR reference) |

### GG-118 — `pmbot-web-dashboard.service` OOM-kill restart loop left the C8 dashboard fully dark for ~2 days; no session-start check would have caught it

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-118 |
| Category | Unbounded-memory bug in production C8 code + missing operational health check for that specific service |
| What was missed | `scripts/web_dashboard.py`'s `_load_jsonl()` parsed every line of an audit file into a full Python dict, held the entire resulting list in memory, and only sliced to the last `tail` entries at the very end. `audit_mlb.jsonl` (14MB) and `audit_mlb_t4.jsonl` (30MB) grew large enough after continuous MLB T1/T4 activity that a single `/api/data` request's in-memory parse exceeded the systemd unit's `MemoryMax=128M` cgroup cap, triggering `oom-kill`. `Restart=always`/`RestartSec=5` meant the service just re-crashed every few seconds indefinitely (NRestarts hit 50+ in the journal) rather than surfacing as a clear failure. |
| When it should have been caught | At initial sizing of `MemoryMax=128M` — the comment in the unit file ("stdlib HTTP + JSON parsing is tiny") assumed small log files and did not account for audit-log growth over a multi-day live run. Also should have been caught earlier operationally: the dashboard was down for ~2 days before the operator noticed via the browser error banner. |
| Phases it survived | Live/deployed since the dashboard's original build; the OOM loop itself ran undetected from ~2026-07-12T01:04 UTC (first `oom-kill` in the journal) until reported by the operator 2026-07-13. |
| Why it survived | **CLAUDE.md's Step 4 operational health check has no probe for `pmbot-web-dashboard.service` at all** — it checks T1 MLB cron output, T4 MLB systemd `is-active`, and NBA cron, but never this service. `systemctl is-active` alone would also not have caught this reliably, since systemd reports `active` again within ~5s of each restart — the service is almost never observed in a `failed` state even though it is crash-looping continuously. The only signal is `journalctl`'s `oom-kill` lines or a sustained `NRestarts` climb, neither of which any existing session-start command greps for. |
| Impact | HIGH for operator visibility: the user's own words were "I can't see any of the activity for our bot - I'm blind!!" — the dashboard is the sole human-facing view into live paper-trading activity (positions, P&L, kill-switch state, alerts) referenced throughout this file's own protocol. No trading/capital impact — the dashboard is read-only and the underlying T1/T4 pipelines kept running and logging normally throughout; this is a monitoring/observability gap, not a trading-safety one. |
| Discovery | 2026-07-13, reported directly by the operator with a screenshot showing "Cannot fetch data: Load failed... Check SSH tunnel is open." Root-caused this session via `journalctl -u pmbot-web-dashboard.service` (found the `oom-kill` loop) and `ls -la logs/*.jsonl` (found the oversized audit files driving it). |
| Resolution | Fixed same-session: `_load_jsonl()` now buffers only the last `tail` raw lines via a bounded `collections.deque(maxlen=tail)` before parsing, so memory usage no longer scales with file size. Verified with a synthetic 200k-line file (correct tail slice, ~15MB peak RSS) before deploying. Deployed via `scp` to the VPS, service restarted, confirmed stable across repeated requests (`MemoryCurrent` ~20MB vs. the 128MB cap, `NRestarts=0` after fix) and end-to-end through the real SSH-tunnel path (`Open Dashboard.command`). Committed (`f6d7a85`) and pushed to `main`. **Not fixed this session, registered as T-356:** add a `pmbot-web-dashboard` health probe (recent `oom-kill`/`NRestarts` count, not just `is-active`) to CLAUDE.md's Step 4 operational health check, so a future recurrence of this failure mode (or a similar one in any other systemd-managed service) surfaces at session start instead of requiring the operator to notice a broken browser tab. |
| Status | RESOLVED — code fix live and verified on VPS; systemic detection gap (T-356) OPEN, not yet implemented |

### GG-119 — T1/T2 MLB's own gating formulas carry the same NO-direction vig sign-flip pattern as GG-113, deliberately deferred out of that fix's scope

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-119 |
| Category | Fee/edge-frame correctness bug — live gating math, same pattern as GG-113, different band/file |
| What was missed | GG-113's fix (ADR-0043, 2026-07-14) corrected T4M-PROJ's (`run_t4_mlb_live.py`, `paper_trader.py::open_position()`) NO-direction edge computation, which had been negating a vig-inclusive `gross_edge` expression wholesale and thereby flipping vig from a cost into a credit for NO bets. T1's own write path, `run_mlb_paper_trade.py::_directional_edge()`, was not inspected line-by-line during GG-113's initial fix — it is named here as a suspected parallel instance of the identical algebraic pattern (a plain sign-negation of an already-vig-inclusive edge expression), not yet confirmed by direct code read against `run_exp017.py`'s symmetric-cost convention. T2's own local gating formula (`run_t2_mlb_sameday.py` lines ~421-423, `gross_edge = p_calibrated - mid - vig`; `net_edge = gross_edge - kalshi_fee`) is similarly unconfirmed — not yet read closely enough to determine whether it independently exhibits the same NO-direction pattern. |
| When it should have been caught | Ideally alongside GG-113 itself, since T1/T2 both compute directional edge from a YES-frame `gross_edge` term. Was not, because the DS sign-off obtained for GG-113 was explicitly scoped to T4M-PROJ only (Window 1 instruction), and T1 is the active/primary G6 book — extending scope to T1/T2 without their own fresh sign-off would violate Hard Rule 3 / ADR-0039 Tier B precedent regardless of whether the bug is confirmed present. |
| Phases it survived | Live in production (T1/T2/T3 MLB) since whenever `_directional_edge()`/T2's local formula was last touched — not yet dated, pending the confirmation read. |
| Why it survived | Same root cause as GG-113: no test asserts vig's sign independent of direction in either file, and no prior review checked `_directional_edge()` or T2's local formula against `run_exp017.py`'s established symmetric-vig convention specifically. |
| Impact | UNCONFIRMED, but potentially HIGH for T1 — T1 MLB is the active/primary G6 book (49/30+ resolved paper bets, the dominant lever toward Tier 2 commercial validation per APR-0024). If confirmed, the same class of edge inflation on NO-direction bets that affected 81.8% of T4M-PROJ's book could be present here, which would directly affect G6 gate integrity. T2's impact is lower-priority (cron-active but thin sample, `raw_n=2 clean_n=0` per the 2026-07-10 read cited in CLAUDE.md). Severity is unconfirmed pending a dedicated read of both formulas; this entry registers the suspected gap per the project's standing invariant (register before remediation, and — per GG-113's own precedent — before even full diagnosis, so the concern is not lost between sessions). |
| Discovery | 2026-07-14, this session (GG-113 fix, Window 1). Named in ADR-0043 D3 as an explicit scope exclusion while drafting the T4M-PROJ fix. **A related, more concrete finding surfaced during the same session's test-suite run, after GG-113's fix was initially drafted**: `paper_trader.py::open_position()` is not a T4M-PROJ-exclusive function — it is *shared* by T1, T2, and T4M-PROJ, each of which passes its own locally-computed `gross_edge`/`band` into it and lets it independently recompute `fee_net_edge`. The first draft of the GG-113 fix applied the corrected symmetric-vig formula unconditionally in `open_position()`, which would have silently changed T1's and T2's *stored ledger* `fee_net_edge` values too — even though their own gating/approval decisions (`_directional_edge()` / T2's local formula) were correctly left unfixed. This was caught before merge/deploy by `tests/test_approval_path_invariants.py::TestFeeSignInvariance` (a fail-closed gate asserting `paper_trader.py` and `_directional_edge()` must agree, which failed loudly once one path was fixed and the other wasn't) and corrected same-session: `open_position()`'s fix is now band-gated (`band == "T4M-PROJ"` only; every other band, including `None`, keeps the pre-GG-113 formula), with a dedicated regression test (`tests/test_paper_trader.py::TestOpenPosition::test_gg113_fix_is_band_gated_to_t4m_proj_only`, parametrized over `T1/T2/T3/None`) locking the boundary in. No unconditional version of the fix was ever merged to `main`. This does not change GG-119's own OPEN status or scope — T1's/T2's *gating* formulas remain unconfirmed and unfixed — but it closes what would otherwise have been an unauthorized scope leak into T1/T2's ledger data. |
| Resolution | Not yet resolved. Next step: direct code read of `run_mlb_paper_trade.py::_directional_edge()` and `run_t2_mlb_sameday.py`'s local gating formula (~lines 421-423) to confirm or rule out the same algebraic pattern (next-free T-number at claim time — **not T-357**, which was the pre-existing reservation this session used for the GG-113 fix itself, see DECISION_LOG.md 2026-07-14). If confirmed, any live-formula change requires its own fresh, explicit DS sign-off and a superseding ADR per Hard Rule 3 / ADR-0039 Tier B precedent — GG-113's sign-off does not extend here. |
| Status | OPEN — deferred, unconfirmed, requires its own DS sign-off before any fix. The adjacent `open_position()` scope-leak risk (see Discovery) is closed via band-gating, independent of this gap's own resolution. |

## Gap Registration Template

When a new governance process failure is discovered:

```markdown
### GG-NNN — [Short title]

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-NNN |
| Category | [Architectural placeholder / Incremental architecture / Systemic blind spot / other] |
| What was missed | [The specific decision, value, or check that was absent] |
| When it should have been caught | [Gate, phase, or trigger that should have flagged it] |
| Phases it survived | [G0→G1→...] |
| Why it survived | [Root cause of the governance failure — what scan/check/protocol did NOT catch it] |
| Impact | [What happened or could have happened as a result] |
| Discovery | [How and when it was found — especially if human eye test vs. automated] |
| Resolution | [ADR/APR/task that closes it] |
| Status | OPEN / IN PROGRESS / RESOLVED [date] |
```

---

## Invariant

**Every governance gap must be registered here before remediation work begins.** The act of registering a gap is itself load-bearing — it ensures that the process failure is documented independently of the fix, so that future sessions can distinguish "we fixed the bug" from "we fixed the system that allowed the bug."
